# Implementation Plan: Drain Annotation or Uncordon Coordination Mismatch

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and obtain matching MCO source/runbook revision.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: MCC/MCD log contains `uncordon.*during update` or `desiredDrain.*lastAppliedDrain`.
- [ ] Confirm `index=ocp_logs`, `index=k8s_objects` (with `managedFields` stripped), and `index=ocp_metrics` are populated.
- [ ] Verify that Node objects and drain annotations are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) for instant retrieval.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, node identity, and annotation keys into structured state.
- [ ] Redact secrets, authorization headers, and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract target node from MCC log or alert.
- [ ] Check `spec.unschedulable` and evaluate against MCO rollout phase.
- [ ] Compare `desiredDrain` with `lastAppliedDrain`.
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Has the node been uncordoned while an update is active (`spec.unschedulable == false`)?
  - Are all non-daemonset pods drained (count == 0) while `desiredDrain != lastAppliedDrain`?
  - Check for API `409 Conflict` in MCC logs when attempting to update node annotations.
  - Check whether user pods are still actively running (prohibiting annotation completion).

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - If premature uncordon or annotation desync with 0 pods is verified:
    - Match `playbook_reconcile_drain_annotation`.
    - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, draining_node, expected_mco_drain_state, action: "reconcile_and_cordon" }`.
    - Mark risk as `low` and require human approval before execution.
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:** If live workloads are present on a node marked drained or competing controllers persist:
  - Explicitly set `playbook_match: null`.
  - Output targeted manual `oc` CLI runbook:
    ```bash
    # 1. Immediately re-cordon the node
    oc adm cordon <node>
    # 2. Check for running pods
    oc get pods -A --field-selector spec.nodeName=<node>
    # 3. Check for competing uncordon events
    oc get events -n default --field-selector involvedObject.name=<node>
    ```
- [ ] Keep all mutation tools disabled inside the agent; hand off execution to AAP or human SRE.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching trigger, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and contradictory evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Playbook matching test: premature uncordon produces exact `aap_extra_vars` matching schema.
- [ ] Fallback test: live workload conflict correctly outputs emergency manual cordon runbook.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: `desiredDrain == lastAppliedDrain`, node remains cordoned, and MCD advances.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller logs, Machine Config Daemon logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `Node` (annotations, schedulability), `Pod` (node assignment, count), `MachineConfigPool`.
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `machine_config_pool_status_updating`, `machine_config_daemon_state`.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Drain Annotation or Uncordon Coordination Mismatch (`14-uncordon-or-drainer-annotation`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | MachineConfigPool is `Updating`, and target node is in drain or post-drain configuration phase. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_updating` |
| **Trigger Signal** | MCC or MCD logs match: `(?i)(uncordon.*during update|desiredDrain.*lastAppliedDrain|drain.*mismatch|node.*unexpectedly schedulable|waiting for drain.*annotation)` OR Node annotations `desiredDrain` and `lastAppliedDrain` diverge while 0 drainable pods remain. | Exit: `not_supported: no_drain_coordination_error_logged` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract Node Coordination State]     [No Coordination Error]
            Node name, spec.unschedulable,                ──► Exit: not_supported
            desiredDrain & lastAppliedDrain annotations
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: Evaluate Schedulability & Desync]
            Check spec.unschedulable vs lifecycle phase
                         │
         ┌───────────────┴───────────────────────────────┐
         ▼                                               ▼
  [spec.unschedulable == false]                 [spec.unschedulable == true]
  Node is prematurely uncordoned!               Node is cordoned.
  ──► Branch 3A: External Uncordon              Compare desiredDrain vs lastAppliedDrain
                                                         │
                         ┌───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                                               ▼
            [desiredDrain == lastAppliedDrain]                              [desiredDrain != lastAppliedDrain]
            Drain is officially complete!                                   Check pods remaining on node
            Handoff to 12-mcd-apply / 13-os-reboot                                           │
                                                         ┌───────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┐
                                                         ▼                                                                       ▼
                                              [0 Drainable Pods Remain]                                               [Pods Still Evicting/Stuck]
                                              Drain completed but annotation dropped!                                 Legitimate drain in progress.
                                              ──► Branch 3B: Annotation Desync (API 409)                              Handoff to 01, 03, or 04
```

### Step 1: Extract Node Coordination State
1. In `k8s_objects` / KV Store, lookup the target `Node` object:
   * Record `spec.unschedulable` (boolean).
   * Record annotation `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/desiredDrain`.
   * Record annotation `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/lastAppliedDrain`.
2. Extract MCC and MCD logs from `index=ocp_logs`:
   * Look for optimistic locking conflicts: `(?i)Operation cannot be fulfilled on nodes.*the object has been modified`.
   * Look for MCD wait states: `(?i)waiting for node to drain.*lastAppliedDrain`.
   * Look for premature uncordon alerts: `(?i)node.*became schedulable during update`.

### Step 2: Differentiate External Uncordon vs. Annotation Desync
1. **Check Schedulability Flag:**
   * If `spec.unschedulable == false` (or not present) while `desiredDrain` is non-empty and update is in-flight:
     * **Premature Uncordon Confirmed.** Proceed to **Branch 3A**.
2. **Check Annotation Equivalence:**
   * If `desiredDrain == lastAppliedDrain`:
     * Drain coordination is fully synchronized. The stall is NOT in drain coordination. Handoff to `use-cases/12-mcd-config-render-apply` (Ignition apply failure) or `use-cases/13-os-pivot-reboot-failure`.
   * If `desiredDrain != lastAppliedDrain`:
     * Scan `k8s_objects` / KV Store for running non-daemonset pods assigned to `spec.nodeName == target_node`:
       * If **0 drainable pods remain**: Pod eviction is finished, but MCC failed to persist `lastAppliedDrain`. Proceed to **Branch 3B**.
       * If **drainable pods are still present**: Eviction or termination is still blocked by workloads. Handoff to `use-cases/01-pdb-eviction-blocked`, `use-cases/03-pod-termination-finalizer`, or `use-cases/04-storage-unmount-detach`.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                            [Drain Coordination Mismatch]
                                          │
            ┌─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┐
            ▼                             ▼                             ▼
  [Branch 3A: External Uncordon] [Branch 3B: Annotation Desync] [Branch 3C: Forged Annotation]
  Autoscaler/Admin uncordoned    MCC 409 Conflict / dropped     lastAppliedDrain set manually
  node while update in flight    write after drain finished     while pods were still running
            │                             │                             │
            ▼                             ▼                             ▼
  Playbook: reconcile_drain_     Playbook: reconcile_drain_     Playbook: null (Data Risk)
  annotation (Re-cordon node)    annotation (Sync annotation)   CLI Fallback: Emergency Halt
```

* **Branch 3A: Premature Uncordon by External Actor or Competing Automation**
  * *Evidence:* `spec.unschedulable == false`, newly created pods scheduled onto the node after update started, and MCC logs report `node became schedulable during update`.
  * *Diagnosis:* An external actor (e.g. cluster autoscaler, automated node maintenance script, or manual admin intervention) executed `oc adm uncordon` during an active MCO rollout, creating a race condition where workloads land on a node undergoing OS pivot or reboot.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_reconcile_drain_annotation`**. (Re-cordons the node to prevent additional pod scheduling and triggers MCC reconciliation).

* **Branch 3B: Annotation Desync / API Concurrency Drop (`409 Conflict`)**
  * *Evidence:* `desiredDrain != lastAppliedDrain`, but count of non-daemonset pods on the node is exactly 0. `ocp_logs` show MCC logged `drain complete for node`, but a subsequent node update failed with `409 Conflict` or etcd context timeout. MCD is indefinitely logging `waiting for drain`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The node has successfully emptied, but the coordination flag `lastAppliedDrain` was never written to the API server due to an optimistic concurrency collision, leaving MCD in a permanent wait loop.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_reconcile_drain_annotation`**. (Patches `lastAppliedDrain` to equal `desiredDrain`, releasing MCD to begin applying machine config).

* **Branch 3C: Forged / Premature Completion Annotation (Corrupt State)**
  * *Evidence:* `lastAppliedDrain == desiredDrain`, but live user workload pods are still actively running and writing to local disks on the node.
  * *Diagnosis:* An operator or automation forcibly forged `lastAppliedDrain` without draining the node, endangering application integrity as MCD initiates reboot.
  * *Remediation Route:* **CRITICAL SAFETY HALT $\rightarrow$ Manual CLI Fallback Only.** Do NOT execute playbooks.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Rule 1: Automated Playbook Match (`playbook_reconcile_drain_annotation`)
**Conditions for Match:**
1. Branch 3A (node uncordoned prematurely while update in-flight) OR Branch 3B (0 drainable pods remain, but `lastAppliedDrain != desiredDrain`).
2. Node is part of an active, unpaused `MachineConfigPool`.
3. No active storage corruption or forged annotation detected.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": true,
  "playbook_id": "playbook_reconcile_drain_annotation",
  "playbook_file": "playbooks/remediate-drain-annotation.yml",
  "risk_level": "low",
  "requires_approval": true,
  "aap_extra_vars": {
    "target_cluster": "{{ cluster_id }}",
    "draining_node": "{{ node_name }}",
    "expected_mco_drain_state": "{{ desired_drain_hash }}",
    "action": "reconcile_and_cordon"
  },
  "verification_goal": "node_desired_drain == node_last_applied_drain and spec.unschedulable == true"
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3C or Complex Competing Automation)
**Triggered when:** Live workloads are running while annotations indicate completion, or persistent external controllers continuously toggle `spec.unschedulable`.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Live workloads detected on node despite completion annotation; emergency human intervention required.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "high",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Check current node cordoning and MCO drain annotations:",
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='Schedulable: {.spec.unschedulable}{\"\\n\"}Desired: {.metadata.annotations.machineconfiguration\\.openshift\\.io/desiredDrain}{\"\\n\"}Applied: {.metadata.annotations.machineconfiguration\\.openshift\\.io/lastAppliedDrain}{\"\\n\"}'",
      "# 2. Check for running pods that should have been drained:",
      "oc get pods -A --field-selector spec.nodeName={{ node_name }} -o wide"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Immediately re-cordon the node to halt new scheduling:",
      "oc adm cordon {{ node_name }}",
      "# 4. Identify and disable competing automation controllers toggling node schedulability:",
      "oc get events -n default --field-selector involvedObject.name={{ node_name }} | grep -Ei 'uncordon|cordon'",
      "# 5. Once competing automation is silenced, let MCC reconcile naturally or trigger node drain:",
      "oc adm drain {{ node_name }} --force --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Never manually set lastAppliedDrain unless you have verified that all non-daemonset pods have finished terminating.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='{.spec.unschedulable}'",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-config-operator logs -l k8s-app=machine-config-daemon -c machine-config-daemon --tail=50"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Missing Node Record:** Target node specified in alert is not found in `index=k8s_objects` or KV Store. Report: `inconclusive: node_record_missing`.
3. **MCO Release Annotation Schema Ambiguity:** On legacy or custom releases where `desiredDrain` annotation key differs from standard schema. Report: `inconclusive: unsupported_drain_annotation_schema`.
4. **Active Eviction Conflict:** Drainable pods are still failing to evict due to PDB or volume locks. Report: `not_supported: drain_in_progress_route_to_workload_skill`.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via AAP playbook or manual CLI, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **Annotation Convergence:** `metadata.annotations['machineconfiguration.openshift.io/desiredDrain'] == metadata.annotations['machineconfiguration.openshift.io/lastAppliedDrain']`.
2. **Node Cordon Integrity:** `node.spec.unschedulable == true` throughout the configuration apply and reboot cycle.
3. **MCD Advancement:** MachineConfigDaemon logs in `index=ocp_logs` transition from `waiting for drain` to `Applying MachineConfig` or `rebooting node`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule After Eviction (`07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP/upgrade stall is caused by **replacement workloads unable to schedule after eviction**, isolate the exact scheduler constraint preventing pod placement (compute resource starvation, hard pod anti-affinity deadlock, or taint/topology zone mismatch), and generate an actionable manual CLI fallback runbook using evidence from Splunk without direct Kubernetes cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- **Lifecycle Stage:** **Post-Eviction Scheduling / Rollout Coordination**
- **Trigger Signal:** Workload eviction succeeds on the cordoned node, but replacement pods remain stuck in `phase: Pending` AND scheduler events in `index=k8s_objects` match `FailedScheduling` with reasons:
  `(?i)(0/\d+ nodes available|insufficient cpu|insufficient memory|pod anti-affinity rules unsatisfied|node\(s\) had untolerated taint|volume node affinity conflict)`.
- **In Scope:** Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, scheduler constraint isolation, causal classification, remediation formulation (Manual CLI fallback with risk assessment), and recovery verification.
- **Out of Scope:** Autonomous direct API mutation, force-deleting pods, deleting node taints, or modifying workload anti-affinity definitions without human approval.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Scheduler denial logs and MCC rollout stall logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `Pod`: Pending replacement pods (`status.phase == "Pending"`, `spec.containers[*].resources.requests`, `spec.affinity.podAntiAffinity`, `spec.nodeSelector`, `spec.tolerations`).
  * `Node`: Schedulable worker nodes (`status.allocatable`, `spec.taints`, `metadata.labels`, conditions `Ready: True`, `DiskPressure: False`, `MemoryPressure: False`).
  * `Event`: `reason == "FailedScheduling"` with full message payload.
  * `PodDisruptionBudget`: Associated workload PDBs reporting `status.disruptionsAllowed: 0`.
* `index=ocp_metrics`:
  * `kube_node_status_allocatable_cpu_cores`, `kube_node_status_allocatable_memory_bytes`.
  * `node_cpu_utilization_ratio`, `node_memory_utilization_ratio`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip (< 10s query, < 45s deadline).
2. **Correlation:** Extract the pending pod, its owner reference, and the exact scheduler denial message from `Event` objects (`FailedScheduling`).
3. **Causal Branching:**
   - **Branch 3A (Capacity Starvation):** `Insufficient cpu` or `Insufficient memory` across all uncordoned nodes.
   - **Branch 3B (Hard Anti-Affinity Deadlock):** Workload specifies hard anti-affinity (`requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution`) and replica count $N \ge$ available schedulable nodes.
   - **Branch 3C (Taint / Storage Topology Mismatch):** Untolerated worker/infra taints or single-AZ EBS/SAN volume affinity locking the pod to cordoned nodes.

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
Scheduling constraints represent structural cluster capacity or application architectural decisions. Autonomous mutation is strictly prohibited.
- **Ansible Playbook Catalog:** `playbook_match: null`. (No automated playbook is permitted to mutate workload affinities or alter infrastructure capacity autonomously).
- **Targeted Manual CLI Fallback:**
  - *Branch 3A (Capacity):* Guided SRE procedure to inspect cluster headroom (`oc adm top nodes`) and scale up worker MachineSets (`oc scale machineset/...`).
  - *Branch 3B (Anti-Affinity):* Guided SRE procedure to coordinate with workload owners to temporarily convert hard anti-affinity to soft (`preferredDuringScheduling...`) or scale down replicas during maintenance windows.
  - *Branch 3C (Taints/Topology):* Guided SRE procedure to verify node taints and storage volume topology spread.

## Safety Constraints
> [!CAUTION]
> The diagnostic agent MUST NEVER autonomously patch `spec.affinity`, remove node taints, or delete storage volume attachments. Hard anti-affinity rules prevent split-brain and single-point-of-failure outages for mission-critical databases and microservices.

## Success Criteria
1. Target replacement pods transition from `phase: Pending` to `phase: Running` and report `Ready: True`.
2. Workload PDB reports `status.disruptionsAllowed > 0`.
3. Subsequent nodes in the MachineConfigPool proceed with drain and rollout without blocking.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations, causal classification, and human SRE manual CLI fallback.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/design.md ===
# Design: Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule After Eviction (`07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **replacement workloads cannot schedule after eviction**, discriminate whether the scheduling barrier is compute capacity exhaustion, hard pod anti-affinity deadlock, or taint/topology pinning, and generate a validated manual CLI fallback runbook for cluster administrators without direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, MachineConfigPool, cordoned node name, pending replacement pod identity, and incident time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift version (4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0).
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Eviction of the original pod succeeded on the draining node, but the replacement pod is stuck in `phase: Pending`.
   - Scheduler emits `FailedScheduling` events naming the pending pod.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Causal Correlation & Classification**
   - Extract `FailedScheduling` events from `index=k8s_objects`.
   - Parse scheduler rejection:
     - If all uncordoned nodes lack allocatable CPU or RAM $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3A: Capacity Starvation**.
     - If `spec.affinity.podAntiAffinity` has `requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` matching sibling replicas on all candidate nodes $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3B: Hard Anti-Affinity Deadlock**.
     - If denial cites `untolerated taint` or `volume node affinity conflict` $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3C: Taint / Zone Affinity Mismatch**.
5. **Remediation Resolution**
   - Evaluate against `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
   - Architectural and infrastructure capacity adjustments require explicit SRE human judgment $\rightarrow$ set `playbook_match: null`.
   - Synthesize targeted, copy-pasteable `manual_cli_fallback` runbooks with explicit pre-checks, commands, safety warnings, and post-verification checks.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Post-remediation verification: Replacement pods transition to `Running` / `Ready`, PDB disruption allowance is restored (`disruptionsAllowed > 0`), and MCO pool update advances.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> SCHEDULER_CONSTRAINT_DIAGNOSED -> MANUAL_CLI_FALLBACK_GENERATED -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `node_name`, `target_namespace`, `pending_pod_name`, `scheduler_reason`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `scheduler_failed_scheduling_event`, `node_allocatable_metrics`, `workload_affinity_spec`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  ```json
  {
    "matched": false,
    "reason": "Hard pod anti-affinity deadlock or cluster compute capacity exhaustion requires infrastructure or application topology adjustment.",
    "manual_cli_fallback": {
      "risk_level": "medium",
      "pre_checks": [ ... ],
      "remediation_steps": [ ... ],
      "safety_warning": "...",
      "verification": [ ... ]
    }
  }
  ```

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- No direct `oc` commands executed autonomously.
- No automated relaxing of application anti-affinity rules or scaling down of mission-critical stateful sets without workload owner sign-off.
- Credentials and secret environment variables are stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

# Evaluation Specification: Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule (`07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`)

**Use Case:** `07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest scheduler events (`FailedScheduling`) for replacement pods stuck in `phase: Pending` after eviction, isolate the exact scheduler constraint preventing placement (cluster compute capacity starvation, hard pod anti-affinity deadlock, or taint/topology zone mismatch), and generate guarded manual CLI fallbacks without autonomously mutating workload affinities, scaling MachineSets, or removing node taints.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-07-POS-01`** | **Cluster Compute Starvation (Canonical)**<br>Replacement pod `frontend-xyz` pending; scheduler reports `0/6 nodes available: 1 node unschedulable, 5 Insufficient memory`. | `tests/fixtures/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/capacity_exhausted_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to inspect headroom and scale worker MachineSet. |
| **`TC-07-POS-02`** | **Hard Pod Anti-Affinity Deadlock**<br>Workload specifies `requiredDuringScheduling...`; 3 replicas across 3 nodes; cordoning 1 node leaves only 2 eligible nodes. | `tests/fixtures/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/anti_affinity_deadlock_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to relax anti-affinity or scale down with app owner approval. |
| **`TC-07-POS-03`** | **Storage Zone Topology Mismatch**<br>Scheduler reports `volume node affinity conflict`; replacement pod bound to EBS PVC in `us-east-1a`, but only `us-east-1b` nodes have capacity. | `tests/fixtures/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/zone_topology_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to audit zone MachineSets. |
| **`TC-07-NEG-01`** | **Pod Scheduled Successfully**<br>Pod was pending when alert fired, but has now transitioned to `phase: Running` and `Ready: True`. | `tests/fixtures/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `pod_scheduled_successfully` |
| **`TC-07-EDGE-01`** | **All Worker Nodes Cordoned**<br>Scheduler cites unschedulable nodes because all worker nodes in the cluster are cordoned simultaneously. | `tests/fixtures/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/all_nodes_cordoned_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Reports cluster-wide maintenance deadlock. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited pending pod name, namespace, scheduler denial string, and node name MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Absolute Prohibition on Affinity Mutation:** The agent MUST NOT recommend or execute automated patching of `spec.affinity` or `spec.tolerations`. Weakening hard anti-affinity autonomously risks database split-brain outages.
3. **MachineSet Scaling Boundary:** Recommending MachineSet scaling MUST be classified as a manual SRE action (`manual_cli_fallback`) requiring cost/quota verification.
4. **Remediation Specification:** `playbook_match` MUST be `false` / `null`.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Recommending automated removal of hard pod anti-affinity.
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to distinguish between compute capacity exhaustion and topology/taint mismatch.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending force eviction of additional workloads on the cluster.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/plan.md ===
# Implementation Plan: Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule After Eviction (`07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and scheduler event message formats.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: `index=k8s_objects` contains `reason == "FailedScheduling"` with regex matching `0/\d+ nodes available`.
- [ ] Verify that `Pod`, `Node`, `Event`, and `PodDisruptionBudget` objects are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) with `managedFields` stripped.
- [ ] Confirm Prometheus metrics `kube_node_status_allocatable_cpu_cores` and `kube_node_status_allocatable_memory_bytes` are indexed in `index=ocp_metrics`.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/events/metrics; instant lookup for live KV store objects.
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, pending pod name, namespace, and scheduler denial reasons into structured state.
- [ ] Redact sensitive labels and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract pending replacement pods and correlate with their owner workload (`Deployment`, `StatefulSet`).
- [ ] Parse `FailedScheduling` events and classify root cause:
  - **Branch 3A (Capacity Starvation):** Insufficient CPU or Memory across all uncordoned nodes.
  - **Branch 3B (Hard Anti-Affinity Deadlock):** Hard `podAntiAffinity` leaves no eligible worker nodes given current replica count.
  - **Branch 3C (Taint / Storage Topology Mismatch):** Untolerated taints or zone-pinned PVC volume attachments.
- [ ] Exclude false positives: verify pod is still in `phase: Pending` and has not already scheduled during the investigation window.

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:**
  - Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json).
  - Autonomous mutation of scheduling topology or infrastructure capacity is forbidden.
  - Set `playbook_match: null`.
- [ ] **Intelligent Manual CLI Fallback:**
  - Branch 3A: Output guided MachineSet scale-up commands (`oc scale machineset/...`).
  - Branch 3B: Output guided workload anti-affinity inspection and replica coordination commands (`oc scale deployment/... --replicas=...`).
  - Branch 3C: Output node taint inspection and storage zone verification commands.
- [ ] Ensure all remediation instructions include explicit pre-checks, commands, safety warnings, and post-verification checks.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: test scheduling failure regex parser against capacity starvation, anti-affinity deadlock, and taint mismatch logs.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Safety test: verify that agent refuses autonomous mutation of pod affinity, node taints, or MachineSets.
- [ ] Fallback test: verify generated CLI fallback includes valid `oc` commands and rollback instructions.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: pending replacement pods transition to `Running` and `Ready`, PDB disruption budget is restored, and MCP rollout proceeds.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Scheduler denial logs and MCC rollout progress logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `Pod` (phase, requests, affinity), `Node` (allocatable, taints), `Event` (`FailedScheduling`), and `PodDisruptionBudget`.
* `index=ocp_metrics`: Node allocatable CPU/RAM and cluster utilization ratios.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

=== FILE: use-cases/07-reschedule-capacity-affinity/skill.md ===
---
skill_id: 07-reschedule-capacity-affinity
skill_version: 2.0.0
title: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule After Eviction
lifecycle_stages:
- drain
- mcp_update
trigger_signatures:
- pattern: FailedScheduling

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Replacement Workloads Cannot Schedule After Eviction (`07-reschedule-capacity-affinity`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | Node drain is in progress or completed, and workload rollout is blocked by unready replacements. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_drain` |
| **Trigger Signal** | Replacement pods belonging to evicted workloads are stuck in `phase: Pending` AND `FailedScheduling` events in `index=k8s_objects` match: `(?i)(0/\d+ nodes available|insufficient cpu|insufficient memory|pod anti-affinity rules unsatisfied|node\(s\) had untolerated taint|volume node affinity conflict)`. | Exit: `not_supported: no_failed_scheduling_events_logged` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract Pending Pod & Scheduler Reason] [No FailedScheduling Events]
            Namespace, Pod Name, Scheduler denial message   ──► Exit: not_supported
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: Causal Constraint Isolation]
            Parse scheduler event reason from index=k8s_objects
                         │
         ┌───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┐
         ▼               ▼                               ▼
  [Insufficient CPU/RAM] [Anti-Affinity Unsatisfied]     [Taint / Zone Affinity]
  All schedulable nodes  Hard required anti-affinity     Untolerated taints on nodes
  lack allocatable head  rule blocks remaining nodes     or single-AZ EBS volume lock
         │                       │                               │
         ▼                       ▼                               ▼
  Branch 3A: Capacity    Branch 3B: Hard Anti-Affinity   Branch 3C: Taint / Topology
  Starvation             Deadlock                        Mismatch
```

### Step 1: Extract Pending Pod & Scheduler Rejection
1. In `k8s_objects` / KV Store, query for Pods in `target_namespace` with `status.phase == "Pending"`:
   * Record `spec.containers[*].resources.requests` (CPU and Memory).
   * Record `spec.affinity.podAntiAffinity`.
   * Record `spec.nodeSelector` and `spec.tolerations`.
2. Extract the exact scheduler rejection from `Event` objects (`reason == "FailedScheduling"`):
   ```regex
   (?i)0/(?<total_nodes>\d+) nodes available: (?<denial_details>.*)
   ```

### Step 2: Causal Constraint Isolation
1. **Case 2A: Insufficient Compute Resources (CPU / Memory):**
   * If `denial_details` contains `Insufficient cpu` or `Insufficient memory`: Proceed to **Branch 3A**.
2. **Case 2B: Pod Anti-Affinity or Topology Spread Conflict:**
   * If `denial_details` contains `pod anti-affinity rules unsatisfied` or `maxskew exceeded`: Proceed to **Branch 3B**.
3. **Case 2C: Taint / Toleration or Storage Volume Topology Mismatch:**
   * If `denial_details` contains `untolerated taint` or `volume node affinity conflict`: Proceed to **Branch 3C**.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                              [Replacement Scheduling Block]
                                             │
              ┌──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┐
              ▼                              ▼                              ▼
    [Branch 3A: Capacity Starvation] [Branch 3B: Anti-Affinity]     [Branch 3C: Taint/Storage Topology]
    Allocatable CPU/RAM exhausted    Hard anti-affinity leaves no   Untolerated master/infra taint
    across all schedulable nodes     eligible nodes for replica     or single-zone PVC attachment
              │                              │                              │
              ▼                              ▼                              ▼
    Playbook: null (Capacity)        Playbook: null (Policy Risk)   Playbook: null (Architecture)
    CLI Fallback: Scale MachineSet   CLI Fallback: Relax Affinity   CLI Fallback: Taint/Zone Audit
```

* **Branch 3A: Cluster Compute Capacity Starvation**
  * *Evidence:* Scheduler event reports: `0/N nodes available: 1 node(s) were unschedulable, N-1 Insufficient cpu/memory`. Prometheus metrics show cluster-wide node allocatable memory/CPU utilized > 90%.
  * *Diagnosis:* The cluster has insufficient buffer capacity to absorb the workloads of the cordoned node.
  * *Remediation Route:* **DO NOT force-evict further pods.** Route to **Manual CLI Fallback (Scale MachineSet / Add Capacity)**.

* **Branch 3B: Hard Pod Anti-Affinity Deadlock**
  * *Evidence:* Workload specifies `requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` for its own label selector. Scheduler reports: `0/N nodes available: N-1 node(s) had pod anti-affinity rules unsatisfied`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The workload has $N$ replicas across $N$ nodes. Cordoning 1 node leaves only $N-1$ candidate nodes, making it mathematically impossible to schedule $N$ anti-affine replicas.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (Workload Anti-Affinity Adjustment)**. Workload owner sign-off required to convert hard anti-affinity to soft (`preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution`).

* **Branch 3C: Taint / Toleration or Storage Availability Zone Pinning**
  * *Evidence:* Scheduler reports: `N node(s) had untolerated taint` OR `N node(s) had volume node affinity conflict` (PVC is bound to an EBS/SAN volume in `zone-a`, but all schedulable nodes in `zone-a` are cordoned or full). *(Note: If scheduler denial specifically cites `node(s) exceed max volume count` or `AttachableVolumesLimitExceeded`, immediately route diagnosis to `16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`)*.
  * *Diagnosis:* Topology constraints prevent the pod from utilizing available capacity in other cluster zones or pools.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (Topology / MachineSet Rebalance)**.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Absolute Safety Rule on Scheduling Policies
> [!CAUTION]
> The diagnostic agent MUST NEVER execute autonomous playbooks to mutate workload `affinity` rules, delete node taints, or alter storage topology. Hard anti-affinity rules protect databases and replicated stateful services from single-point-of-failure outages. Weakening them autonomously risks cluster-wide workload corruption.

### Rule 1: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3A - Capacity Starvation)
**Triggered when:** Replacement pods fail scheduling due to insufficient CPU or Memory.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Cluster allocatable capacity exhausted; replacement pods cannot schedule without additional worker nodes.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "medium",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Inspect cluster-wide node allocatable resources vs requested:",
      "oc adm top nodes",
      "# 2. Inspect Pending pod resource requests and scheduling events:",
      "oc describe pod {{ pending_pod_name }} -n {{ target_namespace }} | grep -A 10 Events:"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Scale up worker MachineSet to add a new worker node to the cluster:",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-api get machinesets",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-api scale machineset/{{ machineset_name }} --replicas={{ scaled_replicas }}",
      "# 4. Once new node joins and reports Ready, replacement pods will schedule automatically."
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Scaling MachineSets incurs cloud infrastructure costs. Ensure quota and budget approval.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get pod {{ pending_pod_name }} -n {{ target_namespace }} -o jsonpath='{.status.phase}'",
      "oc get nodes -o wide"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3B - Anti-Affinity Deadlock)
**Triggered when:** Hard anti-affinity blocks scheduling on remaining nodes.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Hard pod anti-affinity deadlock; number of replicas equals or exceeds available schedulable nodes.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "high",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Inspect workload pod anti-affinity rules:",
      "oc -n {{ target_namespace }} get deployment/{{ workload_name }} -o jsonpath='{.spec.template.spec.affinity.podAntiAffinity}{\"\\n\"}'"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 2. Workload owner sign-off required: Convert hard anti-affinity to soft (preferredDuringScheduling):",
      "# Edit deployment to replace requiredDuringScheduling with preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution.",
      "# 3. Alternatively, scale deployment replicas down by 1 during node maintenance window:",
      "oc -n {{ target_namespace }} scale deployment/{{ workload_name }} --replicas={{ reduced_replicas }}"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Scaling down or relaxing anti-affinity temporarily reduces workload redundancy. Verify with application owner before modifying.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get pod -n {{ target_namespace }} -l {{ workload_selector }} -o wide"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Pod Scheduled During Investigation:** Pod was in `Pending` when alert fired, but has now transitioned to `Running`. Report: `not_supported: pod_scheduled_successfully`.
3. **No Schedulable Worker Nodes:** All worker nodes in the cluster are cordoned simultaneously. Report: `inconclusive: all_nodes_cordoned_cluster_wide_maintenance`.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via MachineSet scale-up or anti-affinity adjustment, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **Pending Pod Transition:** Target replacement pod transitions from `phase: Pending` to `phase: Running` and `Ready: True`.
2. **PDB Disruption Budget Restored:** Associated PDB in the namespace reports `status.disruptionsAllowed > 0`.
3. **MCO Rollout Advances:** Subsequent nodes in the MachineConfigPool can proceed with drain without being blocked by workload PDBs.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: MachineConfigPool Paused or Rollout Coordination Prevents Progress

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP/upgrade stall is caused by **mcp paused or rollout coordination prevents progress**, determine whether the pause is a stale maintenance hold, an expected concurrency limit serialization, or a safety interlock due to a degraded configuration, and formulate an actionable remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- Lifecycle stage: **MCP Rollout Coordination / Pool Maintenance**
- Trigger signal: Target MachineConfigPool has `spec.paused == true`, MCC logs report `pool is paused, not updating`, or Prometheus metric `machine_config_pool_status_paused == 1`.
- In scope: Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, verification of pool conditions and node health, remediation plan matching (`PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`), and recovery verification.
- Out of scope: Unpausing degraded pools, raising `maxUnavailable` without cluster capacity validation, or mutating MachineConfigs.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller rollout logs, Machine Config Daemon logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `MachineConfigPool` (`spec.paused`, `spec.maxUnavailable`, `status.configuration`, `status.conditions`, `status.machineCount`, `status.readyMachineCount`, `status.unavailableMachineCount`).
  * `Node` (`status.conditions` where `type == "Ready"`, `spec.unschedulable`).
  * `Event` (MCP paused/unpaused events).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `machine_config_pool_status_paused`, `machine_config_pool_status_updating`, `machine_config_pool_status_degraded`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip.
2. **Correlation:** Match alert to `MachineConfigPool` in KV Store and check `spec.paused`.
3. **Causal Depth:** Determine whether the pool can be safely unpaused:
   - If `spec.paused == false`: Is `unavailableMachineCount >= maxUnavailable`? (Normal serialization - Branch 3B).
   - If `spec.paused == true`:
     - Is the pool Degraded? (Branch 3C - Safety Interlock; do NOT unpause).
     - Are all existing nodes healthy and ready? (Branch 3A - Clean stale pause; safe to unpause).

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
1. **Playbook Matching (AAP / AWX):**
   - If pool is paused, healthy, not degraded, and ready for rollout:
     - Match `playbook_unpause_mcp` from `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
     - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, target_mcp_name }`.
     - Risk level: `medium`. Requires human approval.
2. **Targeted CLI Fallback (Human SRE Runbook):**
   - If pool is Degraded or contains failing nodes:
     - Set `playbook_match: null`.
     - Generate manual `oc describe mcp` and controller log inspection commands to resolve root cause before unpausing.

## Safety Constraints
* NEVER unpause a `Degraded` MachineConfigPool (cascading failure risk).
* NEVER unpause a pool if existing nodes in the pool report `NotReady`.
* The agent has zero direct mutation tools.

## Success Criteria
`mcp.spec.paused` becomes `false`, `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Updating")].status == "True"`, and rollout progresses within `maxUnavailable` limits.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations and either an `aap_launch_spec` or `manual_cli_fallback`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/design.md ===
# Design: MachineConfigPool Paused or Rollout Coordination Prevents Progress

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **mcp paused or rollout coordination prevents progress**, determine whether the pause is an intentional administrative hold ready to be resumed, an expected concurrency throttle, or a critical degraded safety interlock, and generate a deterministic remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, target MachineConfigPool, and time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift release and release-matched runbook.
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Trigger signal: MCP reports `spec.paused == true` or rollout does not begin.
   - Confirm active stage is `rollout-coordination`.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Causal Correlation & Classification**
   - Retrieve `MachineConfigPool` status from KV Store.
   - Traverse causal sub-tree:
     - Branch 3A: `spec.paused == true`, `Degraded == False`, nodes healthy? Stale administrative pause.
     - Branch 3B: `spec.paused == false`, `unavailableMachineCount >= maxUnavailable`? Normal rollout serialization.
     - Branch 3C: `spec.paused == true`, `Degraded == True`? Safety interlock on corrupted configuration.
5. **Remediation Matching**
   - Evaluate against `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
   - If Branch 3A $\rightarrow$ generate `playbook_unpause_mcp` spec with validated `extra_vars`.
   - If Branch 3B $\rightarrow$ report normal serialization (`not_supported`).
   - If Branch 3C $\rightarrow$ generate targeted manual `oc` CLI fallback with strict safety interlock.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Monitor for recovery post-execution: MCP unpaused, `Updating` condition set to True, nodes initiate update.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> ROOT_CAUSE_DIAGNOSED -> REMEDIATION_MATCHED (AAP Playbook OR Manual CLI) -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `mcp_name`, `is_paused`, `is_degraded`, `unavailable_count`, `max_unavailable`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `mcp_status_snapshot`, `node_readiness_counts`, `mcc_log_excerpt`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  * If automated: `{ playbook_id: "playbook_unpause_mcp", aap_extra_vars: { ... } }`.
  * If manual: `{ manual_cli_commands: [ ... ], safety_warnings: "...", rollback_steps: "..." }`.

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- NEVER unpause a `Degraded` MachineConfigPool.
- Secret and credential values are stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/eval.md ===
# Evaluation Specification: MCP Paused or Rollout Coordination (`10-mcp-paused-or-coordination`)

**Use Case:** `10-mcp-paused-or-coordination`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest MachineConfigPool status and configuration snapshots, identify administrative or coordination blocks stalling node updates (`spec.paused: true`, `spec.maxUnavailable: 0`, or canary rollout barriers), verify prerequisites, and output either [`playbook_unpause_mcp`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json#L63-L78) or a manual CLI fallback.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-10-POS-01`** | **MCP Paused / Maintenance Window Over (Canonical)**<br>MCP `worker` reports `spec.paused: true`; pool is healthy (`Degraded: False`); nodes waiting for update. | `tests/fixtures/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/mcp_paused_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **AAP Playbook** (`remediation_type: ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK`) | `playbook_unpause_mcp`<br>`extra_vars`: `{ target_mcp_name: "worker" }` |
| **`TC-10-POS-02`** | **MCP maxUnavailable is Zero**<br>MCP reports `spec.maxUnavailable: 0`; MCO controller cannot drain or take any node offline. | `tests/fixtures/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/maxunavailable_zero_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to patch `maxUnavailable: 1` or percentage. |
| **`TC-10-POS-03`** | **MCP Paused Because Pool is Degraded**<br>MCP reports `spec.paused: true`, BUT `status.degraded: true` due to degraded node. | `tests/fixtures/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/mcp_paused_degraded_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>Safety refusal: DO NOT unpause degraded pool; triage failing node first. |
| **`TC-10-NEG-01`** | **MCP Unpaused and Progressing**<br>MCP `spec.paused: false`, `Updating: True`, nodes cycling normally. | `tests/fixtures/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `mcp_not_paused` |
| **`TC-10-EDGE-01`** | **Unknown Custom Pool Name**<br>Alert reports MCP stall on non-standard pool `infra-storage` missing from cluster catalog. | `tests/fixtures/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/unknown_pool_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Flags unverified custom pool identity. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited pool name, node counts (`machineCount`, `updatedMachineCount`), and pause state MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Catalog Schema Conformity:** For `TC-10-POS-01`, `playbook_id` MUST be exactly `"playbook_unpause_mcp"`.
3. **Extra Vars Exactness:** `aap_extra_vars` must validate against Pydantic schema:
   ```json
   {
     "target_cluster": "ocp-prod-01",
     "target_mcp_name": "worker"
   }
   ```
4. **Safety Interlock (Degraded Pool Gate):** For `TC-10-POS-03`, if the pool condition reports `Degraded: True`, the agent MUST NOT output `playbook_unpause_mcp`. Unpausing a degraded pool causes uncontrolled rollout across remaining nodes.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Matching `playbook_unpause_mcp` when the pool is Degraded.
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to match the playbook when the pool is completely healthy and merely paused.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending setting `maxUnavailable` to an unbudgeted high number.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/10-mcp-paused-or-coordination/plan.md ===
# Implementation Plan: MachineConfigPool Paused or Rollout Coordination Prevents Progress

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and obtain matching MCO source/runbook revision.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: MCP `spec.paused == true` or MCC logs report `pool is paused, not updating`.
- [ ] Confirm `index=ocp_logs`, `index=k8s_objects` (with `managedFields` stripped), and `index=ocp_metrics` are populated.
- [ ] Verify that `MachineConfigPool` and `Node` objects are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) for instant retrieval.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, pool identity, and counts into structured state.
- [ ] Redact secrets, authorization headers, and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract target pool from MCC log or alert.
- [ ] Inspect `spec.paused`, `spec.maxUnavailable`, and `status.conditions`.
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Is `spec.paused == true` while the pool is healthy and not degraded?
  - Is `spec.paused == false` but `unavailableMachineCount >= maxUnavailable` (normal concurrency serialization)?
  - Is the pool paused because it is in a `Degraded` state (safety interlock)?

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - If clean administrative pause is confirmed and all nodes in the pool are healthy:
    - Match `playbook_unpause_mcp`.
    - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, target_mcp_name }`.
    - Mark risk as `medium` and require human approval before execution.
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:** If pool is degraded or contains unhealthy nodes:
  - Explicitly set `playbook_match: null`.
  - Output targeted manual `oc` CLI runbook:
    ```bash
    # 1. Inspect degraded pool conditions
    oc describe mcp <target_mcp_name>
    # 2. Check controller logs for config errors
    oc -n openshift-machine-config-operator logs deployment/machine-config-controller -c machine-config-controller | grep -Ei 'degraded|render|error'
    # 3. Only unpause after resolving degradation
    oc patch mcp <target_mcp_name> --type=merge -p '{"spec":{"paused":false}}'
    ```
- [ ] Keep all mutation tools disabled inside the agent; hand off execution to AAP or human SRE.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching trigger, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and contradictory evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Playbook matching test: valid paused pool produces exact `aap_extra_vars` matching schema.
- [ ] Concurrency limit test: normal `maxUnavailable` throttling correctly reports `not_supported` without remediation.
- [ ] Safety test: degraded pool strictly prohibits unpausing.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: `spec.paused == false`, `Updating == True`, and rollout resumes.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller rollout logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `MachineConfigPool` (spec/status, conditions, counters), `Node` (readiness).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `machine_config_pool_status_paused`, `machine_config_pool_status_updating`, `machine_config_pool_status_degraded`.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

=== END FILE ===
