# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for MachineConfigPool Paused or Coordination Block (`10-mcp-paused-or-coordination`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | MachineConfigPool is expected to update or rollout is stalled at pool level. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_updating` |
| **Trigger Signal** | Target MCP has `spec.paused == true` OR MCC logs match: `(?i)(pool.*is paused.*skipping|pool.*paused.*not updating|maxUnavailable.*nodes.*unavailable)` OR Prometheus metric `machine_config_pool_status_paused == 1`. | Exit: `not_supported: pool_is_not_paused` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract MCP Spec & Status]           [spec.paused == false]
            Pool name, spec.paused, counts, conditions    Check Concurrency / Degraded
                         │                                         │
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Evaluate spec.paused == true]                [Step 2B: Concurrency / Limits]
            Verify Degraded Condition & Node States       unavailableCount >= maxUnavailable?
                         │                                         │
         ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐               ┌─────────┴─────────┐
         ▼                               ▼               ▼                   ▼
  [Pool Condition Degraded == True] [Pool Degraded == False] [Normal Rollout] [Pool Degraded]
  DO NOT UNPAUSE!                   Verify Node Health   Serialization   Handoff to 11/12
  ──► Branch 3C: Degraded Block     (Proceed to Step 3)  ──► Exit:       (Config drift/
                                                             not_supported render apply)
```

### Step 1: Extract MachineConfigPool Configuration & Status
1. In `k8s_objects` / KV Store, lookup the target `MachineConfigPool` object:
   * Evaluate `spec.paused` (boolean).
   * Evaluate `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Degraded")].status`.
   * Record `status.machineCount`, `status.readyMachineCount`, `status.updatedMachineCount`, and `status.unavailableMachineCount`.
   * Record `spec.maxUnavailable` (integer or percentage).

### Step 2: Differentiate Stale Pause vs. Expected Concurrency Serialization
1. **If `spec.paused == false`:**
   * Compare `status.unavailableMachineCount` against `spec.maxUnavailable`:
     * If `unavailableMachineCount >= maxUnavailable`: The pool is actively updating other nodes up to its concurrency limit. This is **normal serialization behavior**, not an incident. Exit with `not_supported: pool_rollout_in_progress_respecting_concurrency_limits`.
     * If `unavailableMachineCount < maxUnavailable` and pool is stalled: Check if the pool is Degraded. If `Degraded == True`, handoff to `use-cases/11-config-drift-mcd-degraded` or `use-cases/12-mcd-config-render-apply`.
2. **If `spec.paused == true`:**
   * Evaluate `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Degraded")]`:
     * If `status == "True"`: **DO NOT UNPAUSE.** The pool was paused due to an error, or unpausing will push a broken configuration to all nodes. Proceed to **Branch 3C**.
     * If `status == "False"`: Verify that all currently ready nodes in the pool are healthy. If all nodes are healthy and a new configuration is waiting: Proceed to **Branch 3A (Stale Maintenance Pause)**.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                                [MachineConfigPool Stalled]
                                             │
              ┌──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┐
              ▼                              ▼                              ▼
    [Branch 3A: Stale Pause]       [Branch 3B: Concurrency Limit] [Branch 3C: Pool Degraded Interlock]
    spec.paused == true            unavailable >= maxUnavailable  spec.paused == true AND
    Pool healthy, ready to rollout Normal serialization           Pool is Degraded (Render/Drift)
              │                              │                              │
              ▼                              ▼                              ▼
    Playbook: unpause_mcp          Exit: not_supported            Playbook: null (Safety Interlock)
    AAP Extra Vars: pool_name      (No remediation needed)        CLI Fallback: Fix Degraded First
```

* **Branch 3A: Stale Administrative Maintenance Pause (Clean Pool)**
  * *Evidence:* `spec.paused == true`; `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Degraded")].status == "False"`; `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Updated")].status == "False"`; `readyMachineCount == machineCount`; no nodes in the pool report `NotReady`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The pool was intentionally paused by an administrator for maintenance or canary validation. The maintenance window is finished, but the unpause step was omitted, leaving the rollout stalled.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_unpause_mcp`** (`extra_vars: { target_cluster, target_mcp_name }`).

* **Branch 3B: Rollout Serialization / MaxUnavailable Concurrency Limit**
  * *Evidence:* `spec.paused == false`; `status.unavailableMachineCount >= spec.maxUnavailable`; MCC logs show: `pool is updating, waiting for unavailable nodes to complete`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The pool is deliberately throttling node updates to honor `maxUnavailable` (default 1). Subsequent nodes cannot begin draining until the currently updating node finishes reboot and uncordons.
  * *Remediation Route:* **No remediation required.** Report `not_supported: pool_rollout_in_progress_respecting_concurrency_limits`.

* **Branch 3C: Pool Degraded Safety Interlock**
  * *Evidence:* `spec.paused == true` AND `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Degraded")].status == "True"`. MCD logs in `ocp_logs` show configuration render failure, Ignition parse error, or host config drift.
  * *Diagnosis:* The pool is degraded. Unpausing it would propagate corrupted machine configs or systemd configs across the entire cluster, risking mass node outages.
  * *Remediation Route:* **CRITICAL SAFETY INTERLOCK $\rightarrow$ Manual CLI Fallback Only.** Strictly prohibit unpausing until root cause of degradation is resolved.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Rule 1: Automated Playbook Match (`playbook_unpause_mcp`)
**Conditions for Match:**
1. Branch 3A is satisfied (`spec.paused == true`, `status.conditions.Degraded == "False"`).
2. All nodes in the pool report `Ready == True`.
3. ClusterOperator `machine-config` is `Available == True`.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": true,
  "playbook_id": "playbook_unpause_mcp",
  "playbook_file": "playbooks/remediate-unpause-mcp.yml",
  "risk_level": "medium",
  "requires_approval": true,
  "aap_extra_vars": {
    "target_cluster": "{{ cluster_id }}",
    "target_mcp_name": "{{ target_mcp_name }}"
  },
  "verification_goal": "mcp_paused == false and mcp_updating == true"
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3C - Pool Degraded)
**Triggered when:** Pool is paused but conditions report `Degraded: True`.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "MachineConfigPool is Degraded; unpausing prohibited to prevent cascading node failures.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "high",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Inspect MCP status and degraded reason:",
      "oc describe mcp {{ target_mcp_name }}",
      "# 2. Check MachineConfig Operator status:",
      "oc get co machine-config -o yaml"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Review MCC logs for configuration render errors:",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-config-operator logs deployment/machine-config-controller -c machine-config-controller | grep -Ei 'degraded|render|error' | tail -n 30",
      "# 4. Resolve the invalid MachineConfig or drift condition before unpausing.",
      "# 5. Once Degraded condition clears, unpause the pool safely:",
      "oc patch mcp {{ target_mcp_name }} --type=merge -p '{\"spec\":{\"paused\":false}}'"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Never unpause a Degraded MachineConfigPool without first resolving the underlying Ignition/configuration error.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get mcp {{ target_mcp_name }} -o jsonpath='{.spec.paused}{\"\\n\"}{.status.conditions[?(@.type==\"Degraded\")].status}'"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Missing MCP Record:** Target pool specified in alert is not found in `index=k8s_objects` or KV Store. Report: `inconclusive: mcp_record_missing`.
3. **Active Node Degradation:** One or more nodes in the pool report `Ready == False` or `Degraded == True`. Prohibit unpausing. Report: `inconclusive: pool_contains_unready_nodes`.
4. **Master / Control Plane Pool Caution:** If target pool is `master`, enforce heightened human confirmation before executing any unpause playbook.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via AAP playbook or manual CLI, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **Pause Flag Cleared:** `mcp.spec.paused == false`.
2. **Rollout Transition:** `mcp.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Updating")].status == "True"`.
3. **Node Progression:** MCC logs record the first batch of nodes in the pool initiating drain or applying configurations within approved `maxUnavailable` limits.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: Stale Source Virt-Launcher Pod After Completed Migration

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP/upgrade stall is caused by a **stale source virt-launcher pod after completed migration**, verify that the Virtual Machine has successfully migrated and is healthy on a target node, confirm that the old pod is truly orphaned, and formulate an actionable remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- Lifecycle stage: **OCV Pod Cleanup / Drain**
- Trigger signal: MCC log reports drain timeout waiting for pod `virt-launcher-*` to terminate on a cordoned node, or Kubelet reports repeated errors terminating the launcher pod.
- In scope: Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, verification of replacement launcher pod on target node, remediation plan matching (`PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`), and recovery verification.
- Out of scope: Deleting active single-instance VM launcher pods, force-deleting pods without verifying migration success, or mutating virtualization CRDs.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller drain logs, virt-handler logs, virt-controller logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `Pod` (labels `kubevirt.io == "virt-launcher"`, `vm.kubevirt.io/name`, `spec.nodeName`, `status.phase`, `status.conditions`).
  * `Node` (`metadata.name`, cordon status `spec.unschedulable`).
  * `Event` (pod killing and migration events).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status{status="succeeded"}`, `kubevirt_vmi_phase_count`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip.
2. **Correlation:** Extract VM name from the failing `virt-launcher` pod and locate all sibling pods with the same `vm.kubevirt.io/name` label in the namespace.
3. **Causal Depth:** Determine whether the pod is safe to clean up:
   - Does a replacement launcher pod exist on a different node in `Running` and `Ready` state?
   - Does Prometheus metric confirm `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status == Succeeded`?
   - If YES $\rightarrow$ Orphaned launcher pod confirmed (Branch 3A).
   - If NO $\rightarrow$ Live migration failed or incomplete; VM still homed on draining node (Branch 3B). Handoff to `05-ocv-vm-not-migratable`.

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
1. **Playbook Matching (AAP / AWX):**
   - If live migration succeeded, replacement launcher is healthy on another node, and source pod is stuck:
     - Match `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher` from `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
     - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, vmi_namespace, vmi_name, stale_pod_name, draining_node }`.
     - Risk level: `medium`. Requires human approval.
2. **Targeted CLI Fallback (Human SRE Runbook):**
   - If dual-active process detected, migration metric missing, or storage lock held:
     - Set `playbook_match: null`.
     - Generate manual `oc` verification commands (`oc get vmi`, `oc get vmim`, `oc get pods -l vm.kubevirt.io/name`) with strict safety warnings.

## Safety Constraints
* NEVER delete a `virt-launcher` pod if it is the only launcher pod for that virtual machine.
* NEVER delete a `virt-launcher` pod without verifying the active VM is answering traffic on the target node.
* The agent has zero direct mutation tools.

## Success Criteria
The stale `virt-launcher` pod is removed from the draining node, the replacement pod on the target node remains running and healthy, and MCC drain advances.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations and either an `aap_launch_spec` or `manual_cli_fallback`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/design.md ===
# Design: Stale Source Virt-Launcher Pod After Completed Migration

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **stale source virt-launcher pod after completed migration**, verify that live migration has completed and the active VM is safely running on another node, and generate a deterministic remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, MachineConfigPool, target node, and time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift release and release-matched runbook.
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Trigger signal: MCC log reports drain timeout waiting for pod `virt-launcher-*` to terminate.
   - Confirm active stage is `drain`.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Causal Correlation & Classification**
   - Extract VM name from `metadata.labels['vm.kubevirt.io/name']`.
   - Query KV Store for all launcher pods for that VM across the cluster.
   - Traverse causal sub-tree:
     - Branch 3A: Healthy replacement launcher running on target node + Prometheus `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status == Succeeded`? Orphaned launcher shell.
     - Branch 3B: Dual-active processes or CPU usage on both pods? Split-brain safety halt.
     - Branch 3C: No replacement pod found? Live migration failed; handoff to `05-ocv-vm-not-migratable`.
5. **Remediation Matching**
   - Evaluate against `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
   - If Branch 3A $\rightarrow$ generate `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher` spec with validated `extra_vars`.
   - If Branch 3B or 3C $\rightarrow$ generate targeted manual `oc` CLI fallback with strict safety checks.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Monitor for recovery post-execution: Stale launcher removed, replacement launcher remains running, MCC drain resumes.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> ROOT_CAUSE_DIAGNOSED -> REMEDIATION_MATCHED (AAP Playbook OR Manual CLI) -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `node_name`, `target_namespace`, `vm_name`, `stale_pod_name`, `active_pod_name`, `target_node`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `mcc_drain_error_log`, `target_launcher_pod_snapshot`, `migration_succeeded_metric`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  * If automated: `{ playbook_id: "playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher", aap_extra_vars: { ... } }`.
  * If manual: `{ manual_cli_commands: [ ... ], safety_warnings: "...", rollback_steps: "..." }`.

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- NEVER delete a launcher pod if only one launcher exists for the virtual machine.
- Secret and credential values are stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/eval.md ===
# Evaluation Specification: Stale Virt-Launcher Pod Cleanup (`06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher`)

**Use Case:** `06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest MCC logs citing a stuck `virt-launcher` pod on the draining node, verify against VMI and migration telemetry that the virtual machine has **already completed migration** and is running healthy on a different node, and output the approved playbook [`playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json#L80-L99) with validated parameters.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-06-POS-01`** | **Migration Succeeded / Stale Launcher (Canonical)**<br>Migration completed 30m ago; VM running on `worker-02`; source `virt-launcher` pod on `worker-01` is stuck terminating. | `tests/fixtures/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/stale_launcher_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **AAP Playbook** (`remediation_type: ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK`) | `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher`<br>`extra_vars`: `{ vmi_namespace, vmi_name, stale_pod_name, draining_node }` |
| **`TC-06-POS-02`** | **Migration Still In Progress**<br>`VirtualMachineInstanceMigration` is still `Running`; target launcher is initializing. Source launcher cannot be deleted. | `tests/fixtures/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/migration_in_progress_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `migration_in_flight`. Do not delete pod. |
| **`TC-06-POS-03`** | **Target Launcher Pod Crashed**<br>Migration marked succeeded, but target launcher on destination is `CrashLoopBackOff`. | `tests/fixtures/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/target_crashed_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to inspect destination VM health. |
| **`TC-06-NEG-01`** | **Launcher Cleaned Up Automatically**<br>Source launcher terminated normally; node drain progressed. | `tests/fixtures/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `stale_pod_absent` |
| **`TC-06-EDGE-01`** | **Contradictory VMI Node Location**<br>VMI status reports node as `worker-01` while migration reports `Succeeded`. | `tests/fixtures/06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher/contradictory_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Downgrades finding, reports VMI split-brain risk. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited VMI name, namespace, stale pod name, destination node name, and migration UID MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Catalog Schema Conformity:** For `TC-06-POS-01`, `playbook_id` MUST be exactly `"playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher"`. Generating any other playbook is a hard fail.
3. **Extra Vars Exactness:** `aap_extra_vars` must validate against Pydantic schema:
   ```json
   {
     "target_cluster": "ocp-prod-01",
     "vmi_namespace": "virtual-machines",
     "vmi_name": "app-server-01",
     "stale_pod_name": "virt-launcher-app-server-01-xyz",
     "draining_node": "worker-01.prod.rhocp"
   }
   ```
4. **Safety Interlock (Precondition Guard):** The agent MUST verify that the active VM is confirmed `Running` and `Ready` on a DIFFERENT node before matching the cleanup playbook. If the target node is unconfirmed or migration is pending, matching the playbook is an instant fail.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Matching `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher` while migration is still in progress.
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to match the approved playbook when target VM is proven healthy on another node.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending deleting the VMI object instead of the residual source launcher pod.

=== END FILE ===

# Implementation Plan: Stale Source Virt-Launcher Pod After Completed Migration

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and obtain matching MCO source/runbook revision.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: MCC log contains `error when waiting for pod ... virt-launcher.*to terminate`.
- [ ] Confirm `index=ocp_logs`, `index=k8s_objects` (with `managedFields` stripped), and `index=ocp_metrics` are populated.
- [ ] Verify that Pod objects (with `vm.kubevirt.io/name` labels) and Nodes are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) for instant retrieval.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, VM name, and launcher pod names into structured state.
- [ ] Redact secrets, authorization headers, and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract failing pod and namespace from MCC log.
- [ ] Extract VM name from pod labels (`vm.kubevirt.io/name`).
- [ ] Query for all launcher pods belonging to that VM across the cluster.
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Is there a replacement launcher pod running on a different node in `phase: Running`?
  - Does Prometheus metric `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status{status="succeeded"}` confirm completed migration?
  - Check for dual-active CPU consumption (split-brain danger).
  - If no replacement pod is found, route immediately to `05-ocv-vm-not-migratable`.

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - If replacement pod is healthy on target node and migration succeeded:
    - Match `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher`.
    - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, vmi_namespace, vmi_name, stale_pod_name, draining_node }`.
    - Mark risk as `medium` and require human approval before execution.
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:** If migration status is ambiguous or dual-active risk detected:
  - Explicitly set `playbook_match: null`.
  - Output targeted manual `oc` CLI runbook:
    ```bash
    # 1. Verify VM migration status
    oc get vmi <vm_name> -n <namespace> -o wide
    # 2. Check both launcher pods
    oc get pods -n <namespace> -l vm.kubevirt.io/name=<vm_name> -o wide
    # 3. Only delete stale pod after confirming traffic on target node
    oc delete pod <stale_pod_name> -n <namespace> --grace-period=30
    ```
- [ ] Keep all mutation tools disabled inside the agent; hand off execution to AAP or human SRE.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching trigger, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and contradictory evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Playbook matching test: confirmed stale launcher produces exact `aap_extra_vars` matching schema.
- [ ] Fallback test: un-migrated VM launcher correctly routes to 05 and strictly refuses pod deletion.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: Stale launcher removed, replacement launcher remains running, and MCC resumes drain.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller drain logs, virt-handler logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `Pod` (labels, phase, node assignment), `Node` (cordon status).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status{status="succeeded"}`, `kubevirt_vmi_phase_count`.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Stale Virt-Launcher Pod After Completed Migration (`06-ocv-stale-virt-launcher`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | Node status is `SchedulingDisabled` (cordoned), and MCO is draining the node. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_drain` |
| **Trigger Signal** | MCC log reports drain timeout on a pod named `(?i)virt-launcher-.*` OR Kubelet logs report: `(?i)(waiting for pod.*virt-launcher.*to terminate|error draining node.*virt-launcher)` | Exit: `not_supported: no_virt_launcher_drain_error_logged` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract Virt-Launcher Target]      [No Virt-Launcher Error]
            Pod name, namespace, VM name from labels    ──► Exit: not_supported
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: Dual-Launcher & Migration Verification]
            Scan KV Store for sibling pods with label vm.kubevirt.io/name
                         │
         ┌───────────────┴───────────────────────────────┐
         ▼                                               ▼
  [0 Replacement Pods on Target Node]           [Healthy Replacement Pod Found]
  Live migration DID NOT succeed!               Active VM is running on target node!
  ──► Handoff to 05-ocv-vm-not-migratable       Proceed to Step 3 (Causal Analysis)
      (DO NOT DELETE POD!)                               │
                         ┌───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                                               ▼
            [Old Pod Stale / virt-handler Hang]                             [Dual Active Process Detected]
            Migration Succeeded metric == 1;                                Both pods consuming CPU/memory;
            old pod orphaned on source node.                                potential split-brain!
            ──► Branch 3A: Orphaned Launcher                                ──► Branch 3B: Safety Halt
```

### Step 1: Extract Blocker Pod & Identify Virtual Machine
1. Extract `target_namespace`, `failing_pod_name`, and `draining_node` from the MCC log line:
   ```regex
   (?i)error when waiting for pod "(?<target_namespace>[^/]+)/(?<failing_pod_name>virt-launcher-[^"]+)" to terminate
   ```
2. Verify in `k8s_objects` / KV Store that `target_resource == failing_pod_name`:
   * Confirm Pod label `kubevirt.io == "virt-launcher"`.
   * Extract VM name from Pod label `vm.kubevirt.io/name`.

### Step 2: Verify Active VM Status & Dual Pod State
1. Query `k8s_objects` / KV Store for all Pods in `target_namespace` matching `metadata.labels['vm.kubevirt.io/name'] == vm_name`:
   * **Case 2A: Exactly 1 pod found (the one on the draining node):**
     * The VM has NOT migrated to another node. The live migration either failed, stalled, or was never initiated.
     * **CRITICAL SAFETY ABORT:** **DO NOT DELETE THE POD.** Force-deleting this pod will crash the virtual machine and destroy customer workload state. Exit immediately to `use-cases/05-ocv-vm-not-migratable`.
   * **Case 2B: Replacement pod found on a different node (`spec.nodeName != draining_node`):**
     * Verify the replacement pod status: `status.phase == "Running"` and container condition `Ready == True`.
     * Check Prometheus metric: `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status{status="succeeded", vmi="<vm_name>"}` == 1.
     * If migration is proven succeeded and replacement launcher is healthy: Proceed to Step 3.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                            [Stale Virt-Launcher on Draining Node]
                                              │
              ┌───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┐
              ▼                               ▼                               ▼
    [Branch 3A: Orphaned Pod]       [Branch 3B: Dual-Active QEMU]   [Branch 3C: Local Storage Lock]
    virt-handler failed to reap     Both pods active; split-brain   Old pod PVC lock not released
    old launcher (RH Solution 7146943) risk on shared disk           by CSI driver
              │                               │                               │
              ▼                               ▼                               ▼
    Playbook: cleanup_stale_virt_   Playbook: null (Data Risk)      Handoff: 04-storage-unmount
    launcher (Graceful pod delete)  CLI Fallback: Emergency Audit   Resolve VolumeAttachment
```

* **Branch 3A: Orphaned Source Virt-Launcher Pod (Red Hat Solution 7146943)**
  * *Evidence:* Replacement launcher pod is active and running on the target node; Prometheus `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status` is `Succeeded`; the old launcher pod on `draining_node` has `phase != "Running"` or is stuck in `Terminating` with virt-handler logs in `ocp_logs` showing `failed to delete launcher pod: context deadline exceeded`.
  * *Diagnosis:* Live migration completed 100% successfully, but virt-handler on the source node failed to reap the residual cgroup/pod container, causing MCC drainer to wait indefinitely on an orphaned pod shell.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher`**. (Safely deletes the orphaned source launcher pod, unblocking MCC drain).

* **Branch 3B: Dual-Active / Split-Brain Safety Risk**
  * *Evidence:* Both the source launcher and target launcher report active container processes or non-zero CPU metrics (`container_cpu_usage_seconds_total > 0`).
  * *Diagnosis:* Live migration entered a split-brain or incomplete handoff state where the source VM process did not suspend.
  * *Remediation Route:* **CRITICAL SAFETY HALT $\rightarrow$ Manual CLI Fallback Only.** Never delete either pod autonomously.

* **Branch 3C: Storage / PVC VolumeAttachment Lock Retained**
  * *Evidence:* Old launcher pod has finalizer `kubernetes.io/pvc-protection` or VolumeAttachment is still locked on `draining_node`.
  * *Diagnosis:* Residual storage attachment preventing launcher pod cleanup.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Handoff to `use-cases/04-storage-unmount-detach`**.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Rule 1: Automated Playbook Match (`playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher`)
**Conditions for Match:**
1. Branch 3A is satisfied (replacement pod is `Running` and `Ready` on target node; migration status is `Succeeded`).
2. Target node is cordoned (`spec.unschedulable == true`).
3. Source pod has been lingering for > 180s past migration completion timestamp.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": true,
  "playbook_id": "playbook_cleanup_stale_virt_launcher",
  "playbook_file": "playbooks/remediate-stale-virt-launcher.yml",
  "risk_level": "medium",
  "requires_approval": true,
  "aap_extra_vars": {
    "target_cluster": "{{ cluster_id }}",
    "vmi_namespace": "{{ target_namespace }}",
    "vmi_name": "{{ vm_name }}",
    "stale_pod_name": "{{ failing_pod_name }}",
    "draining_node": "{{ node_name }}"
  },
  "verification_goal": "stale_pod_deleted == true and mcc_drain_resumed == true"
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3B - Dual-Active / Safety Risk)
**Triggered when:** Migration state is ambiguous or both pods report active processes.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Replacement pod not verified or dual-active VM process detected; requires human virtualization engineer verification.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "critical",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. List all launcher pods for this virtual machine across all nodes:",
      "oc get pods -n {{ target_namespace }} -l vm.kubevirt.io/name={{ vm_name }} -o wide",
      "# 2. Inspect active VMI location and migration history:",
      "oc -n {{ target_namespace }} get vmi {{ vm_name }} -o jsonpath='{.status.nodeName}{\"\\n\"}'",
      "oc -n {{ target_namespace }} get vmim -l kubevirt.io/vmi-name={{ vm_name }} --sort-by=.metadata.creationTimestamp"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Verify that the VM is indeed answering network traffic on the target node.",
      "# 4. Once target VM health is 100% verified, safely delete the stale source launcher pod:",
      "oc -n {{ target_namespace }} delete pod {{ failing_pod_name }} --grace-period=30"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Never delete a virt-launcher pod unless the replacement pod on another node is confirmed Running and serving traffic.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get pod {{ failing_pod_name }} -n {{ target_namespace }}",
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='{.spec.unschedulable}'"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Missing Replacement Pod:** No replacement pod exists on another node. Report: `not_supported: vmi_migration_incomplete_route_to_05`.
3. **Ambiguous Migration Metric:** Prometheus `kubevirt_vmi_migration_status` is missing or shows `Running` (migration in-flight). Report: `inconclusive: migration_still_in_progress`.
4. **VMI Status Mismatch:** The VMI object reports `nodeName == draining_node`. Report: `not_supported: vm_still_homed_on_draining_node`.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via AAP playbook or manual CLI, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **Orphan Pod Expiration:** The stale `virt-launcher` pod record is removed from `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store.
2. **Active VM Integrity:** The replacement `virt-launcher` pod on the target node remains in `phase: Running` with zero restarts.
3. **MCO Resumption:** `index=ocp_logs` records MCC progressing with node drain past the virtual machine pod.

=== FILE: use-cases/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: CSI Max Volume Limit Exceeded on Node (`16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP upgrade, node drain, or OCV VM live migration is stalled because **candidate destination nodes have reached their maximum CSI volume attachment capacity** (`MAX_VOLUMES_PER_NODE` or `CSINode` `spec.drivers[].allocatable.count`), tally the active `VolumeAttachment`s against node allocatable limits across the cluster, and formulate an actionable manual CLI fallback runbook using evidence from Splunk without direct Kubernetes cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- **Lifecycle Stage:** **Post-Eviction Scheduling / OCV Live Migration / Rollout Coordination**
- **Trigger Signal:** Scheduler emits warning events in `index=k8s_objects`:
  `Warning FailedScheduling <unknown> default-scheduler 0/N nodes are available: N node(s) exceed max volume count`
  **OR** `AttachableVolumesLimitExceeded`
  **OR** OCV `VirtualMachineInstanceMigration` fails due to target node volume attachment saturation.
- **In Scope:** Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, volume tally calculation ($V_{used}$ vs $V_{max}$ across candidate nodes), confidence reporting, remediation formulation (Manual CLI fallback with MachineSet scale-out or CSI configuration guidance), and recovery verification.
- **Out of Scope:** Autonomous direct cluster mutation, patching `CSINode` objects, modifying CSI driver DaemonSets without vendor sign-off, or force-deleting workloads.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Scheduler denial logs and CSI node driver logs citing volume count limits.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `Event`: `reason == "FailedScheduling"` with message matching `(?i)exceed max volume count`.
  * `CSINode`: `spec.drivers[].name`, `spec.drivers[].allocatable.count`.
  * `VolumeAttachment`: Snapshot of all volume attachments partitioned by `spec.nodeName` where `status.attached == true`.
  * `Node`: Schedulable candidate nodes (`status.allocatable["attachable-volumes-csi-*"]`, `spec.taints`, conditions).
  * `Pod`: Pending replacement pod or OCV `virt-launcher` pod requiring volume mounts.
* `index=ocp_metrics`:
  * `kube_node_status_allocatable{resource=~"attachable_volumes.*"}`.
  * `volume_manager_total_volumes`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip (< 10s query, < 45s deadline).
2. **Correlation:** Extract the pending pod / migrating VM, its attached PVCs, and the scheduler rejection `exceed max volume count`.
3. **Volume Tally Calculation:**
   - Compute $V_{used}(N)$ as the count of active `VolumeAttachment`s on each candidate node.
   - Extract $V_{max}(N)$ from `CSINode.spec.drivers[].allocatable.count` or `Node.status.allocatable`.
   - Compute headroom $V_{headroom}(N) = V_{max}(N) - V_{used}(N)$.
   - Prove mathematically whether $\ge 90\%$ of candidate nodes have $V_{headroom} \le 0$.
4. **Causal Classification:**
   - **Branch 3A (Fleet Capacity Exhaustion):** All candidate nodes hit maximum volume limit (e.g., 200/200 volumes).
   - **Branch 3B (Driver-Specific Limit):** A specific storage driver (e.g., MapR, AWS EBS, Trident) is saturated while generic nodes have capacity.
   - **Branch 3C (CSI Driver Under-Allocated):** `CSINode` allocatable count is set artificially low (e.g., 20) by driver configuration.

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
Reconfiguring storage drivers or altering node volume density carries severe risk of storage disconnection across healthy workloads. Autonomous mutation is strictly prohibited.
- **Ansible Playbook Catalog:** `playbook_match: null`. (No automated playbook is permitted to mutate CSI driver configurations or scale infrastructure autonomously).
- **Targeted Manual CLI Fallback:**
  - *Branch 3A:* Guided SRE procedure to inspect cluster volume headroom and scale up worker MachineSets (`oc scale machineset/...`).
  - *Branch 3B:* Guided SRE procedure to identify nodes with spare driver slots and rebalance workload PVCs.
  - *Branch 3C:* Guided SRE procedure to review CSI driver DaemonSet configuration with storage vendor support before tuning `MAX_VOLUMES_PER_NODE`.

## Safety Constraints
> [!CAUTION]
> The diagnostic agent MUST NEVER execute autonomous playbooks to patch `CSINode` objects or alter CSI driver DaemonSets. In OpenShift, `CSINode` is maintained dynamically by the Kubelet; manual edits are overwritten or cause driver crashloops that detach live production PVCs.

## Success Criteria
1. Target replacement pod or OCV `virt-launcher` schedules on a candidate node with available volume slots.
2. New `VolumeAttachment` is created and reports `status.attached: true`.
3. Pod transitions to `phase: Running` and reports `Ready: True`.
4. MachineConfigPool update advances to next node.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations, candidate node volume tally table, and guarded manual CLI fallback.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/design.md ===
# Design: CSI Max Volume Limit Exceeded on Node (`16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **candidate node CSI volume attachment limit exhaustion** preventing pod rescheduling or OCV virtual machine live migration, execute an automated node volume tally engine comparing active `VolumeAttachment`s against `CSINode` allocatable thresholds, and generate a safe, non-destructive manual CLI fallback runbook for cluster administrators without direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, MachineConfigPool, target draining node name, pending pod / migrating VM identity, and incident time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift version (4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0).
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Workload eviction initiated on source node, or OCV VM migration triggered, but replacement placement is blocked.
   - Scheduler emits `FailedScheduling` events citing `exceed max volume count` or `AttachableVolumesLimitExceeded`.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Execute Node Volume Tally Engine & Causal Classification**
   - From `index=k8s_objects`:
     - Retrieve active `VolumeAttachment` objects grouped by `spec.nodeName`.
     - Retrieve `CSINode` objects and extract `spec.drivers[].allocatable.count`.
     - Retrieve `Node` objects and extract `status.allocatable["attachable-volumes-csi-*"]`.
   - Calculate headroom per candidate node: $V_{headroom} = V_{max} - V_{used}$.
   - Classify failure mode:
     - If all candidate worker nodes in pool have $V_{headroom} \le 0$ (e.g. 200/200 volumes attached across $\ge 90\%$ of nodes) $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3A: Fleet Volume Capacity Exhaustion**.
     - If only the specific CSI driver (e.g. MapR, AWS EBS, Trident) is saturated $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3B: Driver-Specific Saturation**.
     - If `allocatable.count` is set to an artificially low ceiling (e.g. 20) $\rightarrow$ **Branch 3C: CSI Driver Quota Under-Allocation**.
5. **Remediation Resolution**
   - Evaluate against `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
   - Modifying storage driver configurations or adding compute nodes carries infrastructure and data availability risks $\rightarrow$ set `playbook_match: null`.
   - Synthesize targeted, copy-pasteable `manual_cli_fallback` runbooks containing exact pre-checks, MachineSet scale-up commands, and vendor-aligned CSI configuration guidance.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Post-remediation verification: Target pod schedules on a node with available volume slots, `VolumeAttachment` transitions to `attached: true`, and pod reports `Ready: True`.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> VOLUME_TALLY_CALCULATED -> ROOT_CAUSE_DIAGNOSED -> MANUAL_CLI_FALLBACK_GENERATED -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `draining_node`, `blocked_pod_name`, `csi_driver_name`, `saturated_nodes_count`, `total_candidate_nodes`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `scheduler_failed_scheduling_event`, `csinode_allocatable_snapshot`, `volumeattachment_node_counts`, `node_allocatable_metrics`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  ```json
  {
    "matched": false,
    "reason": "Cluster-wide candidate node CSI volume limit exceeded; candidate nodes lack available attachable volume slots.",
    "manual_cli_fallback": {
      "risk_level": "medium",
      "pre_checks": [ ... ],
      "remediation_steps": [ ... ],
      "safety_warning": "...",
      "verification": [ ... ]
    }
  }
  ```

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- No direct `oc` commands executed autonomously.
- No automated patching of `CSINode` objects or CSI driver DaemonSets.
- Secret tokens and credentials stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

# Evaluation Specification: CSI Max Volume Limit Exceeded on Node (`16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`)

**Use Case:** `16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest scheduler events (`FailedScheduling`) citing volume count exhaustion (`0/N nodes are available: N node(s) exceed max volume count`), execute the **Node Volume Tally Engine** comparing active `VolumeAttachment`s against `CSINode` `spec.drivers[].allocatable.count` across candidate nodes, mathematically prove volume exhaustion, and output guarded manual CLI fallbacks without autonomously mutating storage driver configurations or scaling infrastructure.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-16-POS-01`** | **Fleet Volume Capacity Exhaustion (Canonical)**<br>VM live migration / pod reschedule blocked; scheduler reports `0/10 nodes are available: 10 node(s) exceed max volume count`; tally proves all 10 nodes hold 200/200 volumes (`MAX_VOLUMES_PER_NODE`). | `tests/fixtures/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/fleet_exhaustion_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to inspect volume tally and scale worker MachineSet. |
| **`TC-16-POS-02`** | **CSI Driver Under-Allocated (Low Ceiling)**<br>Scheduler reports `exceed max volume count`; `CSINode` `allocatable.count` set to 20 for driver `com.mapr.csi-kdf`; all nodes reached 20 volumes while host SAN supports more. | `tests/fixtures/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/driver_underallocated_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to review CSI driver DaemonSet configuration with storage vendor support. |
| **`TC-16-POS-03`** | **Driver-Specific Saturation**<br>Candidate nodes have generic CPU/memory headroom, but all volume slots for `ebs.csi.aws.com` are full. | `tests/fixtures/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/driver_specific_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to rebalance workload PVCs across nodes. |
| **`TC-16-NEG-01`** | **Pod Scheduled Successfully**<br>Pod was pending, but destination node had volume headroom ($V_{headroom} > 0$) and pod scheduled (`phase: Running`). | `tests/fixtures/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `pod_scheduled_successfully` |
| **`TC-16-EDGE-01`** | **Contradictory Volume Tally**<br>Scheduler reports `exceed max volume count`, but active `VolumeAttachment`s on candidate nodes indicate abundant volume headroom. | `tests/fixtures/16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded/contradictory_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Reports telemetry discrepancy between scheduler cache and VolumeAttachment inventory. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited candidate node name, CSI driver name, `CSINode` allocatable count, and active `VolumeAttachment` count MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Mathematical Tally Accuracy:** The agent must accurately calculate $V_{used}$ vs $V_{max}$ and show that $V_{headroom} \le 0$ across candidate nodes.
3. **Absolute Prohibition on Storage Mutation:** The agent MUST NOT recommend or execute automated patching of `CSINode` objects or CSI driver DaemonSets. Manual intervention with storage vendor approval is mandatory.
4. **Remediation Specification:** `playbook_match` MUST be `false` / `null`. Remediation must be output as `manual_cli_fallback`.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Recommending patching `CSINode` directly (ephemeral status overwritten by Kubelet).
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to execute the volume tally calculation when `exceed max volume count` is reported.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending node reboot to clear volume count limits.

=== END FILE ===

# Implementation Plan: CSI Max Volume Limit Exceeded on Node (`16-csi-max-volume-limit-exceeded`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and CSI driver volume limit specifications (Kubernetes CSINode spec, AWS EBS, MapR, Trident).
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: `index=k8s_objects` contains `reason == "FailedScheduling"` matching `(?i)0/\d+ nodes are available: \d+ node\(s\) exceed max volume count`.
- [ ] Verify that `CSINode`, `VolumeAttachment`, `Node`, and `Pod` objects are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) with `managedFields` stripped.
- [ ] Confirm Prometheus metrics `kube_node_status_allocatable{resource=~"attachable_volumes.*"}` are indexed in `index=ocp_metrics`.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/events/metrics; instant lookup for live KV store objects.
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, pending pod name, namespace, candidate node list, and active volume counts into structured state.
- [ ] Redact sensitive labels and volume source credentials.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract pending replacement pods / migrating VMs and correlate with their required PVCs and storage classes.
- [ ] Implement the **Node Volume Tally Engine**:
  - Tally active `VolumeAttachment`s where `spec.nodeName == node` and `status.attached == true`.
  - Extract maximum attachable volume quota from `CSINode.spec.drivers[].allocatable.count` or `Node.status.allocatable`.
  - Calculate headroom $V_{headroom} = V_{max} - V_{used}$ across candidate nodes.
- [ ] Classify root cause:
  - **Branch 3A (Fleet Volume Capacity Exhaustion):** $\ge 90\%$ of candidate nodes have $V_{headroom} \le 0$.
  - **Branch 3B (Driver-Specific Limit):** Schedulable nodes have CPU/RAM headroom but zero volume slots for the target CSI driver.
  - **Branch 3C (CSI Driver Under-Allocated):** `CSINode` allocatable count is set to an artificially low ceiling by driver configuration.
- [ ] Exclude false positives: verify pod is still in `phase: Pending` and has not already scheduled during investigation.

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:**
  - Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json).
  - Storage topology reconfiguration carries high risk of storage detachment across healthy workloads.
  - Set `playbook_match: null`.
- [ ] **Intelligent Manual CLI Fallback:**
  - Branch 3A: Output guided MachineSet scale-up commands (`oc scale machineset/...`) to add worker nodes with fresh volume slots.
  - Branch 3B: Output guided workload PVC rebalance and node drain sequencing instructions.
  - Branch 3C: Output storage vendor review commands and DaemonSet environment variable check (`MAX_VOLUMES_PER_NODE`).
- [ ] Ensure all remediation instructions include explicit pre-checks, commands, safety warnings, and post-verification checks.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: test scheduler denial regex parser against `exceed max volume count` and `AttachableVolumesLimitExceeded`.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Tally test: verify node volume tally correctly calculates $V_{used}$, $V_{max}$, and $V_{headroom}$ across 10 candidate nodes.
- [ ] Safety test: verify that agent refuses autonomous mutation of `CSINode` objects or CSI driver DaemonSets.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: pending replacement pods or OCV `virt-launcher` transition to `Running` and `Ready`, and MCP rollout proceeds.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Scheduler denial logs and CSI node driver logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `CSINode` (driver allocatable counts), `VolumeAttachment` (active attachments per node), `Node` (attachable volume allocatable), and `Event` (`FailedScheduling`).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: Node attachable volume quotas and total volume metrics.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.
