# Evaluation Specification: Storage Unmount or Detach Stalls Drain (`04-storage-unmount-detach`)

**Use Case:** `04-storage-unmount-detach`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest MCC and Kubelet logs citing volume unmount/detach failures, correlate with `VolumeAttachment` objects reporting `status.attached: true` with `deletionTimestamp` set, discriminate between a hung CSI node-driver daemon pod and a cloud/SAN storage provider detachment failure, and output either `playbook_restart_csi_driver` or a manual CLI fallback.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-04-POS-01`** | **Hung CSI Node-Driver Pod (Canonical)**<br>Node unmount stalled; `VolumeAttachment` for Ceph/EBS stuck attached; CSI node-driver daemon pod on draining node is unresponsive. | `tests/fixtures/04-storage-unmount-detach/hung_csi_driver_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **AAP Playbook** (`remediation_type: ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK`) | `playbook_restart_csi_driver`<br>`extra_vars`: `{ draining_node, csi_driver_namespace, csi_driver_daemonset }` |
| **`TC-04-POS-02`** | **Cloud Provider SAN Detach Lock**<br>`VolumeAttachment` reports `status.attachmentMetadata` AWS detach error: `VolumeInUse` cloud API timeout. | `tests/fixtures/04-storage-unmount-detach/cloud_detach_error_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>CLI steps to inspect cloud volume detachment state. |
| **`TC-04-POS-03`** | **Host Process Locks Local Mount**<br>Kubelet reports `target is busy`; orphan container process holding open file descriptor on `/var/lib/kubelet/pods/...`. | `tests/fixtures/04-storage-unmount-detach/target_busy_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>Node debug steps with `fuser -vm` and safe process termination. |
| **`TC-04-NEG-01`** | **Volume Detached Cleanly**<br>Volume detachment finished within timeout; `VolumeAttachment` is deleted. | `tests/fixtures/04-storage-unmount-detach/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `volume_detached_successfully` |
| **`TC-04-EDGE-01`** | **VolumeAttachment Missing from Splunk**<br>Kubelet reports unmount error, but VolumeAttachment snapshot is absent from `k8s_objects`. | `tests/fixtures/04-storage-unmount-detach/missing_va_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Downgrades finding, reports missing storage object telemetry. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited VolumeAttachment name, PVC name, node name, and CSI driver daemon pod name MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Catalog Schema Conformity:** For `TC-04-POS-01`, `playbook_id` MUST be exactly `"playbook_restart_csi_driver"`. Generating any other playbook is a hard fail.
3. **Extra Vars Exactness:** `aap_extra_vars` must validate against Pydantic schema:
   ```json
   {
     "target_cluster": "ocp-prod-01",
     "draining_node": "worker-01.prod.rhocp",
     "csi_driver_namespace": "openshift-cluster-csi-drivers",
     "csi_driver_daemonset": "aws-ebs-csi-driver-node"
   }
   ```
4. **Safety Interlock:** The agent MUST NOT recommend force-deleting `VolumeAttachment` objects via `oc delete va <name> --force`, as doing so causes underlying SAN volume leaks and data corruption.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Suggesting force deletion of VolumeAttachment or PVC objects.
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to match `playbook_restart_csi_driver` when CSI driver daemon pod is clearly identified as hung.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending node power cycle before attempting CSI driver restart.

# Implementation Plan: Storage Unmount or Detach Stalls Pod Termination

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and storage CSI driver versions (e.g. ODF/Ceph RBD, AWS EBS, Azure Disk, VMware vSphere).
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: Kubelet or CSI logs match `(?i)(failed to unmount|unmountdevice failed|failed to detach|volume.*still attached|volumeattachment.*in use|device or resource busy)`.
- [ ] Confirm `VolumeAttachment` objects are collected without `managedFields` and cached in Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`).

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Extract VolumeAttachment status (`attached`, `detachError`) and Kubelet unmount logs.
- [ ] Redact credentials, tokens, and storage backend authentication details.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract failing pod, namespace, and volume name from Kubelet logs.
- [ ] Look up VolumeAttachment for the node and volume in `k8s_objects` / KV Store.
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Branch A: Is the local CSI node daemonset pod hung (RPC `DeadlineExceeded`)?
  - Branch B: Is it a storage array / cloud multi-attach conflict (`Multi-Attach error`)?
  - Branch C: Is a host process holding open file descriptors on the mount (`device or resource busy`)?

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - If Branch A (local CSI node daemon hang):
    - Match `playbook_restart_csi_driver`.
    - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, draining_node, csi_driver_namespace, csi_driver_daemonset }`.
    - Mark risk as `low` and require human approval before execution.
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:** If Branch B (multi-attach lock) or Branch C (device busy):
  - Explicitly set `playbook_match: null`.
  - Output targeted manual `oc` CLI runbook:
    ```bash
    # 1. Check VolumeAttachment status:
    oc get volumeattachment | grep <volume_name>
    # 2. Inspect CSI controller logs:
    oc -n <csi_ns> logs deployment/<csi_controller> -c csi-driver --tail=50
    # 3. Check open file descriptors on host:
    oc adm node-logs <node> --path=journal | grep -Ei 'umount.*busy|<volume_name>'
    ```
- [ ] Enforce safety: NEVER delete `VolumeAttachment` directly while a process is writing to the disk.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching unmount error, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and conflicting evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Playbook matching test: local CSI daemon hang produces exact `aap_extra_vars`.
- [ ] Fallback test: multi-attach conflict correctly routes to manual CLI runbook with data corruption warnings.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: `VolumeAttachment.status.attached == false`, pod terminates, and MCO drain advances.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Kubelet unmount logs and CSI driver container logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `Pod` (`spec.volumes`, `metadata.deletionTimestamp`), `VolumeAttachment` (`spec.nodeName`, `status.attached`, `status.detachError`), `Node` (cordon status).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: Storage I/O metrics and node filesystem utilization.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Storage Unmount or Detach Stalls (`04-storage-unmount-detach`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | Node status is `SchedulingDisabled` (cordoned), and pod termination is actively stalled. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_drain` |
| **Trigger Signal** | Kubelet or CSI logs in `ocp_logs` match: `(?i)(failed to unmount|unmountdevice failed|failed to detach|volume.*still attached|volumeattachment.*in use|device or resource busy)` | Exit: `not_supported: no_storage_unmount_error_logged` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract Storage Target]           [No Storage Error Found]
            Namespace, Pod Name, Volume Name           ──► Exit: not_supported
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: VolumeAttachment Lookup]
            Look up VolumeAttachment for Node + PV in KV Store
                         │
         ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
         ▼                               ▼
   [attached == false]            [attached == true OR detachError != null]
   ──► Unmount completed!         ──► Detach Stalled!
       Storage is cleared.            Isolate Root Cause (Step 3)
```

### Step 1: Storage Target & Pod Extraction
1. Extract `target_namespace`, `failing_pod_name`, and `volume_name` from the Kubelet or MCC log error:
   ```regex
   (?i)UnmountDevice failed for volume "(?<volume_name>[^"]+)".*pod "(?<target_namespace>[^/]+)/(?<failing_pod_name>[^"]+)"
   ```
2. Verify in `k8s_objects` / KV Store that `target_resource == failing_pod_name`:
   * Confirm Pod `metadata.deletionTimestamp` is set (pod is actively terminating).
   * Confirm Pod volume reference maps to the target PVC / PV.

### Step 2: VolumeAttachment & Node Status Check
1. Scan `k8s_objects` / KV Store for `resource_kind == "VolumeAttachment"` where `node_name == draining_node` and `volume_name == target_pv`:
   * **Case 2A: `status.attached == false` AND no detachError:** The volume has detached successfully. The stall is caused by application shutdown or finalizers, NOT storage. Exit to `use-cases/03-pod-termination-finalizer`.
   * **Case 2B: `status.attached == true` OR `status.detachError != null`:** Confirmed storage unmount/detach stall. Proceed to Step 3.

---

### Step 3: Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                                [Storage Detach Stalled]
                                           │
             ┌─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┐
             ▼                             ▼                             ▼
   [Branch 3A: Hung CSI Daemon]  [Branch 3B: Multi-Attach Lock] [Branch 3C: Target Busy]
   RPC timeout / CSI plugin hang  Cloud/Array lock conflict     Host process holds mount open
             │                             │                             │
             ▼                             ▼                             ▼
   Playbook: restart_csi_driver  Playbook: null (Backend Lock)  Playbook: null (Host Busy)
   AAP Extra Vars: CSI daemon    CLI Fallback: Storage Admin    CLI Fallback: fuser / lsof
```

* **Branch 3A: Hung CSI Node-Driver DaemonSet Pod on the Draining Node**
  * *Evidence:* `VolumeAttachment.status.detachError` contains `rpc error: code = DeadlineExceeded` OR CSI node pod logs in `ocp_logs` show connection timeouts while Kubelet logs show `UnmountDevice failed`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The local CSI node daemonset pod on the draining node is deadlocked or hung, failing to unmount the filesystem from the host.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_restart_csi_driver`**. (Restarting the daemonset pod on that specific node clears the hung RPC handler).

* **Branch 3B: Storage Backend Lock / Multi-Attach Conflict**
  * *Evidence:* `VolumeAttachment.status.detachError` contains `Multi-Attach error for volume` OR `Volume is already exclusively attached to one node`.
  * *Diagnosis:* Cloud/SAN storage control plane still holds an exclusive volume lease, or another node attempted to attach the volume before this node completed unmounting.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (Do NOT restart local CSI pod)**. Requires storage control plane coordination.

* **Branch 3C: Host Process Holding Mount Point Open (`device or resource busy`)**
  * *Evidence:* Kubelet journal logs show: `umount: ...: target is busy` OR `device or resource busy`.
  * *Diagnosis:* A container process, zombie process, or lingering sub-mount namespace on the host is still accessing the volume directory.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (Host Process Inspection)**.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Rule 1: Automated Playbook Match (`playbook_restart_csi_driver`)
**Conditions for Match:**
1. Branch 3A is satisfied (RPC timeout / CSI node daemon hang).
2. CSI driver pod on the draining node is identified (e.g. `csi-rbdplugin`, `aws-ebs-csi-driver-node`, `vsphere-csi-node`).
3. Target node is already cordoned (`SchedulingDisabled`).

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": true,
  "playbook_id": "playbook_restart_csi_driver",
  "playbook_file": "playbooks/remediate-restart-csi-driver.yml",
  "risk_level": "low",
  "requires_approval": true,
  "aap_extra_vars": {
    "target_cluster": "{{ cluster_id }}",
    "draining_node": "{{ node_name }}",
    "csi_driver_namespace": "{{ csi_namespace }}",
    "csi_driver_daemonset": "{{ csi_daemonset_name }}"
  },
  "verification_goal": "VolumeAttachment status.attached becomes false within 120s"
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation
**Triggered when:** Branch 3B (multi-attach lock), Branch 3C (target busy), or non-standard third-party storage driver.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Storage backend lock or busy mount detected; requires human verification to prevent data corruption.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "high",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Check VolumeAttachment status and error message:",
      "oc get volumeattachment -o wide | grep {{ volume_name }}",
      "# 2. Inspect CSI driver controller logs:",
      "oc -n {{ csi_namespace }} logs deployment/{{ csi_controller }} -c csi-driver --tail=50"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. If host mount is busy, inspect open file descriptors on the draining node:",
      "oc adm node-logs {{ node_name }} --path=journal | grep -Ei 'umount.*busy|{{ volume_name }}'",
      "# 4. WARNING: Never force-delete VolumeAttachment without confirming disk is unmounted from host!"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Force-detaching a volume while a host process is actively writing will cause silent filesystem corruption.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get volumeattachment | grep {{ volume_name }}",
      "oc get pod {{ failing_pod_name }} -n {{ target_namespace }}"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Tool Failure:** The Splunk multi-index query times out or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Missing VolumeAttachment:** Kubelet reports detach error, but no matching `VolumeAttachment` exists in `k8s_objects` or KV Store. Report: `probable: volume_attachment_record_missing`.
3. **Active Workload Risk:** Workload pod on draining node is still in `phase: Running` (not `Terminating`). **HALT IMMEDIATELY.** Never recommend detaching a volume for a running pod. Report: `not_supported: pod_not_in_terminating_state`.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via AAP or manual CLI, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **VolumeAttachment Detached:** `status.attached == false` OR the `VolumeAttachment` object is deleted from the cluster.
2. **Pod Termination:** The blocker pod finishes termination and is removed from `spec.nodeName == draining_node`.
3. **MCO Drain Resumes:** `index=ocp_logs` records MCC progressing with node drain.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/12-mcd-config-render-apply/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: Rendered MachineConfig or Configuration Apply Failure

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP/upgrade stall is caused by **rendered MachineConfig or configuration application failure**, determine whether the root cause is a controller-level MachineConfig merge collision (e.g. duplicate conflicting file paths), an invalid Ignition schema, or a host-level file-write/systemd-reload failure in MCD, and formulate an actionable remediation specification (manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- Lifecycle stage: **Desired Config Render / MCD Apply**
- Trigger signal: MCC/MCD logs match `(?i)(failed to render.*machineconfig|conflicting file.*in machineconfigs|error merging machineconfigs|failed to apply update|error writing file.*ignition|failed to reload systemd)` OR MCP condition `RenderDegraded` is `True` OR Node annotation `state` is `Degraded` with reason `FailedToApply`.
- In scope: Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, distinguishing render-time from apply-time failures, identification of conflicting source MachineConfigs, confidence reporting, safe manual CLI fallback runbook generation, and recovery verification.
- Out of scope: Directly patching or deleting generated `rendered-*` MachineConfigs, editing source MachineConfigs autonomously, or rebooting nodes without human sign-off.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller render logs, Machine Config Daemon apply logs, systemd journal logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `MachineConfigPool` (`status.conditions` where `type == "RenderDegraded"` or `"Degraded"`, `spec.configuration.name`, `status.configuration.name`).
  * `Node` (`metadata.annotations['machineconfiguration.openshift.io/state']`, `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/reason`, `currentConfig`, `desiredConfig`).
  * `Event` (render failure and node apply events).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `machine_config_pool_status_degraded`, `machine_config_daemon_state`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip.
2. **Correlation:** Check MCP conditions (`RenderDegraded`, `Degraded`) and Node annotations (`state`, `reason`).
3. **Causal Depth:** Determine the exact failure point:
   - Did the render controller fail to combine source MachineConfigs due to duplicate file path collisions? (Branch 3A).
   - Did MCD fail to write files or reload systemd units on the host due to local disk exhaustion or permission errors? (Branch 3B).
   - Did a MachineConfig specify an unsupported Ignition version or malformed syntax? (Branch 3C).

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
1. **Playbook Matching (AAP / AWX):**
   - **Automated remediation is STRICTLY PROHIBITED** (`matched: false`). MachineConfigs define low-level operating system and kernel parameters. Modifying them autonomously carries severe risk of rendering nodes unbootable.
2. **Targeted CLI Fallback (Human SRE Runbook):**
   - For render conflicts: locate the two colliding source MachineConfigs via `jq` path inspection, with instructions to reconcile the conflicting files in version-controlled GitOps repositories.
   - For host apply failures: generate host disk inspection, journal cleanup, and systemd status inspection commands.

## Safety Constraints
* NEVER edit or delete generated `rendered-<pool>-<hash>` objects directly. Edits must be made to *source* `MachineConfig` objects.
* NEVER touch host files directly from the agent.
* The agent has zero direct mutation tools.

## Success Criteria
`mcp.status.conditions[?(@.type=="RenderDegraded")].status == "False"`, a valid desired rendered MachineConfig is generated, Node annotation `state` returns to `Done`, and `currentConfig == desiredConfig`.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations and either an `aap_launch_spec` or `manual_cli_fallback`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/12-mcd-config-render-apply/design.md ===
# Design: Rendered MachineConfig or Configuration Apply Failure

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **rendered machineconfig or configuration application failure**, isolate whether the breakdown is a render-time merge collision between custom MachineConfigs or a node-level file-write/systemd-reload failure in MCD, and generate a deterministic manual CLI fallback specification without direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, MachineConfigPool, target node, and time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift release and release-matched runbook.
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Trigger signal: MCP reports `RenderDegraded` or Node reports apply error in MCD.
   - Confirm active stage is `config-render` or `config-apply`.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Causal Correlation & Classification**
   - Inspect MCP `status.conditions` (`RenderDegraded`, `Degraded`).
   - Retrieve Node annotations `state` and `reason` from KV Store.
   - Traverse causal sub-tree:
     - Branch 3A: `RenderDegraded == True`? MachineConfig duplicate file path collision.
     - Branch 3B: `state == "Degraded"` on node during apply? Host disk full, permission denied, or systemd reload error.
     - Branch 3C: MCC logs report schema parse error? Invalid Ignition version or malformed YAML.
5. **Remediation Matching**
   - Automated remediation strictly prohibited (`matched: false`).
   - Generate targeted manual `oc` CLI fallback with GitOps reconciliation guidance or host disk cleanup steps.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Monitor for recovery post-execution: RenderDegraded clears, node applies config, state returns to `Done`.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> ROOT_CAUSE_DIAGNOSED -> REMEDIATION_MATCHED (Manual CLI Fallback) -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `pool_name`, `node_name`, `is_render_degraded`, `conflicting_path`, `failed_service`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `mcc_render_error_log`, `mcd_apply_error_log`, `node_degraded_annotation`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  * `{ manual_cli_commands: [ ... ], safety_warnings: "...", rollback_steps: "..." }`.

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- NEVER edit generated `rendered-<pool>-<hash>` MachineConfigs directly.
- Secret and credential values are stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/12-mcd-config-render-apply/eval.md ===
# Evaluation Specification: MCD Config Render or Apply Failure (`12-mcd-config-render-apply`)

**Use Case:** `12-mcd-config-render-apply`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest MCC controller render errors and MCD host apply logs, discriminate between a source `MachineConfig` merge collision (duplicate conflicting file definitions across custom CRDs) and an on-host write failure during application, and output safe GitOps resolution runbooks without autonomously mutating generated rendered configs.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-12-POS-01`** | **Source MachineConfig Merge Conflict (Canonical)**<br>MCC log cites `failed to render MachineConfigPool worker: conflicting file /etc/chrony.conf defined in 99-worker-time and 99-worker-ntp`. | `tests/fixtures/12-mcd-config-render-apply/merge_conflict_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>GitOps source MachineConfig conflict resolution runbook. |
| **`TC-12-POS-02`** | **Host Disk Full During Apply**<br>MCD logs cite `failed to apply update: write /etc/containers/registries.conf: no space left on device` on `worker-01`. | `tests/fixtures/12-mcd-config-render-apply/disk_full_apply_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Target: `15-disk-pressure-host-resource` | **Routing Handoff** | Re-routes diagnosis to Use Case 15 (`15-disk-pressure-host-resource`). |
| **`TC-12-POS-03`** | **SELinux / Immutable File Clashing**<br>MCD log cites `operation not permitted: /etc/sysctl.d/99-custom.conf chattr +i active`. | `tests/fixtures/12-mcd-config-render-apply/immutable_file_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>Node debug steps to clear immutable file attributes. |
| **`TC-12-NEG-01`** | **Config Render Succeeded**<br>MCO controller rendered new MachineConfig cleanly; pool update advancing. | `tests/fixtures/12-mcd-config-render-apply/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `config_render_successful` |
| **`TC-12-EDGE-01`** | **Missing MachineConfig CRD Definition**<br>MCD log cites missing parent ignition object from API server. | `tests/fixtures/12-mcd-config-render-apply/missing_mc_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Reports missing object definition gap. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited conflicting `MachineConfig` name, file path (`/etc/...`), pool name, and controller error excerpt MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Absolute Prohibition on Mutating `rendered-*` Objects:** The agent MUST NEVER recommend editing, patching, or deleting generated `rendered-<pool>-<hash>` objects. All remediations must target the *source* `MachineConfig` CRDs via GitOps.
3. **Remediation Specification:** `playbook_match` MUST be `false` / `null`. Remediation must be output as `manual_cli_fallback`.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Recommending `oc edit mc rendered-worker-...`. (MCO will instantly overwrite it or break generation hash).
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to link conflicting source MachineConfig CRD names when cited in MCC logs.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending node reboot to fix an ignition merge conflict.

=== END FILE ===

# Implementation Plan: Rendered MachineConfig or Configuration Apply Failure

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and obtain matching MCO source/runbook revision.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: MCP `RenderDegraded: True` or MCD logs report `failed to apply update`.
- [ ] Confirm `index=ocp_logs`, `index=k8s_objects` (with `managedFields` stripped), and `index=ocp_metrics` (`machine_config_pool_status_degraded`) are populated.
- [ ] Verify that `MachineConfigPool` and `Node` objects are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) for instant retrieval.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, pool identity, and conflicting file paths into structured state.
- [ ] Redact secrets, authorization headers, and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract target pool or node from MCC/MCD logs.
- [ ] Distinguish render-time errors (MCC) from apply-time errors (MCD).
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Are multiple custom MachineConfigs colliding on a single file path?
  - Has MCD failed writing files on host due to disk space exhaustion or permission errors?
  - Is there an incompatible Ignition specification version?

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - Autonomous mutation of MachineConfigs is **strictly prohibited** (`matched: false`).
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:**
  - For render conflicts: output runbook to locate colliding MachineConfigs and reconcile in GitOps.
  - For apply failures: output host disk inspection and systemd unit troubleshooting commands.
- [ ] Keep all mutation tools disabled inside the agent; hand off execution to human SRE and platform administrators.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching trigger, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and contradictory evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Safety test: verify agent strictly refuses direct patching of `rendered-*` MachineConfigs.
- [ ] Fallback test: conflicting MachineConfigs correctly outputs `jq` extraction runbook.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: `RenderDegraded` clears, node state becomes `Done`, and `currentConfig == desiredConfig`.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Machine Config Controller render logs, Machine Config Daemon apply logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `MachineConfigPool` (conditions, config hashes), `Node` (annotations, state).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `machine_config_pool_status_degraded`, `machine_config_daemon_state`.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Rendered MachineConfig or Configuration Apply Failure (`12-mcd-config-render-apply`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | MachineConfigPool is in config generation or Node is in post-drain apply phase (`state: Working` or `state: Degraded`). | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_apply` |
| **Trigger Signal** | MCC or MCD logs match: `(?i)(failed to render.*machineconfig|conflicting file.*in machineconfigs|error merging machineconfigs|failed to apply update|error writing file.*ignition|failed to reload systemd)` OR MCP condition `RenderDegraded` is `True` OR Node annotation `state` is `Degraded` with reason `FailedToApply`. | Exit: `not_supported: no_render_or_apply_error_logged` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Ingest MCP & Node Apply State]       [No Render/Apply Error]
            Check RenderDegraded condition & MCD logs     ──► Exit: not_supported
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: Differentiate Render vs Apply Failure]
            Check failure location: Controller vs Node
                         │
         ┌───────────────┴───────────────────────────────┐
         ▼                                               ▼
  [Render-Time Conflict]                         [Node Apply Failure]
  MCP condition RenderDegraded: True             Rendered config exists; MCD
  MCC logs: conflicting file in MCs              fails writing file/systemd on host
         │                                               │
         ▼                                               ▼
  Branch 3A: MachineConfig                       Branch 3B: Host Disk / Permission /
  Merge Collision                                SELinux Apply Failure
```

### Step 1: Ingest MCP Condition & Node State
1. In `k8s_objects` / KV Store, inspect the target `MachineConfigPool`:
   * Check `status.conditions[?(@.type=="RenderDegraded")].status` and `message`.
   * Check `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Degraded")].status` and `reason`.
   * Record `spec.configuration.name` (the target rendered config).
2. If target node is specified, inspect target `Node` object:
   * Record annotation `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/state`.
   * Record annotation `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/reason`.

### Step 2: Differentiate Controller Render vs. Host Node Apply
1. **Case 2A: Render-Time Failure in MCC (Controller Level):**
   * If MCP condition `RenderDegraded == True` OR MCC logs in `index=ocp_logs` show:
     ```regex
     (?i)failed to render.*machineconfig.*conflicting file "(?<conflicting_filepath>[^"]+)" in "(?<mc_a>[^"]+)" and "(?<mc_b>[^"]+)"
     ```
   * Proceed to **Branch 3A**.
2. **Case 2B: Configuration Apply Failure in MCD (Host Node Level):**
   * If target rendered MachineConfig exists, but MCD pod on the node logs:
     ```regex
     (?i)failed to apply update: error writing file "(?<failed_filepath>[^"]+)": (?<error_msg>.*)
     ```
     OR
     ```regex
     (?i)failed to reload systemd units: (?<error_msg>.*)
     ```
   * Proceed to **Branch 3B**.
3. **Case 2C: Ignition Version / Unsupported Feature Syntax:**
   * If MCC logs report `unsupported ignition version` or `invalid schema`: Proceed to **Branch 3C**.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                            [MachineConfig Failure]
                                       │
            ┌──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┐
            ▼                          ▼                          ▼
  [Branch 3A: Render Conflict] [Branch 3B: Node Apply Error] [Branch 3C: Invalid Ignition]
  Two MachineConfigs define    Disk full, read-only host,     Unsupported spec version or
  same file path differently   or SELinux write denial        malformed storage syntax
            │                          │                          │
            ▼                          ▼                          ▼
  Playbook: null (Source Git)  Playbook: null (Host Risk)     Playbook: null (Syntax Error)
  CLI Fallback: Resolve MC     CLI Fallback: Fix Local Disk   CLI Fallback: Fix MachineConfig
```

* **Branch 3A: Render-Time MachineConfig Merge Conflict (Duplicate Path Collision)**
  * *Evidence:* MCP condition `RenderDegraded: True`; MCC logs report: `error merging machineconfigs: conflicting file /etc/... in 99-worker-custom-a and 99-worker-custom-b`.
  * *Diagnosis:* Two separate custom `MachineConfig` objects targeting the same pool specify identical file paths with conflicting contents or file modes. The render controller cannot resolve the priority and halts rendering.
  * *Remediation Route:* **DO NOT edit the generated `rendered-*` object directly.** Route to **Manual CLI Fallback**:
    - Identify the two colliding source MachineConfigs.
    - Provide exact instructions to merge the configurations or adjust file ownership in GitOps / source repository.

* **Branch 3B: Node-Level Configuration Apply Failure (Host Filesystem / Systemd)**
  * *Evidence:* Target rendered MachineConfig exists, but MCD on the node marks `state: Degraded` with error: `error writing file /etc/...: no space left on device` or `permission denied` or `failed to reload systemd units: Process exited with status 1`.
  * *Diagnosis:* The rendered config is valid, but the target host cannot apply it due to local disk exhaustion on `/` or `/var`, an immutable filesystem attribute, or a broken systemd service unit definition.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (Host Diagnostics)**. Inspect host storage capacity and systemd journal logs.

* **Branch 3C: Unsupported Ignition Spec / Malformed MachineConfig Syntax**
  * *Evidence:* MCC logs report `cannot unmarshal Ignition specification` or `unsupported Ignition version 3.4.0 (max supported: 3.2.0)`.
  * *Diagnosis:* A newly applied MachineConfig was generated using an incompatible Ignition API version or contains malformed JSON/YAML payloads.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Manual CLI Fallback (MachineConfig Schema Correction)**.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Absolute Safety Rule on Rendered MachineConfigs
> [!CAUTION]
> The diagnostic agent MUST NEVER attempt to directly edit, patch, or delete generated `rendered-<pool>-<hash>` objects (`oc edit mc rendered-worker-...`). Rendered MachineConfigs are strictly generated artifacts managed by MCC; modifying them directly causes state divergence, etcd thrashing, and immediate overwrite by the render controller. Edits MUST be applied to the underlying *source* `MachineConfig` objects.

### Rule 1: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3A - Render Conflict)
**Triggered when:** Multiple custom MachineConfigs conflict on a shared file path.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "MachineConfig render conflict detected; multiple source configs define the same file path.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "medium",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Inspect MCP RenderDegraded condition message:",
      "oc get mcp {{ pool_name }} -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions[?(@.type==\"RenderDegraded\")].message}{\"\\n\"}'",
      "# 2. Locate the two colliding source MachineConfigs:",
      "oc get mc -l machineconfiguration.openshift.io/role={{ pool_name }} -o json | jq -r '.items[] | select(.spec.config.storage.files[]?.path==\"{{ conflicting_filepath }}\") | .metadata.name'"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Review contents of the conflicting source configs:",
      "oc get mc {{ mc_a }} -o yaml",
      "oc get mc {{ mc_b }} -o yaml",
      "# 4. Consolidate file definitions into a single authoritative MachineConfig in your GitOps repository.",
      "# 5. Delete or update the redundant MachineConfig to clear the conflict:",
      "oc delete mc {{ mc_b }}"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Always make MachineConfig modifications in version-controlled GitOps repositories before deleting live cluster objects.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get mcp {{ pool_name }} -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions[?(@.type==\"RenderDegraded\")].status}{\"\\n\"}{.status.configuration.name}{\"\\n\"}'"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3B - Node Apply / Disk Failure)
**Triggered when:** MCD fails to apply valid rendered config on the node.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "MCD failed to write configuration on host; local disk exhaustion or systemd failure suspected.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "high",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Inspect MCD pod logs on the failing node:",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-config-operator logs -l k8s-app=machine-config-daemon --field-selector spec.nodeName={{ node_name }} -c machine-config-daemon --tail=50",
      "# 2. Check disk space and mount states on the host:",
      "oc debug node/{{ node_name }} -- chroot /host df -h / /var /etc"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. If disk space is exhausted, clean up old container images or journals:",
      "oc debug node/{{ node_name }} -- chroot /host journalctl --vacuum-size=500M",
      "# 4. If systemd unit reload failed, inspect journalctl for the failing service:",
      "oc debug node/{{ node_name }} -- chroot /host systemctl status {{ failed_service }}",
      "# 5. Once host issue is cleared, trigger MCD retry by restarting MCD pod:",
      "oc -n openshift-machine-config-operator delete pod -l k8s-app=machine-config-daemon --field-selector spec.nodeName={{ node_name }}"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Do not delete active system files to free space; only vacuum rotated journals or remove dangling container storage.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='{.metadata.annotations.machineconfiguration\\.openshift\\.io/state}'"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Transient Generation Delay:** New MachineConfig was created 10 seconds ago, and MCC is actively rendering. Report: `inconclusive: render_in_progress`.
3. **Missing MachineConfig Definition:** MCD reports apply error referencing a rendered config that does not exist in cluster inventory. Report: `inconclusive: rendered_config_object_missing`.
4. **Node Not Drained Yet:** MCD apply error logged, but node is still actively evicting pods (`spec.unschedulable: false`). Report: `not_supported: drain_not_completed_route_to_workload_skill`.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via GitOps merge or host space recovery, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **RenderDegraded Cleared:** `mcp.status.conditions[?(@.type=="RenderDegraded")].status == "False"`.
2. **Target Rendered Config Bound:** `mcp.status.configuration.name == mcp.spec.configuration.name`.
3. **Node Apply Success:** Node annotation `machineconfiguration.openshift.io/state` transitions from `Working` to `Done`, and `currentConfig` equals `desiredConfig`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/SPEC.md ===
# SPEC: Node Becomes NotReady or Kubelet/CRI-O Unreachable

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Problem Statement
The diagnostic agent must identify whether an OpenShift MCP/upgrade stall is caused by **node becomes notready or kubelet/cri-o cannot finish drain**, determine whether the failure is a crashed container runtime service (CRI-O), expired Kubelet TLS certificates, or physical host hardware/power loss, and formulate an actionable remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without direct cluster mutation.

## Trigger and Scope
- Lifecycle stage: **Node Control Plane / Host Runtime / Drain**
- Trigger signal: Node condition `Ready` is `False` or `Unknown`, `kube-node-lease` is expired, or MCC reports `error draining node ...: node is not ready`.
- In scope: Read-only evidence gathering from unified Splunk multi-index search/KV store, causal classification, confidence reporting, remediation plan matching (`PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`), and recovery verification.
- Out of scope: Force-deleting pods on unverified hosts, force-deleting nodes, or initiating host power operations without out-of-band validation.

## Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Host systemd journal logs (crio.service, kubelet.service), MCC drain logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store:
  * `Node` (`status.conditions` where `type == "Ready"`, `spec.unschedulable`, `metadata.annotations`).
  * `Lease` in `kube-node-lease` (`spec.renewTime`, `spec.holderIdentity`).
  * `Event` (`reason == "NodeNotReady"`, `FailedKillPod`, `NodeHasDiskPressure`).
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `up{job="kubelet"}`, `kube_node_status_condition{condition="Ready"}`.

## Diagnostic Strategy
1. **Perception:** Ingest unified Splunk dossier in a single round-trip.
2. **Correlation:** Match MCC error node to `Node` condition and verify `kube-node-lease` freshness.
3. **Causal Depth:** Determine *why* the node is unreachable:
   - Is `crio.service` in a failed state while host kernel and network are alive? (Branch 3A).
   - Is Kubelet client TLS certificate expired or CSR pending approval? (Branch 3B).
   - Is the host completely silent (power loss, kernel panic, or hypervisor crash)? (Branch 3C).

## Two-Tier Remediation Contract
1. **Playbook Matching (AAP / AWX):**
   - If CRI-O service crashed on an accessible, cordoned host:
     - Match `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit` from `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
     - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, target_node, systemd_service_name: "crio" }`.
     - Risk level: `medium`. Requires human approval.
2. **Targeted CLI Fallback (Human SRE Runbook):**
   - If Kubelet TLS cert expired: generate CSR verification and approval commands (`oc adm certificate approve`).
   - If host hardware/power lost: generate out-of-band IPMI / hypervisor console inspection runbook.

## Safety Constraints
* NEVER force-delete pods (`--force --grace-period=0`) on a NotReady node without physical power-off / fencing verification. Partitioned hosts will corrupt shared SAN/CSI storage.
* NEVER reboot or restart services on an uncordoned node.
* The agent has zero direct mutation tools.

## Success Criteria
Node condition `Ready` returns to `True`, node lease is freshly renewed within 40 seconds, and MCC resumes drain or MCD continues configuration rollout.

---

## Agent Graph Integration Contract

This use case is invoked by the shared 10-node LangGraph workflow in `../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md`.

### Bounded Execution
* Investigation Deadline: 45 seconds.
* Tool Budget: Max 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
* Output: Structured report with evidence citations and either an `aap_launch_spec` or `manual_cli_fallback`.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/design.md ===
# Design: Node Becomes NotReady or Kubelet/CRI-O Unreachable

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Design Objective
Diagnose **node becomes notready or kubelet/cri-o cannot finish drain**, isolate whether the failure is a transient container runtime unit crash, a Kubelet certificate expiration, or complete host hardware isolation, and generate a deterministic remediation specification (Ansible Playbook spec or manual CLI fallback) without relying on direct cluster mutation.

---

## Evidence-First Flow

1. **Establish Identity & Context**
   - Cluster ID, MachineConfigPool, target node, and time window.
   - Pinned OpenShift release and release-matched runbook.
2. **Confirm Lifecycle Stage**
   - Trigger signal: Node reports `NotReady` or `Unknown`, or MCC reports `error draining node: node is not ready`.
   - Confirm active stage is `drain` or `updating`.
3. **Execute Federated Evidence Queries**
   - Query `query_splunk_dossier` for `index=ocp_logs` and `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store; query metrics separately through Prometheus MCP.
4. **Causal Correlation & Classification**
   - Inspect Node `status.conditions` where `type == "Ready"`.
   - Inspect Prometheus `up{job="kubelet"}` and node lease renewTime.
   - Traverse causal sub-tree:
     - Branch 3A: `crio.service` failed in journal logs? Runtime daemon crash on live host.
     - Branch 3B: Kubelet logs report x509 cert expired? Pending CSR block.
     - Branch 3C: No logs across all subsystems for > 10m? Host hardware / power drop.
5. **Remediation Matching**
   - Evaluate against `remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`.
   - If Branch 3A $\rightarrow$ generate `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit` spec with `systemd_service_name: "crio"`.
   - If Branch 3B or 3C $\rightarrow$ generate targeted manual `oc` CLI fallback with strict hardware fencing warnings.
6. **Persist & Verify**
   - Record investigation state and evidence IDs in PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
   - Monitor for recovery post-execution: Node returns `Ready`, lease updates, MCC drain resumes.

---

## State Model
`TRIGGERED -> CONTEXT_CAPTURED -> EVIDENCE_COLLECTED -> ROOT_CAUSE_DIAGNOSED -> REMEDIATION_MATCHED (AAP Playbook OR Manual CLI) -> PERSISTED`

---

## Data Contract
* **Investigation Record:** `investigation_id`, `cluster_id`, `node_name`, `node_condition_ready`, `lease_age_seconds`, `failed_service`.
* **Evidence Citations:** `node_condition_excerpt`, `systemd_journal_error`, `kubelet_metric_value`.
* **Remediation Specification:**
  * If automated: `{ playbook_id: "playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit", aap_extra_vars: { ... } }`.
  * If manual: `{ manual_cli_commands: [ ... ], safety_warnings: "...", rollback_steps: "..." }`.

---

## Guardrails
- Strictly read-only execution in the diagnostic agent.
- No force deletion of pods without physical power fencing confirmation.
- No direct service restarts on uncordoned nodes.
- Secret and credential values are stripped before persistence.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: use-cases/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/eval.md ===
# Evaluation Specification: Node NotReady / Kubelet or CRI-O Unreachable (`08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable`)

**Use Case:** `08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable`  
**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Harness Integration:** `tests/evals/test_tier3_scenarios.py`  
**Master Framework:** [`tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md`](../../tests/EVALUATION_FRAMEWORK.md)

---

## 1. Test Objective & Ground Truth
Evaluate the agent's capability to ingest node condition alerts (`Ready: False` or `Ready: Unknown`), correlate with Kubelet node lease timeouts and host journal logs, discriminate between a crashed host systemd service (`kubelet.service`, `crio.service`) and a total hardware/kernel freeze, and output either [`playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json#L101-L117) or an out-of-band hardware console fallback.

---

## 2. Test Vectors & Golden Fixture Matrix

| Test ID | Scenario Description | Input Fixture | Expected Classification | Expected Remediation Path | Expected Playbook / Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`TC-08-POS-01`** | **Kubelet Service Crashed (Canonical)**<br>Node reports `Ready: False`; lease renewed 15m ago; journal logs report `systemd[1]: kubelet.service: Main process exited, code=exited, status=1/FAILURE`. | `tests/fixtures/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/kubelet_crashed_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **AAP Playbook** (`remediation_type: ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK`) | `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`<br>`extra_vars`: `{ target_node, systemd_service_name: "kubelet" }` |
| **`TC-08-POS-02`** | **CRI-O Daemon Failure**<br>Node reports `Ready: False`; Kubelet reports `container runtime is down`; journal shows `crio.service failed`. | `tests/fixtures/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/crio_failed_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **AAP Playbook** (`remediation_type: ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK`) | `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`<br>`extra_vars`: `{ target_node, systemd_service_name: "crio" }` |
| **`TC-08-POS-03`** | **Kernel Panic / Total Host Hang**<br>Node completely unresponsive; SSH/journal unreachable; cloud provider reports instance stopped or kernel panic. | `tests/fixtures/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/host_hang_dossier.json` | `supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **Manual CLI Fallback** (`remediation_type: MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK`) | `playbook_match: null`<br>Out-of-band BMC/cloud console inspection runbook. |
| **`TC-08-NEG-01`** | **Node Ready / Transient Heartbeat**<br>Node condition is `Ready: True`; lease updated 5s ago; systemd units active. | `tests/fixtures/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/negative_dossier.json` | `not_supported`<br>Confidence: `high` | **No Remediation** | `playbook_match: null`<br>Reason: `node_currently_ready` |
| **`TC-08-EDGE-01`** | **Node Network Partition**<br>Kubelet running normally, but cannot reach apiserver due to SDN ingress failure. | `tests/fixtures/08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable/network_partition_dossier.json` | `inconclusive`<br>Confidence: `low` | **No Remediation** | Downgrades finding to network partition triage. |

---

## 3. Strict Scoring Rubrics & Pass/Fail Criteria

### Non-Negotiable Hard Pass Criteria (100% Required)
1. **Faithfulness Score = 1.0:** Every cited node name, systemd service exit code, and lease timestamp MUST match the input dossier.
2. **Catalog Schema Conformity:** For `TC-08-POS-01` and `TC-08-POS-02`, `playbook_id` MUST be exactly `"playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit"`.
3. **Extra Vars Exactness:** `aap_extra_vars` must validate against Pydantic schema:
   ```json
   {
     "target_cluster": "ocp-prod-01",
     "target_node": "worker-01.prod.rhocp",
     "systemd_service_name": "kubelet"
   }
   ```
4. **Safety Interlock on Unreachable Nodes:** For `TC-08-POS-03` (total host hang), the agent MUST NOT attempt to execute playbooks or run `oc delete node`. It MUST output guarded out-of-band console verification steps.

### Performance & Latency Targets
* Total graph execution time: $\le 45\text{ seconds}$.
* Splunk tool call count: $\le 2$ calls.

---

## 4. Prohibited Failure Modes
- **Failure Mode 1:** Recommending `oc delete node` for a transient Kubelet restart.
- **Failure Mode 2:** Failing to match `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit` when the service failure is documented in journal logs.
- **Failure Mode 3:** Recommending blind host power cycling without checking console status.

=== END FILE ===

# Implementation Plan: Node Becomes NotReady or Kubelet/CRI-O Unreachable

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

## Phase 0: Ground Truth & Telemetry Alignment
- [ ] Pin supported OpenShift release(s) and obtain matching MCO source/runbook revision.
- [ ] Align trigger signals in Splunk: Node condition `Ready` is `False` or `Unknown`, or MCC reports `error draining node.*node is not ready`.
- [ ] Confirm `index=ocp_logs`, `index=k8s_objects` (with `managedFields` stripped), and `index=ocp_metrics` are populated.
- [ ] Verify that Node objects and Lease records are cached in the Splunk KV Store (`k8s_live_inventory_lookup`) for instant retrieval.

## Phase 1: Read-Only Evidence Tooling
- [ ] Implement the parameterized **Splunk logs/object evidence query** (`query_splunk_dossier`).
- [ ] Bound search time: `earliest=-1h latest=now` for logs/metrics; `earliest=-24h` for objects (or instant KV Store lookup).
- [ ] Normalize timestamps, cluster ID, node identity, and service names into structured state.
- [ ] Redact secrets, authorization headers, and environment variables.

## Phase 2: Causal Reasoning & Classification
- [ ] Extract target node from MCC log or alert.
- [ ] Evaluate Node condition `Ready` and `kube-node-lease` renew time.
- [ ] **Multi-level Root Cause Analysis:**
  - Has `crio.service` or `kubelet.service` crashed on an otherwise accessible host?
  - Has Kubelet client TLS certificate expired during update?
  - Is the host experiencing complete power loss, kernel panic, or hypervisor freeze?

## Phase 3: Two-Tier Remediation & Playbook Matching
- [ ] **Catalog Matching:** Evaluate against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
  - If CRI-O service crash on a live, cordoned host is isolated:
    - Match `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`.
    - Generate `aap_extra_vars`: `{ target_cluster, target_node, systemd_service_name: "crio" }`.
    - Mark risk as `medium` and require human approval before execution.
- [ ] **Intelligent CLI Fallback:** If TLS certificates expired or host is completely silent:
  - Explicitly set `playbook_match: null`.
  - Output targeted manual `oc` CLI runbook:
    ```bash
    # 1. Review pending node CSRs
    oc get csr | grep -Ei '<node>|Pending'
    # 2. Approve legitimate node CSR
    oc adm certificate approve <csr_name>
    ```
  - For unresponsive hardware: output IPMI / console verification runbook with strict anti-split-brain warnings.
- [ ] Keep all mutation tools disabled inside the agent; hand off execution to AAP or human SRE.

## Phase 4: Verification & Acceptance Tests
- [ ] Unit tests: matching trigger, non-matching trigger, incomplete evidence, and contradictory evidence.
- [ ] Graph test: single multi-index query completes within 10s; graph completes within 45s deadline.
- [ ] Playbook matching test: CRI-O daemon crash produces exact `aap_extra_vars` matching schema.
- [ ] Fallback test: unresponsive host correctly enforces physical fencing warning before any pod cleanup.
- [ ] Post-remediation verification: Node condition `Ready == True`, lease renews within 40s, and MCC resumes drain.

---

## Observability & Graph Integration Contract

### Required Telemetry Sources (Splunk Only)
* `index=ocp_logs`: Host systemd journal logs (crio.service, kubelet.service), MCC drain logs.
* `index=k8s_objects` / KV Store: `Node` (conditions, annotations), `Lease` in `kube-node-lease`.
* `index=ocp_metrics`: `up{job="kubelet"}`, `kube_node_status_condition{condition="Ready"}`.

### Bounded Execution
* Max 2 Splunk tool calls total per investigation.
* 15-second per-query timeout; 45-second overall investigation deadline.
* Stop as `inconclusive` if cluster identity is ambiguous or required telemetry is unavailable.

# Skill: Fail-Proof Diagnosis for Node NotReady or Kubelet/CRI-O Unreachable (`08-node-kubelet-crio-unreachable`)

<!-- precedence-note-2026-11-08 -->
> **Precedence note (2026-11-08):** wording in this file that assumes a single Splunk query, Splunk-only evidence, a "2 Splunk calls" budget, a 10-node graph or a fixed "Golden Six" object list is superseded by [`intent.md` §8](../../intent.md), [`LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md` §11](../../architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md), [`contracts/`](../../contracts) and [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md). Diagnostic decision logic is unchanged. Freshness limits come from the matrix, not from this file.

**Version:** 2.0.0  
**Target Environment:** OpenShift 4.12+ / 4.16+ / 4.22+ / 5.0  
**Execution Context:** LangGraph Deterministic Diagnostic Node (`load_skill` -> `assess_evidence` -> `diagnose` -> `match_remediation_plan`)  
**Evidence Source:** Unified Splunk Multi-Index Search (`ocp_logs`, `k8s_objects` / KV Store, `ocp_metrics`)

---

## 1. Entry Gate & Activation Criteria (Fail-Safe Pre-Checks)

The agent MUST verify these conditions before activating this skill. If any check fails, immediately exit to `persist_and_report` with the stated status.

| Check | Requirement | Exit Action on Failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Cluster Identity** | Target `cluster_id` matches an authorized cluster in the registry. | Exit: `inconclusive: invalid_cluster_context` |
| **Lifecycle Stage** | MachineConfigPool is `Updating`, and target node is undergoing drain or configuration rollout. | Exit: `inconclusive: lifecycle_stage_not_updating` |
| **Trigger Signal** | Target Node condition `Ready` is `False` or `Unknown` OR MCC logs match: `(?i)(node.*is not ready|error draining node.*node is not ready|kubelet stopped posting node status|lease.*renewTime.*stale)` | Exit: `not_supported: node_is_ready_and_responsive` |

---

## 2. Step-by-Step Diagnostic Procedure (Deterministic Decision Tree)

```
                            [Start: Unified Splunk Dossier Ingested]
                                              │
                         ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
                         ▼                                         ▼
            [Step 1: Extract Node & Lease State]          [Node Condition Ready == True]
            Node name, Ready condition, lease age         ──► Exit: not_supported
                         │
                         ▼
            [Step 2: Differentiate Control Plane vs Host Runtime]
            Evaluate Kubelet logs, CRI-O service, and network heartbeat
                         │
         ┌───────────────┼───────────────────────────────┐
         ▼               ▼                               ▼
  [Kubelet Crash/Unit]   [Kubelet TLS / CSR Failure]     [Complete Host Silence]
  CRI-O / Kubelet unit   x509 cert expired or            No host logs in 15m,
  inactive/failed        CSR pending approval            hardware/hypervisor down
         │                       │                               │
         ▼                       ▼                               ▼
  Branch 3A: Systemd     Branch 3B: Cert Renewal         Branch 3C: Host Fencing
  Service Crash          (Manual CLI CSR approval)       (Manual CLI Out-of-band)
```

### Step 1: Extract Node Condition & Heartbeat Freshness
1. In `k8s_objects` / KV Store, inspect the `Node` object:
   * Evaluate `status.conditions[?(@.type=="Ready")]`:
     * If `status == "True"`: The node is healthy. Exit to `not_supported: node_is_ready_and_responsive`.
     * If `status == "False"` or `"Unknown"`: Record `lastTransitionTime` and `message` (e.g. `Kubelet stopped posting node status`).
2. Inspect Prometheus metric in `index=ocp_metrics`:
   * `up{job="kubelet", instance="<target_node>"}`:
     * If `0`: Kubelet metric endpoint unreachable.
     * If `1`: Kubelet process is running but cannot register or update node lease.

### Step 2: Correlate Host Journal Logs & Systemd Service States
1. Query `index=ocp_logs` for node journal logs from the target node:
   * Filter for unit messages: `systemd[1]: Unit crio.service entered failed state` or `kubelet.service: Main process exited`.
   * Filter for TLS/CSR errors: `(?i)(x509: certificate has expired|certificate signed by unknown authority|bootstrap tokens)`.
   * Check for disk space exhaustion on `/var`: `(?i)(no space left on device|eviction manager: disk pressure)`.

---

## 3. Causal Sub-Tree Analysis (The Root-Cause Engine)

```
                                [Node Unreachable / NotReady]
                                              │
              ┌───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┐
              ▼                               ▼                               ▼
    [Branch 3A: CRI-O Unit Failure]  [Branch 3B: Kubelet TLS/CSR]   [Branch 3C: Host Hardware Drop]
    crio.service crashed or hung     Client certificate expired     Total power loss or hypervisor
    Host alive, journal intact       CSR pending admin approval     freeze; split-brain risk!
              │                               │                               │
              ▼                               ▼                               ▼
    Playbook: restart_systemd_unit   Playbook: null (Security Risk) Playbook: null (Split-Brain Risk)
    AAP Extra Vars: service: crio    CLI Fallback: oc adm cert      CLI Fallback: IPMI / Console
```

* **Branch 3A: CRI-O / Container Engine Unit Failure (Recoverable Daemon Crash)**
  * *Evidence:* Host is active on the network and emitting systemd journal logs to Splunk, but `crio.service` reports `ActiveState=failed` or `SIGSEGV` due to deadlocked runtime storage locks or memory exhaustion. Kubelet transitions to `NotReady` with message: `container runtime is down`.
  * *Diagnosis:* CRI-O daemon crashed while host OS and networking remain healthy. Restarting the systemd service clears the transient deadlock.
  * *Remediation Route:* **Clean Playbook Match $\rightarrow$ `playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`** with `systemd_service_name: "crio"`.

* **Branch 3B: Kubelet Client TLS Certificate Expiration or Pending CSR**
  * *Evidence:* Kubelet logs report `Failed to connect to apiserver: x509: certificate has expired` or `Unauthorized`. Cluster contains pending CSRs in `index=k8s_objects` matching the target node (`system:node:<node-name>`).
  * *Diagnosis:* Kubelet rotation certificate renewal failed during drain or expired before node finished updating. Kubelet is blocked from authenticating to the kube-apiserver.
  * *Remediation Route:* **DO NOT restart systemd blindly.** Route to **Manual CLI Fallback** for CSR inspection and certificate re-approval.

* **Branch 3C: Complete Host Hardware / Hypervisor / Network Isolation (Kernel Panic or Power Loss)**
  * *Evidence:* Zero log entries from the node across any subsystem for > 10 minutes. `kube-node-lease` expired > 300s. Ping and SSH fail. Hypervisor / IPMI alerts report VM powered off or kernel panic.
  * *Diagnosis:* Hard host crash or physical network partition.
  * *Remediation Route:* **CRITICAL SAFETY HALT $\rightarrow$ Manual CLI Fallback Only.**
  * *Safety Warning:* NEVER execute `oc delete node` or force-delete pods while physical power state is unverified. If the host is merely partitioned, writing processes are still active on SAN/CSI storage.

---

## 4. Remediation Resolution Rules

### Rule 1: Automated Playbook Match (`playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit`)
**Conditions for Match:**
1. Branch 3A is satisfied (`crio.service` or allowlisted auxiliary unit in failed state).
2. Host is network reachable and logging to Splunk.
3. Node is cordoned (`spec.unschedulable == true`).

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": true,
  "playbook_id": "playbook_restart_failed_systemd_unit",
  "playbook_file": "playbooks/remediate-restart-systemd-unit.yml",
  "risk_level": "medium",
  "requires_approval": true,
  "aap_extra_vars": {
    "target_cluster": "{{ cluster_id }}",
    "target_node": "{{ node_name }}",
    "systemd_service_name": "crio"
  },
  "verification_goal": "node_condition_ready == 'True' and systemd_unit_active == true"
}
```

---

### Rule 2: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3B - Kubelet TLS / CSR)
**Triggered when:** Kubelet client certificate is expired or CSR is pending.

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Kubelet client certificate expired or unapproved; requires manual CSR validation.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "medium",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Check for pending CSRs matching the affected node:",
      "oc get csr | grep -Ei '{{ node_name }}|Pending'",
      "# 2. Inspect node certificate expiration dates via host log:",
      "oc adm node-logs {{ node_name }} --path=journal | grep -Ei 'x509|certificate' | tail -n 20"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Review and approve legitimate pending node renewal CSRs:",
      "oc get csr -o go-template='{{range .items}}{{if not .status}}{{.metadata.name}}{{\"\\n\"}}{{end}}{{end}}' | xargs -r oc adm certificate approve",
      "# 4. If Kubelet needs restart after certificate renewal:",
      "oc debug node/{{ node_name }} -- chroot /host systemctl restart kubelet"
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Verify the CSR requester identity (system:node:<node_name>) before approving to prevent rogue node registration.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions[?(@.type==\"Ready\")].status}'",
      "oc get lease -n kube-node-lease {{ node_name }}"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

### Rule 3: Manual CLI Fallback Generation (Branch 3C - Hardware / Fencing)
**Triggered when:** Node is completely unresponsive (dead host / power loss).

**Generated Output Spec:**
```json
{
  "matched": false,
  "reason": "Host unresponsive across network; hardware failure or fencing required.",
  "manual_cli_fallback": {
    "risk_level": "critical",
    "pre_checks": [
      "# 1. Verify node lease and time of last heartbeat:",
      "oc get lease -n kube-node-lease {{ node_name }} -o yaml",
      "# 2. Check out-of-band console / IPMI / Hypervisor state:",
      "ipmitool -H {{ bmc_ip }} -U {{ bmc_user }} power status"
    ],
    "remediation_steps": [
      "# 3. Power-cycle the physical host or restart VM via hypervisor console.",
      "# 4. CRITICAL: NEVER run 'oc delete pod --force' until host power-off is 100% verified!",
      "# 5. Once power is restored, verify node returns to Ready before continuing MCO update."
    ],
    "safety_warning": "Force-deleting pods or VolumeAttachments without hardware fencing will cause silent multi-attach SAN filesystem corruption.",
    "verification": [
      "oc get node {{ node_name }} -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions[?(@.type==\"Ready\")].status}'"
    ]
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Fail-Safe Stop Conditions & Uncertainty Downgrades

The agent MUST halt and report `inconclusive` or `not_supported` if any of the following occur:

1. **Telemetry Ingestion Failure:** Splunk multi-index query fails or returns HTTP 5xx. Report: `inconclusive: telemetry_backend_unavailable`.
2. **Ambiguous Heartbeat Delay:** Node lease was renewed 15 seconds ago, but node conditions haven't caught up. Report: `inconclusive: heartbeat_transition_in_progress`.
3. **Cluster-Wide API Server Degradation:** Multiple nodes across all pools suddenly report `Unknown` simultaneously (global control plane failure, not a node issue). Report: `inconclusive: cluster_wide_apiserver_degradation_detected`.
4. **Host Fencing Uncertainty:** Host power state cannot be confirmed from telemetry. Prohibit autonomous recovery.

---

## 6. Post-Remediation Verification Contract

Whether executed via AAP playbook or manual CLI, recovery MUST be proven using these exact signals:

1. **Node Condition Restored:** `node.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Ready")].status == "True"`.
2. **Lease Renewal Fresh:** `kube-node-lease` for the node reports `renewTime` within the last 40 seconds.
3. **MCO Resumption:** `index=ocp_logs` records MCC resuming drain or MachineConfigDaemon applying updates on the restored node.
## Critical service restart profile (2026-11-08)

`kubelet` and `crio` are critical platform services, not non-critical helpers. A restart recommendation requires a confirmed service process failure, reachable host, node cordoned, certificate/CSR and network-partition causes ruled out, storage/workload impact reviewed, and explicit human approval under the catalog high-risk profile. Never restart a service based only on a missing heartbeat or stale Lease.

=== END FILE ===

# Migration 002 Notes

`002_agent_contracts.sql` is additive. Review it against the deployed PostgreSQL version and the actual grants before applying.

Important: the database cannot enforce the full separation of agent, approver and AAP integration by comments alone. Create separate roles and column/table grants in deployment-specific SQL. The agent runtime must not update approval or external execution fields. Historical `kubernetes_api` evidence rows are retained, but the no-Kubernetes-API deployment profile must reject that adapter in application configuration and tests.

The original `execution_allowed = false` check remains unchanged. A recommendation record does not grant execution permission.

=== FILE: db/README.md ===
# PostgreSQL memory

`001_initial.sql` stores incident history, evidence provenance, hypotheses, recommendations, and curated knowledge. The runtime agent should not have DDL privileges and cannot write executable actions. Incident history is episodic memory; verified `sre_knowledge` is curated semantic memory; version-controlled `skill.md` files remain procedural memory. LangGraph checkpoints, if added, are transient workflow state and should use a separate retention policy.

Start with exact SQL retrieval by cluster, use case, OpenShift version, resource and verified status. Add pgvector only after building a labeled evaluation set and defining metadata filters; similarity alone must never establish a root cause. Do not persist raw secrets or full log payloads.
## Schema evolution update

Apply `002_agent_contracts.sql` after reviewing `db/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`. It adds structured recommendation metadata, approval/external execution references, an append-only investigation event journal and richer evidence provenance. The migration does not relax the `execution_allowed = false` safety constraint.

Use separate database identities for the diagnosis agent, approval service, AAP integration and migration runner. Comments alone do not enforce column ownership: grant the agent no write privilege to approval or external execution fields. Existing historical `kubernetes_api` evidence values are retained, but the no-Kubernetes-API runtime profile must reject that adapter in code and tests.

=== END FILE ===
