-- PostgreSQL case memory schema. Apply through a migration runner in a transaction.
CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_investigations (
  investigation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  idempotency_key TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  trigger_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (trigger_type IN ('alert','manual')),
  cluster_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  cluster_name TEXT NOT NULL,
  openshift_version TEXT,
  node_name TEXT,
  mcp_name TEXT,
  lifecycle_stage TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unknown',
  use_case_id TEXT,
  skill_version TEXT,
  status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('running','diagnosed','inconclusive','unclassified_anomaly','failed')) DEFAULT 'running',
  diagnosis_status TEXT CHECK (diagnosis_status IN ('supported','probable','inconclusive','no_matching_skill','version_gate_failed','unclassified_anomaly')),
  root_cause_hypothesis TEXT,
  confidence_band TEXT CHECK (confidence_band IN ('low','medium','high','exploratory')),
  started_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  deadline_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  summary TEXT,
  missing_evidence JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
  safety_flags JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence (
  evidence_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  investigation_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_investigations(investigation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  source TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('splunk','prometheus','kubernetes_api')),
  tool_name TEXT NOT NULL,
  request_id TEXT,
  query_or_request_redacted TEXT,
  cluster_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  resource_ref TEXT,
  event_time TIMESTAMPTZ,
  observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
  ingested_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  result_summary TEXT NOT NULL,
  source_uri TEXT,
  content_hash TEXT,
  freshness TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (freshness IN ('fresh','stale','unknown')) DEFAULT 'unknown',
  redaction_status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'redacted',
  attributes JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_hypotheses (
  hypothesis_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  investigation_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_investigations(investigation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  hypothesis TEXT NOT NULL,
  status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('supported','contradicted','unresolved')),
  rationale TEXT NOT NULL,
  supporting_evidence_ids UUID[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  contradicting_evidence_ids UUID[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  confidence_band TEXT CHECK (confidence_band IN ('low','medium','high','exploratory')),
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_recommendations (
  recommendation_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  investigation_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_investigations(investigation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  recommendation TEXT NOT NULL,
  risk_level TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (risk_level IN ('low','medium','high','disruptive','unknown')),
  requires_human_approval BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  execution_allowed BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false CHECK (execution_allowed = false),
  verification_steps JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_knowledge (
  knowledge_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  use_case_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  knowledge_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (knowledge_type IN ('verified_pattern','runbook_reference','release_note','anti_pattern')),
  title TEXT NOT NULL,
  content TEXT NOT NULL,
  source_uri TEXT,
  source_revision TEXT,
  openshift_version_range TEXT,
  verification_status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (verification_status IN ('unverified','verified','deprecated')) DEFAULT 'unverified',
  verified_by TEXT,
  last_verified_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_cluster_time_idx ON sre_investigations(cluster_id, started_at DESC);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_case_status_idx ON sre_investigations(use_case_id, status, started_at DESC);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_inv_time_idx ON sre_evidence(investigation_id, observed_at DESC);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_resource_time_idx ON sre_evidence(cluster_id, resource_ref, observed_at DESC);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_hypotheses_inv_idx ON sre_hypotheses(investigation_id, status);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_knowledge_case_verified_idx ON sre_knowledge(use_case_id, verification_status, last_verified_at DESC);

-- Operational notes:
-- 1. Grant the agent INSERT/SELECT on incident tables and SELECT on verified knowledge only.
-- 2. Keep schema migrations owned by deployment/admin role, not the runtime agent.
-- 3. Add pgvector in a later migration only after retrieval evaluation and retention/security review.
-- 4. Configure backup, retention, encryption at rest/in transit, and row-level security per tenancy.

    CHECK (verification_status IN ('not_started','pending','recovered','not_recovered','inconclusive'));

  WHERE recommendation_reference IS NOT NULL;

  ON sre_recommendations(external_execution_reference)
  WHERE external_execution_reference IS NOT NULL;

  event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
  event_time TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  actor_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (actor_type IN ('agent','human','adapter','collector','aap_service','system')),
  actor_id TEXT,
  correlation_id TEXT,
  idempotency_key TEXT,
  payload JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
  payload_hash TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_events_inv_time_idx
  ON sre_investigation_events(investigation_id, event_time);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_events_idempotency_idx
  ON sre_investigation_events(investigation_id, idempotency_key)
  WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL;

-- Persist deterministic joins and their provenance, rather than asking an LLM to reconstruct them.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_relationships (
  relationship_id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  investigation_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_investigations(investigation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  from_evidence_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_evidence(evidence_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  to_evidence_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES sre_evidence(evidence_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  relationship_type TEXT NOT NULL,
  join_rule TEXT NOT NULL,
  result TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (result IN ('confirmed','candidate','ambiguous','rejected','unavailable')) ,
  reason TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CHECK (from_evidence_id <> to_evidence_id)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_relationships_inv_idx
  ON sre_evidence_relationships(investigation_id, created_at);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_events_type_time_idx
  ON sre_investigation_events(event_type, event_time DESC);

  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS event_type TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS metric_name TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS metric_labels JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS metric_value DOUBLE PRECISION,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS metric_unit TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS resource_uid TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS resource_version TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS evidence_quality TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'valid'
    CHECK (evidence_quality IN ('valid','partial','malformed','contradictory'));

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_metric_idx
  ON sre_evidence(cluster_id, metric_name, event_time DESC)
  WHERE metric_name IS NOT NULL;

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_evidence_resource_uid_idx
  ON sre_evidence(cluster_id, resource_uid, observed_at DESC)
  WHERE resource_uid IS NOT NULL;

-- Explicit retention metadata. Actual deletion/archival is owned by a scheduled maintenance job.
ALTER TABLE sre_investigations
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS retention_class TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'standard',
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS source_event_id TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS registry_revision TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS model_id TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS prompt_version TEXT,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS skill_content_hash TEXT;

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS sre_inv_source_event_idx
  ON sre_investigations(cluster_id, source_event_id)
  WHERE source_event_id IS NOT NULL;

=== FILE: architecture/LANGGRAPH_DESIGN.md ===
# LangGraph Architecture and Routing Contract

## Chosen Pattern: Dual-Track Bounded Workflow

The agent uses a **Deterministic State Machine with Bounded Cognitive Evaluators** operating in two distinct tracks:

1. **Track A (Known Scenario Track - Use Cases 01 to 16):** Strictly executes the deterministic decision trees defined in the loaded `skill.md`, matching approved Ansible Playbook specs or guarded manual CLI fallbacks.
2. **Track B (Exploratory Unknown Anomaly Track):** When an incident does not match any of the 16 known use cases, the Python engine deterministically collects a bounded error bundle from Splunk, and a dedicated cognitive node invokes the LLM **in exactly one prompt (zero iterative tool calls)** to synthesize the novel anomaly and provide a structured briefing and manual CLI inspection steps for the SRE.

The agent operates in a **strictly read-only mode** with zero cluster mutation tools.

---

## Logical Nodes (Dual-Track Architecture)

### Core Pipeline Nodes (Shared)
1. `ingest_event`: Accept alert or engineer request; assign `investigation_id` and idempotency key; start 45s timer.
2. `normalize_context`: Validate cluster/node identity, event time, OpenShift version, and resolve MCP name from node metadata.
3. `classify_lifecycle`: Classify lifecycle stage (`drain`, `config_apply`, `pivot_reboot`, `uncordon`, `mcp_rollout`, `cluster_upgrade`, or `unknown`).
4. `load_skill`: Match alert/log signatures against the 16 allow-listed use cases.

### Track A Nodes (Known Use Cases 01 - 16)
5. `build_evidence_plan`: Formulate bounded query parameters for the unified Splunk multi-index search and KV Store lookup.
6. `query_splunk_dossier`: Execute a bounded Splunk query for logs, Events and object snapshots; Prometheus MCP is queried separately for metrics.
7. `assess_evidence`: Validate timestamps, freshness, and contradictions; deduplicate evidence.
8. `diagnose`: Evaluate evidence strictly against the loaded `skill.md` decision tree to rank hypotheses.
9. `match_remediation_plan`: Evaluate diagnosed root cause against [`remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json`](../remediations/PLAYBOOK_CATALOG.json):
   - Outputs Day-1 Ansible YAML Pseudo-code + verified variables.
   - Outputs copy-pasteable manual `oc` CLI fallback with safety checks.

### Track B Nodes (Exploratory Unknown Anomaly Track)
5b. `build_broad_anomaly_plan`: Formulate a bounded SPL query targeting top error/warning logs, warning events, and resource spikes on the target node.
6b. `query_broad_anomaly_dossier`: Execute a **single bounded Splunk search** (<5s) returning the top 50 anomalous events on that node.
8b. `synthesize_unknown_anomaly`: Single LLM cognitive invocation (**zero iterative tool loops**). Analyzes the error bundle, identifies the top 2-3 novel anomalies, and formulates a working hypothesis.
9b. `format_sre_briefing`: Enforces `playbook_match: false` / `null`, structures ruled-out standard cases, and outputs safe, read-only `oc` CLI commands for human SRE triage.

### Finalization Node (Shared)
10. `persist_and_report`: Persist incident metadata, evidence citations, and remediation specs to PostgreSQL ([`001_initial.sql`](../db/migrations/001_initial.sql)), returning the final report.

---

## Conditional Edges & Routing Flowchart

```
                            [ingest_event]
                                   │
                                   ▼
[normalize_context] ──(invalid cluster)─────────────► [persist_and_report] (inconclusive: invalid_context)
       │
       ▼ (valid context)
[classify_lifecycle] ──(unsupported stage)──────────► [persist_and_report] (inconclusive: lifecycle_unknown)
       │
       ▼ (stage == drain/upgrade)
  [load_skill]
       │
       ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐
       ▼ (Skill Matched: UC 01-16)                ▼ (No Skill Matched / Unknown Issue)
[build_evidence_plan]                      [build_broad_anomaly_plan]
       │                                          │
       ▼                                          ▼
[query_splunk_dossier]             [query_broad_anomaly_dossier]
       │                                          │
       ▼                                          │
[assess_evidence]                                 │
       │                                          │
       ├─► (missing data & budget < 2) ──► loop   │
       │                                          │
       ▼ (evidence evaluated)                     │
[diagnose]                                        ▼
       │                                   [synthesize_unknown_anomaly]
       ▼                                   (Single LLM call - No tool loop)
[match_remediation_plan]                          │
       │                                          ▼
       │                                   [format_sre_briefing]
       │                                   (playbook_match: null enforced)
       │                                          │
       └────────────────────┬─────────────────────┘
                            │
                            ▼
                  [persist_and_report]
                            │
                            ▼
                          [END]
```

---

## Execution Bounds and Latency Targets

* **Investigation Deadline:** Strict 45 seconds total execution time across both tracks.
* **Tool Call Budget:** Max **2 Splunk tool calls total** per investigation (Track A: 1 primary + at most 1 follow-up; Track B: exactly 1 broad error query).
* **Per-Query Timeout:** 15 seconds. Retry at most once for transient timeout/5xx errors with exponential backoff and jitter. Never retry authentication or query validation errors.
* **Evidence Time Windows:**
  * Logs (`index=ocp_logs`): `earliest=-1h latest=now`.
  * Object State (`index=k8s_objects` or KV Store): `earliest=-24h latest=now` (or instant KV Store lookup).
  * Metrics (`index=ocp_metrics`): `earliest=-1h latest=now`.
* **Hard Stop Safety:** The agent contains zero mutation tools. No `oc delete`, `oc patch`, or direct API writes.

---

## State Contract (`InvestigationState`)

```python
class InvestigationState(BaseModel):
    # Context
    investigation_id: str
    idempotency_key: str
    trigger_type: str
    cluster_id: str
    cluster_name: str
    openshift_version: Optional[str]
    node_name: str
    mcp_name: Optional[str]
    lifecycle_stage: str
    use_case_id: Optional[str]
    track: str = Field(default="TRACK_A_KNOWN", description="TRACK_A_KNOWN or TRACK_B_EXPLORATORY")
    started_at: float
    deadline_at: float

    # Evidence
    dossier_raw: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
    broad_anomalies_raw: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
    query_count: int = 0
    tool_errors: List[str] = Field(default_factory=list)

    # Diagnosis
    classification: str  # supported | probable | inconclusive | not_supported | unclassified_anomaly
    confidence: str      # high | medium | low | exploratory
    summary: str
    ruled_out_scenarios: List[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    anomalous_signals: List[Dict[str, Any]] = Field(default_factory=list)

    # Remediation (Two-Tier / Day-1 Pseudocode)
    remediation_type: str  # ANSIBLE_PLAYBOOK_PSEUDOCODE | MANUAL_CLI_RUNBOOK
    playbook_match: bool = False
    playbook_id: Optional[str] = None
    ansible_pseudocode: Optional[List[str]] = None
    manual_cli_fallback: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None

    # Outcome
    persist_status: Optional[str]
```

---

## Operating and Safety Model

1. **Deterministic Bounded Harness:** Track B exploratory triage **never** enters an open ReAct loop. The Python engine fetches the data; the LLM synthesizes it in a single prompt.
2. **Hardcoded Playbook Disablement for Unknowns:** Track B physically enforces `playbook_match = False` and `playbook_id = null`. Automated playbooks are strictly barred from executing on unclassified anomalies.
3. **Auditability:** Every diagnosis outputs exact timestamped log lines, object resourceVersions, and metric values supporting the conclusion.
4. **PostgreSQL System of Record:** Metadata, hypotheses, and generated remediation specs are persisted to PostgreSQL using [`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`](../db/migrations/001_initial.sql).
---

## 11. Federated evidence adapter clarification (2026-11-08)

The Track A path must not assume every required signal is indexed in Splunk. In the default deployment, `query_splunk_dossier` retrieves indexed logs/object snapshots/events through Splunk MCP, while a separate Prometheus MCP adapter retrieves metric evidence. The evidence aggregation/correlation layer joins these responses into the canonical dossier. The one-query-per-investigation pattern is available only within each adapter where its capabilities prove the required evidence can be returned within the configured bounds.

The previous “max 2 Splunk calls” budget is scoped to Splunk requests and is not a total multi-adapter budget. The default Track A budget is one initial Splunk query, one initial Prometheus query, at most one targeted logical follow-up across either adapter, and at most one transient retry across the entire investigation. Count every transport attempt; do not exceed four external request attempts or the 45-second absolute deadline. Track B performs one bounded broad query and at most one transient retry, with one LLM synthesis call and no iterative tool loop.

All latency figures remain targets until measured at expected concurrency.

=== FILE: agent/skills/dossier.py ===
"""Normalized dossier consumed by the UC01 diagnosis node (shape of tests/fixtures/01-pdb-eviction-blocked)."""
from __future__ import annotations
from typing import Literal
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field

class _Strict(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

class PodDetails(_Strict):
    name: str; namespace: str; owner_kind: str | None = None; owner_name: str | None = None
    labels: dict[str, str] = Field(default_factory=dict); phase: str

class PdbDetails(_Strict):
    name: str; namespace: str
    selector: dict | None = None
    min_available: int | str | None = None
    max_unavailable: int | str | None = None
    current_healthy: int; desired_healthy: int; disruptions_allowed: int
    expected_pods: int | None = None
    snapshot_age_s: int                      # seconds since the PDB snapshot was observed
    overlapping_pdb_count: int = 1           # number of PDBs selecting the failing pod

class WorkloadDetails(_Strict):
    kind: str; name: str; desired_replicas: int; ready_replicas: int
    # None = unknown. Unknown is NOT treated as "safe" by any rule.
    hpa_present: bool | None = None
    gitops_managed: bool | None = None
    has_volume_claim_templates: bool | None = None
    quota_blocks_scale_up: bool | None = None

class SiblingPod(_Strict):
    name: str; phase: str; ready: bool; restart_count: int = 0
    waiting_reason: str | None = None; scheduler_message: str | None = None

class CapacityDetails(_Strict):
    schedulable_nodes: int; free_cpu_m: int; free_mem_mi: int
    pod_request_cpu_m: int; pod_request_mem_mi: int

class NodeDetails(_Strict):
    name: str; conditions: dict[str, str] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    desired_drain: str | None = None; last_applied_drain: str | None = None

class Uc01Record(_Strict):
    cluster_id: str; target_namespace: str; target_resource: str; node_name: str
    first_seen: str; last_seen: str
    controller_eviction_errors: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)   # UNTRUSTED text
    objects_observed: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    pod_details: PodDetails | None = None
    pdb_details: PdbDetails | None = None
    workload_details: WorkloadDetails | None = None
    sibling_pods: list[SiblingPod] = Field(default_factory=list)
    capacity_details: CapacityDetails | None = None
    node_details: NodeDetails | None = None
    relevant_metrics: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)

class AdapterErr(_Strict):
    adapter: str; error: str; retried: bool = False

class Uc01Dossier(_Strict):
    status: Literal["ok", "partial", "error"]
    freshness: Literal["fresh", "stale", "unknown"] = "unknown"
    quality: Literal["valid", "partial", "malformed", "contradictory"] = "valid"
    observed_at: str
    execution_time_ms: int = 0
    adapter_errors: list[AdapterErr] = Field(default_factory=list)
    records: list[Uc01Record] = Field(default_factory=list)

=== FILE: agent/skills/engine.py ===
"""Constrained rule interpreter for skill profile v1 (see contracts/SKILL_RULES.md section 6).
No eval, no templates, no I/O. Rules compare named facts only. First matching rule wins."""
from __future__ import annotations
import re
from typing import Any
from .loader import SkillMeta

OPS = {"eq", "ne", "gt", "gte", "lt", "lte", "in", "is_true", "is_false", "is_null", "not_null"}
TERMINAL_KEYS = {"classification", "confidence", "sub_branch", "reason_code", "remediation"}
REMEDIATION_KEYS = {"kind", "playbook_id", "extra_vars_fact", "blockers_fact"}


def _cmp(actual: Any, op: str, expected: Any = None) -> bool:
    if op == "eq": return actual == expected
    if op == "ne": return actual != expected
    if op in ("gt", "gte", "lt", "lte"):
        if actual is None or expected is None: return False   # unknown never satisfies a numeric test
        return {"gt": actual > expected, "gte": actual >= expected,
                "lt": actual < expected, "lte": actual <= expected}[op]
    if op == "in": return actual in (expected or [])
    if op == "is_true": return actual is True
    if op == "is_false": return actual is False
    if op == "is_null": return actual is None
    if op == "not_null": return actual is not None
    raise ValueError(f"unknown op {op}")


def matches(when: dict, facts: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
    if "always" in when:
        return True
    if "all" in when:
        return all(_cmp(facts[c[0]], c[1], c[2] if len(c) > 2 else None) for c in when["all"])
    return any(_cmp(facts[c[0]], c[1], c[2] if len(c) > 2 else None) for c in when["any"])


def evaluate(meta: SkillMeta, facts: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    for rule in meta.rules:
        if matches(rule.when, facts):
            t = rule.then
            rem = t.get("remediation") or {}
            return {
                "rule_id": rule.id,
                "classification": t["classification"],
                "confidence": t["confidence"],
                "sub_branch": t.get("sub_branch"),
                "stop_reason": t.get("reason_code"),
                "remediation_kind": rem.get("kind", "none"),
                "playbook_id": rem.get("playbook_id"),
                "extra_vars": facts[rem["extra_vars_fact"]] if rem.get("extra_vars_fact") else None,
                "blockers": list(facts[rem["blockers_fact"]]) if rem.get("blockers_fact") else [],
            }
    raise RuntimeError("no rule matched; the last rule of every skill must be an always-rule")


def validate_skill(meta: SkillMeta, *, known_facts: set[str], registry: dict[str, dict],
                   catalog: dict[str, dict], repo) -> list[str]:
    """Static checks. Returns a list of problems (empty means valid)."""
    p: list[str] = []
    ids = [r.id for r in meta.rules]
    if len(ids) != len(set(ids)): p.append("duplicate rule ids")
    if not meta.rules or "always" not in meta.rules[-1].when: p.append("last rule must be an always-rule")
    if set(meta.declared_facts) != known_facts:
        p.append(f"declared_facts differ from code: {sorted(set(meta.declared_facts) ^ known_facts)}")
    if "pdb_pod_snapshot" not in meta.freshness_limits_seconds: p.append("freshness_limits_seconds.pdb_pod_snapshot missing")
    for s in meta.trigger_signatures:
        try: re.compile(s.pattern)
        except re.error as e: p.append(f"trigger pattern does not compile: {e}")
    for name, rel in meta.evaluation_fixtures.items():
        if not (repo / rel).exists(): p.append(f"evaluation fixture missing: {rel}")
    for r in meta.rules:
        for key in ("all", "any"):
            for c in r.when.get(key, []):
                if c[0] not in known_facts: p.append(f"{r.id}: unknown fact {c[0]}")
                if c[1] not in OPS: p.append(f"{r.id}: unknown op {c[1]}")
        if set(r.then) - TERMINAL_KEYS: p.append(f"{r.id}: unknown keys {sorted(set(r.then) - TERMINAL_KEYS)}")
        rem = r.then.get("remediation") or {}
        if set(rem) - REMEDIATION_KEYS: p.append(f"{r.id}: unknown remediation keys")
        cls, conf = r.then.get("classification"), r.then.get("confidence")
        code = r.then.get("reason_code")
        if cls == "supported":
            if code is not None: p.append(f"{r.id}: supported rules must not carry reason_code")
            if conf == "high" and rem.get("kind") != "playbook" and rem.get("kind") != "manual_cli":
                p.append(f"{r.id}: supported/high needs a remediation kind")
        else:
            if code is None: p.append(f"{r.id}: {cls} requires reason_code")
            elif code not in registry: p.append(f"{r.id}: unregistered reason_code {code}")
            elif registry[code]["classification"] != cls:
                p.append(f"{r.id}: {code} registered as {registry[code]['classification']}, used as {cls}")
            if rem.get("kind", "none") != "none": p.append(f"{r.id}: remediation not allowed on {cls}")
        if rem.get("kind") == "playbook":
            pid = rem.get("playbook_id")
            if pid not in catalog: p.append(f"{r.id}: playbook not in catalog: {pid}")
            if pid not in meta.remediation_catalog_refs: p.append(f"{r.id}: playbook not listed in remediation_catalog_refs")
            if rem.get("extra_vars_fact") not in known_facts: p.append(f"{r.id}: extra_vars_fact missing")
        if rem.get("blockers_fact") and rem["blockers_fact"] not in known_facts: p.append(f"{r.id}: blockers_fact missing")
    return p

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: agent/skills/facts_uc01.py ===
"""Deterministic fact derivation for UC01. All matching and arithmetic lives here, in code.
Skill rules only compare the named facts below. Dossier text is data, never instructions."""
from __future__ import annotations
import re
from typing import Any
from .dossier import Uc01Dossier
from .loader import SkillMeta

SUPPORTED_KINDS = {"Deployment", "StatefulSet"}
FACT_NAMES = frozenset({
    "evidence.status", "evidence.record_count", "evidence.target_cluster_record_count",
    "denial.present", "denial.multi_pdb",
    "pdb.present", "pdb.selecting_count", "pdb.below_desired", "pdb.stale", "pdb.disruptions_allowed",
    "workload.single_replica_conflict", "siblings.crashing", "siblings.capacity_starved",
    "pdb.exhausted_all_ready", "scale_up.safe", "scale_up.blockers", "scale_up.extra_vars",
})


def _blockers(r) -> list[str]:
    w, cap, out = r.workload_details, r.capacity_details, []
    if w is None:
        return ["workload_unknown"]
    if w.kind not in SUPPORTED_KINDS:
        out.append("workload_kind_unsupported")
    for value, name in ((w.hpa_present, "hpa_present"), (w.gitops_managed, "gitops_managed"),
                        (w.has_volume_claim_templates, "volume_claim_templates"),
                        (w.quota_blocks_scale_up, "quota_blocks_scale_up")):
        if value is True: out.append(name)
        elif value is None: out.append(f"{name}_unknown")
    if cap is None:
        out.append("capacity_unknown")
    elif not (cap.free_cpu_m > cap.pod_request_cpu_m and cap.free_mem_mi > cap.pod_request_mem_mi):
        out.append("capacity_insufficient")
    return out


def derive(dossier: Uc01Dossier, target: dict[str, Any], meta: SkillMeta) -> dict[str, Any]:
    limit = meta.freshness_limits_seconds["pdb_pod_snapshot"]
    recs = [r for r in dossier.records if r.cluster_id == target["cluster_id"]]
    r = recs[0] if recs else None
    errs = r.controller_eviction_errors if r else []

    def sig_hit(text: str, signal: str | None = None) -> bool:
        return any(re.search(s.pattern, text) for s in meta.trigger_signatures
                   if signal is None or s.signal == signal)

    pdb = r.pdb_details if r else None
    w = r.workload_details if r else None
    sibs = r.sibling_pods if r else []
    all_ready = bool(w and all(s.ready for s in sibs) and w.ready_replicas == w.desired_replicas)
    blockers = _blockers(r) if r else ["no_record"]
    f: dict[str, Any] = {
        "evidence.status": dossier.status,
        "evidence.record_count": len(dossier.records),
        "evidence.target_cluster_record_count": len(recs),
        "denial.present": any(sig_hit(e) for e in errs),
        "denial.multi_pdb": any(sig_hit(e, "multi_pdb") for e in errs),
        "pdb.present": pdb is not None,
        "pdb.selecting_count": pdb.overlapping_pdb_count if pdb else 0,
        "pdb.below_desired": bool(pdb and pdb.current_healthy < pdb.desired_healthy),
        "pdb.stale": bool(pdb and (pdb.snapshot_age_s > limit or dossier.freshness == "stale")),
        "pdb.disruptions_allowed": pdb.disruptions_allowed if pdb else None,
        "workload.single_replica_conflict": bool(
            w and pdb and w.desired_replicas == 1 and (pdb.min_available == 1 or pdb.max_unavailable in (0, "0%"))),
        "siblings.crashing": any(s.waiting_reason == "CrashLoopBackOff" or (not s.ready and s.restart_count >= 3)
                                 for s in sibs),
        "siblings.capacity_starved": any(s.phase == "Pending" and "Insufficient" in (s.scheduler_message or "")
                                         for s in sibs),
        "pdb.exhausted_all_ready": bool(pdb and pdb.disruptions_allowed == 0
                                        and pdb.current_healthy == pdb.desired_healthy and all_ready),
        "scale_up.blockers": blockers,
        "scale_up.safe": not blockers,
        "scale_up.extra_vars": None,
    }
    if not blockers and w and pdb:
        f["scale_up.extra_vars"] = {
            "target_cluster": r.cluster_id, "target_namespace": r.target_namespace,
            "workload_kind": w.kind, "workload_name": w.name, "target_pdb_name": pdb.name,
            "current_replicas": w.desired_replicas, "scale_to": w.desired_replicas + 1}
    assert set(f) == FACT_NAMES, set(f) ^ FACT_NAMES
    return f

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: agent/skills/loader.py ===
"""Load a skill.md: YAML front matter -> strictly validated SkillMeta.
Unknown keys are rejected so a typo in a rule cannot be silently ignored.
The Markdown body is documentation for humans and is not executed."""
from __future__ import annotations
import hashlib, pathlib, re
from typing import Any, Literal
import yaml
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field


class _S(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")


class TriggerSignature(_S):
    pattern: str
    source: str
    signal: Literal["denial", "multi_pdb"] = "denial"


class RequiredEvidence(_S):
    dataset: str
    kinds: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    metrics: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    max_age_seconds: int


class Rule(_S):
    id: str
    when: dict[str, list[list[Any]]]
    then: dict[str, Any]
    why: str


class SkillMeta(_S):
    skill_id: str
    skill_version: str
    title: str
    status: Literal["draft", "candidate", "active", "deprecated"]
    owner: str
    lifecycle_stages: list[str]
    trigger_signatures: list[TriggerSignature]
    supported_environments: dict[str, Any]
    required_evidence: list[RequiredEvidence]
    freshness_limits_seconds: dict[str, int]
    decision_tree_version: str
    prohibited_actions: list[str]
    remediation_catalog_refs: list[str]
    evaluation_fixtures: dict[str, str]
    declared_facts: list[str]
    rules: list[Rule]


FRONT = re.compile(r"\A---\n(.*?)\n---\n(.*)\Z", re.S)


def load(path: str | pathlib.Path) -> tuple[SkillMeta, str, str]:
    raw = pathlib.Path(path).read_bytes()
    m = FRONT.match(raw.decode("utf-8"))
    if not m:
        raise ValueError(f"{path}: missing YAML front matter")
    meta = SkillMeta.model_validate(yaml.safe_load(m.group(1)))
    return meta, m.group(2), hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: agent/skills/registry.py ===
"""Reason-code registry.

Human source: contracts/REASON_CODES.md, section 3 tables.
Machine source: contracts/reason_codes.json, generated by tools/build_reason_registry.py.
A test fails if the two drift apart.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import json, pathlib, re

REPO = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
MD_PATH = REPO / "contracts" / "REASON_CODES.md"
JSON_PATH = REPO / "contracts" / "reason_codes.json"
CODE = re.compile(r"^`([a-z][a-z0-9_]*)`$")
STATUS = re.compile(r"^`(supported|probable|inconclusive|not_supported)`")
UC = re.compile(r"UC\d\d")


def parse_markdown(text: str | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    text = MD_PATH.read_text() if text is None else text
    rows, in_section = [], False
    for line in text.splitlines():
        if line.startswith("## "):
            in_section = line.startswith("## 3")
            continue
        if not in_section or not line.startswith("|"):
            continue
        cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip().strip("|").split("|")]
        if len(cells) < 4:
            continue
        m, s = CODE.match(cells[0]), STATUS.match(cells[1])
        if not (m and s):
            continue
        rows.append({
            "code": m.group(1),
            "classification": s.group(1),
            "remediation": cells[2].strip("`"),
            "description": cells[3],
            "applicable_use_cases": UC.findall(cells[4]) if len(cells) > 4 else [],
            "scope": "global" if len(cells) > 4 and "All" in cells[4] else "scoped",
        })
    return rows


def build_registry() -> dict:
    rows = parse_markdown()
    seen: dict[str, dict] = {}
    for r in rows:
        if r["code"] in seen and seen[r["code"]] != r:
            raise ValueError(f"conflicting duplicate reason code: {r['code']}")
        seen[r["code"]] = r
    return {"schema_version": "1.0", "source": "contracts/REASON_CODES.md section 3",
            "codes": sorted(seen.values(), key=lambda r: r["code"])}


def load_registry() -> dict[str, dict]:
    return {c["code"]: c for c in json.loads(JSON_PATH.read_text())["codes"]}

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/AGENT_OBSERVABILITY.md ===
# Agent Observability and SLO Contract

## Metrics

Recommended low-cardinality metrics:

- `agent_investigations_total{trigger_type,use_case_id,terminal_status}`
- `agent_investigation_duration_seconds{use_case_id,terminal_status}`
- `agent_adapter_requests_total{adapter,operation,status}`
- `agent_adapter_request_duration_seconds{adapter,operation}`
- `agent_evidence_records_total{adapter,evidence_type}`
- `agent_evidence_quality_total{quality,freshness}`
- `agent_missing_evidence_total{use_case_id,evidence_type}`
- `agent_diagnosis_total{use_case_id,classification,confidence}`
- `agent_budget_exhaustions_total{budget_type}`
- `agent_llm_calls_total{purpose,status}` and `agent_llm_duration_seconds{purpose}`
- `agent_persistence_operations_total{operation,status}`
- `agent_recommendations_total{recommendation_type,risk_level}`

Do not use cluster name, node, namespace, pod, user, alert fingerprint or ticket number as metric labels. Put those in access-controlled structured logs/traces when operationally necessary.

## Tracing

Propagate trace/correlation IDs from event ingress through graph nodes, MCP requests, model requests and PostgreSQL operations. Record node name, duration, outcome and sanitized request ID. Do not attach raw logs, credentials, prompts containing sensitive values or unbounded payloads to spans.

## SLO definition

The repository's 45-second investigation deadline is a target, not an established SLO. Before declaring an SLO, measure event queue delay, adapter latency, LLM inference, persistence and concurrency under realistic fleet load. Publish percentiles and error budget by terminal status, not only a single average.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/CLUSTER_REGISTRY.md ===
# Cluster Registry Contract

## Purpose

The cluster registry is the authoritative allowlist used before any evidence query. It is not a Kubernetes API proxy and contains no cluster credentials.

## Required record

- `cluster_id` (stable unique identifier)
- `cluster_name` and environment classification
- region/datacenter and owner/team
- supported OpenShift version range and current observed version
- enabled use cases and required evidence adapters
- Splunk tenant/index aliases and Prometheus tenant/cluster selectors
- timezone and incident routing metadata
- status (`active`, `maintenance`, `disabled`, `retired`)
- policy profile and data-retention class
- last inventory verification and registry revision

Never accept arbitrary query selectors from an event to override registry mappings. Reject unknown/disabled clusters before calling MCP. Do not store API tokens, kubeconfigs, client certificates or passwords in the registry.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/DEPLOYMENT.md ===
# Deployment and Operations Contract

## Deployable components

- Agent API/worker (LangGraph runtime)
- Event ingress (Kafka consumer and/or authenticated HTTP/manual API)
- Splunk MCP adapter and Prometheus MCP adapter
- PostgreSQL database and migration job
- Optional LangGraph checkpoint store, separated from long-term case memory
- Separate Kubernetes object collector with read-only RBAC
- Optional later AAP handoff service, deployed separately from the diagnosis runtime

## Configuration

All environment-specific values come from managed configuration/secrets: MCP endpoints and tool mappings, database DSN, Kafka brokers/topics, cluster registry reference, deadlines, result limits, retention policy, model/prompt versions and feature flags. No secrets in ConfigMaps, repository files, events, logs or prompts. Validate required settings at startup and fail closed.

## Availability and scaling

Use stateless workers with bounded concurrency, durable event consumption, idempotent investigation creation, database connection pooling and backpressure. Partition or shard work by cluster/tenant where required. Define retry and dead-letter behavior. Avoid a single unbounded in-memory queue. Use health/readiness checks that verify dependencies without exposing secrets.

## Reliability

- Persist investigation start and idempotency record before long-running work.
- Set one absolute deadline propagated to every downstream call.
- Cancel outstanding adapter/model calls when the investigation terminates.
- Use retry only for explicitly transient failures; retry at most once unless measured policy says otherwise.
- Persist terminal states including `inconclusive`, `backend_unavailable`, `deadline_exceeded` and `persistence_failed`.
- Define recovery for worker restart and database outage. Checkpoint state is not the system of record for diagnosis evidence.

## Observability

Expose per-cluster and aggregate counts for events, deduplication, selected use case, adapter calls/failures/latency, evidence freshness, missing fields, contradiction rate, LLM latency/cost, database latency, graph duration, terminal status and remediation recommendations. Avoid metric labels containing pod names, namespace names, ticket text or other high-cardinality data.

## Release gates

1. Static/schema checks and unit tests.
2. Contract tests against mocked MCP adapters.
3. Golden/negative/contradictory/injection evaluation suite.
4. Load and backpressure tests at expected fleet concurrency.
5. Read-only staging validation against representative clusters.
6. Security review and RBAC verification.
7. Canary enablement for limited clusters/use cases.
8. Rollback plan for code, skill, catalog, prompt and database migration.

## Backup and retention

Back up PostgreSQL with point-in-time recovery appropriate to the service tier. Define retention and deletion for incidents, evidence summaries, model metadata and checkpoints. Keep raw logs/object snapshots in their source systems unless a documented policy allows copying them.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/MODEL_AND_PROMPT_POLICY.md ===
# Model and Prompt Policy

## Model responsibilities

Use deterministic code for context validation, lifecycle rules, evidence quality, correlation, arithmetic, catalog matching, safety and persistence. Use an LLM only for bounded interpretation of ambiguous alert language, explanation/synthesis of a normalized dossier, and user-readable report generation.

## Inputs

The model receives a structured context, the selected skill ID/version, relevant decision-tree excerpt, evidence records with stable IDs, explicit missing-data/contradiction markers, allowed classification enums and a JSON output schema. It does not receive credentials or unrestricted raw log streams.

## Output contract

The model may return hypotheses, classification proposal, confidence proposal, explanation and citations to supplied evidence IDs. Deterministic validators must reject unknown evidence IDs, fabricated resource fields, unsupported catalog IDs, unsafe instructions, malformed output and confidence inconsistent with evidence gates.

## Injection handling

Logs, Kubernetes annotations, Event messages, tickets, metric labels and manual text are untrusted evidence. Enclose and label them as data. Never allow their contents to override system policy or create new tools. Never execute generated commands. Do not let a model select an arbitrary skill path or query template.

## Model operations

Pin model/provider and prompt versions per release; log non-sensitive model metadata; measure token/cost/latency; implement timeout and fallback behavior. On malformed output or timeout, return a deterministic `inconclusive` report rather than retrying an open-ended conversation. Evaluate against golden, negative, contradictory, missing, stale and injection fixtures before changing a prompt/model.

## Track B

One bounded broad evidence query, one synthesis call, strict output shape, exploratory confidence, no playbook, no mutating action and explicit ruled-out cases only when supported by fresh evidence.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md ===
# Per-Use-Case Observability Matrix

## Collection model

Replace the fixed “Golden Six” label with a **common baseline plus per-use-case additions**. The existing list actually contains seven kinds (Pod, PDB, Node, VolumeAttachment, MCP, MachineConfig and Event). Collect only fields needed by enabled use cases. Never collect Secrets, token values, private keys, kubeconfigs, environment-variable values, `managedFields`, or raw MachineConfig file contents.

### Common baseline

| Kind | Required fields | Applies to |
|---|---|---|
| Pod | metadata identity/UID/labels/ownerReferences/finalizers/deletionTimestamp; phase/conditions/container state; nodeName; volumes and resource requests/limits, with env values excluded | 01-08, 14-16 |
| Node | UID/resourceVersion/labels/taints; Ready/DiskPressure/PIDPressure/MemoryPressure conditions; unschedulable; allocatable/capacity; MCO annotations | 01-16 |
| Event | UID, involvedObject identity, reason, type, message, eventTime/series timestamps, reporting controller | 01-16 where relevant |
| PodDisruptionBudget | selector, minAvailable/maxUnavailable, disruptionsAllowed/currentHealthy/desiredHealthy/expectedPods | 01, 07 |

### Conditional per-use-case objects

| Kind | Required fields | Use cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lease | namespace/name/holderIdentity/renewTime/leaseDurationSeconds | 08, 09, 13 | Collect `kube-node-lease`; name matches Node |
| VolumeAttachment | driver, nodeName, PV name, attached, attachError/detachError, attachment metadata after redaction | 04, 16 | No credentials or secret references expanded |
| PersistentVolumeClaim | namespace/name/UID/phase/storageClass/volumeName/accessModes/volumeMode | 04, 07 | No secret data |
| PersistentVolume | name/UID/phase/storageClass/accessModes/claimRef/nodeAffinity/CSI driver and volumeHandle redacted or hashed per policy | 04, 07 | Include only where topology/attachment reasoning requires it |
| CSINode | node identity; driver name; `allocatable.count` | 16 | Required for driver-specific attach-count comparison |
| MachineConfigPool | selector, paused, maxUnavailable, status configuration, machine/updated/ready/unavailable counts and conditions | 09-14 | Include full condition reasons/messages with bounded size |
| MachineConfig | name/labels; kernelArguments; systemd units; file paths/modes/ownership; Ignition version; OS image reference | 11, 12 | Strip raw file contents and embedded secrets |
| ClusterOperator | name, versions, relatedObjects identity, conditions/reason/message | 09, 10-13 | Needed for control-plane/operator health gates |
| Service | namespace/name/selector/ports/type | 02 | Do not collect credentials |
| EndpointSlice | namespace/service label/ports/endpoints addresses/readiness/conditions/nodeName/zone | 02 | Needed to prove webhook endpoint availability |
| ValidatingWebhookConfiguration / MutatingWebhookConfiguration | webhook name, service reference, rules, failurePolicy, timeoutSeconds, admissionReviewVersions | 02 | Strip `clientConfig.caBundle`; no writes |
| Deployment / ReplicaSet | namespace/name/UID/ownerReferences/replicas/readyReplicas/selector/conditions | 02, 03, 07 | Supports webhook/operator/controller health and workload owner correlation |
| DaemonSet | namespace/name/UID/selector/desired/ready/available counts/update strategy | 04 | CSI node-driver diagnostics; DaemonSet exclusion from eviction does not make it irrelevant to diagnosis |
| CertificateSigningRequest | name/UID/username/groups/usages/conditions/expiration where supported | 08 | Never collect private keys; approval remains human-controlled |
| VirtualMachineInstance / VirtualMachineInstanceMigration | namespace/name/UID/labels/phase/nodeName/conditions/migration state and source/target identity only | 05, 06 | Optional minimal collection if metrics + launcher Pods cannot prove VM identity/state. Do not ingest full VM specs or disk data |

### Use-case mapping

| UC | Mandatory object evidence | Important metrics/logs |
|---|---|---|
| 01 PDB eviction | Pod, PDB, Event, owner workload | MCC eviction denial; PDB allowed/current/desired health; replica readiness |
| 02 Webhook | Event, Service, EndpointSlice, webhook configuration metadata, Deployment/Pod | API-server admission error, webhook controller logs, rejection count and duration |
| 03 Finalizer | Pod, Event; owner workload/controller | deletionTimestamp, finalizers, FailedKillPod, controller and CRI-O logs |
| 04 Storage detach | Pod, PVC, PV, VolumeAttachment, Node, CSI DaemonSet | kubelet unmount, CSI node/controller logs, storage metrics |
| 05 VM not migratable | launcher Pod, Node; optional VMI | eviction interceptor, non-evictable reason, migration metrics |
| 06 Stale launcher | launcher Pods, Node; optional VMI/VMIM | migration success, target launcher readiness, source/target identity |
| 07 Reschedule | Pod, owner workload, Node, Event, PVC/PV, PDB | FailedScheduling, allocatable CPU/memory, pressure and topology metrics |
| 08 Node runtime | Node, Lease, Event, optional CSR | kubelet/CRI-O journals, kubelet scrape/ready metrics, CSR state |
| 09 API/etcd | Node, Lease, MCP, ClusterOperator, Event | etcd fsync/commit/db size; API request latency; control-plane logs |
| 10 MCP paused | MCP, Node, Event, ClusterOperator | paused/updating/degraded metrics and MCC/MCD logs |
| 11 MCD drift | Node, MCP, MachineConfig, Event | MCD degraded state, drift and systemd journal logs |
| 12 Render/apply | MCP, MachineConfig, Node, Event | MCC render, Ignition and MCD apply logs |
| 13 Pivot/reboot | Node, Lease, MCP, Event | rpm-ostree/MCD/journal, kubelet availability and boot verification |
| 14 Drain coordination | Node, Pod, MCP, Event | desired/lastApplied drain annotations, audit and MCC logs |
| 15 Disk/PID | Node, Pod, Event, MCP | filesystem free bytes/inodes, pids/max, kubelet eviction and MCD write failures |
| 16 CSI max volumes | CSINode, VolumeAttachment, Node, Pod, Event; PVC/PV where needed | scheduler max-volume denial, driver metrics and attach/detach logs |

## Freshness requirements

Define freshness by evidence class, not only by one global time window. Recommended starting targets (to be tuned against measured pipeline latency): node/lease conditions <= 2 minutes; PDB/Pod/VolumeAttachment/CSINode snapshots <= 5 minutes for active incidents; MCP/MachineConfig/ClusterOperator snapshots <= 10 minutes; Events and logs retain original event times and are searched over a bounded incident window; metrics use a declared scrape timestamp and range. A stale value may be context but cannot satisfy a mandatory current-state gate.

The collector must emit `cluster_id`, resource UID, resourceVersion when available, event time, observation time and ingestion time. It must expose tombstones/deletions or periodic full snapshots so the agent can distinguish “deleted” from “not collected”.

## Collector deployment notes

The OTel `k8sobjectsreceiver` examples in older documents are illustrative, not a validated drop-in configuration. Verify receiver version, supported API kinds, field filtering syntax, watch/list RBAC, memory limits, sampling, resync behavior and deletion semantics before deployment. Add Lease, CSINode and conditional objects only after the associated use case is enabled. Use a read-only service account and no Secret list/watch permissions.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/OBSERVABILITY_REQUIREMENTS.md ===
# Observability Requirements

The canonical requirement is now a **common baseline plus per-use-case object allowlist**, not a fixed Golden Six. The previous list actually named seven resource kinds and omitted objects explicitly required by several scenarios.

See [`OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md) for the authoritative mapping of all 16 use cases to Kubernetes objects, fields, logs, metrics, freshness rules and collector safeguards.

## Evidence source topology

The default deployment uses two independently queried evidence adapters:

- Splunk MCP: logs, audit records, Events and Kubernetes object snapshots/changes when those datasets are actually ingested.
- Prometheus MCP: metric queries.

Centralizing metrics into Splunk is permitted as an alternative deployment profile, but must be declared in `manifest.json` and proven by adapter capability tests. Do not assume Prometheus metrics are available through Splunk just because the architecture diagram has one search box.

## Mandatory collector identity fields

Every object record must include `cluster_id`, API group/version, kind, namespace where applicable, name, UID, resourceVersion where available, event time, observation time and ingestion time. Deletions/tombstones or periodic authoritative snapshots are required to distinguish deletion from missing collection.

## Security

The collector has list/watch access only to approved object types. Secrets and credential-bearing fields are prohibited. Strip `metadata.managedFields`, environment variable values, raw MachineConfig file contents, webhook CA bundles and all credentials. The diagnostic agent itself has no Kubernetes API credentials or write permissions.

## Freshness

Use evidence-class-specific freshness thresholds. The starter targets are in `OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`; calibrate them using measured end-to-end collection latency. A stale record can supply historical context but cannot satisfy a current-state safety precondition.

=== END FILE ===

# Implementation Phase Task Cards & Acceptance Test Specifications

This document provides concrete, actionable engineering task cards for Phases 0 through 8 defined in [`DEVELOPMENT_BLUEPRINT.md`](../DEVELOPMENT_BLUEPRINT.md) §6. A smaller coding model must follow each task card in order and cannot declare a phase complete until all listed acceptance tests pass.

---

## Phase 0: Contract Hardening, Schemas & Reason Code Registry

### Task Card: `TC-PHASE-0`
- **Objective:** Establish strongly-typed Pydantic v2 schemas, define canonical reason codes, formalize MCP tool contracts, and specify the skill rule format.
- **Dependencies:** `contracts/`, `manifest.json`.
- **Target Deliverables:**
  - `schemas/`: `event.py`, `evidence.py`, `dossier.py`, `state.py`, `report.py`, `__init__.py`.
  - `contracts/REASON_CODES.md`: Full catalog of canonical stop reasons.
  - `contracts/SKILL_RULES.md`: Front matter schema and declarative AST rule spec.
  - `contracts/mcp_schemas/`: JSON schemas for Splunk and Prometheus MCP tools.
  - `contracts/samples/`: Real sample payloads for adapter responses.
- **Hard Invariants:**
  - All schemas must use Pydantic v2.
  - Do not assume logical adapter operation names are exposed by the connected MCP server; bind and test actual discovered tool names.
  - No write tools allowed.
- **Acceptance Tests:**
  1. `pytest tests/test_schemas.py -q` passes 100%.
  2. `python3 tests/validate_repo_contracts.py` exits with code 0.
  3. `python3 -m compileall schemas contracts` returns 0 syntax errors.
- **Exit Gate:** All core schemas parse golden fixtures, reject malformed payloads, and enforce safety invariants (e.g., no playbook on inconclusive/not_supported).

---

## Phase 1: Observability Adapters & Read-Only Access Harness

### Task Card: `TC-PHASE-1`
- **Objective:** Implement `splunk_mcp` and `prometheus_mcp` clients adhering to [`contracts/MCP_INTEGRATION.md`](../contracts/MCP_INTEGRATION.md) and [`contracts/TOOL_CONTRACTS.md`](../contracts/TOOL_CONTRACTS.md).
- **Dependencies:** Phase 0 schemas (`schemas/evidence.py`, `contracts/mcp_schemas/`).
- **Implementation Work Items:**
  1. Create `adapters/base.py`: Define `EvidenceAdapter` protocol with `query()` and `capabilities()`.
  2. Create `adapters/splunk/`:
     - Implement `SplunkMCPAdapter` for log/Event search, exact object lookup and bounded object listing.
     - Bind logical operations to actual discovered Splunk MCP tool names through deployment configuration; do not hard-code an assumed server tool name.
     - Enforce hard time bounds and result caps for every operation.
  3. Create `adapters/prometheus/`:
     - Implement `PrometheusMCPAdapter` for instant and bounded range queries.
     - Bind actual discovered Prometheus MCP tools through deployment configuration.
     - Enforce cluster labeling, metric-template allowlisting, range and step limits.
  4. Implement transient error handling: retry at most once for HTTP 503/timeout; fail-fast with `telemetry_backend_unavailable` without silent swallow.
- **Hard Invariants:**
  - Strictly read-only. Absolutely no direct Kubernetes API client, tokens, or write endpoints.
  - Parameterize all SPL and PromQL templates. Never interpolate unvalidated user or alert text.
- **Acceptance Tests:**
  1. `pytest tests/unit/test_adapters.py -q` (contract tests with mock MCP server).
  2. Test timeout handling: mock timeout returns normalized error, does not raise unhandled exception.
  3. Test query bounds: verify time range and record limits are injected into every query.
- **Exit Gate:** Mock adapter contract tests pass; adapter scopes and query budgets are verified by test assertions.

---

## Phase 2: Evidence Normalization & Deterministic Correlation Engine

### Task Card: `TC-PHASE-2`
- **Objective:** Ingest raw adapter query results, normalize them into canonical `EvidenceRecord` items, evaluate freshness and contradictions, and execute deterministic identity joins.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 0 schemas (`schemas/dossier.py`), Phase 1 adapters.
- **Implementation Work Items:**
  1. Create `evidence/normalizer.py`:
     - Map raw Splunk search rows and Prometheus metric vectors to `EvidenceRecord`.
     - Redact sensitive tokens and credentials before storing excerpts.
     - Compute content hashes (`sha256`).
  2. Create `evidence/freshness.py`:
     - Check evidence age against [`docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md`](../docs/OBSERVABILITY_MATRIX.md) limits (e.g., 300s for active-incident PDB/Pod snapshots).
     - Mark records as `fresh` or `stale`.
  3. Create `correlation/engine.py`:
     - Implement deterministic joins:
       - PDB selector match against Pod labels in the same namespace.
       - OwnerReference resolution (Pod $\rightarrow$ ReplicaSet $\rightarrow$ Deployment).
       - Node name and MachineConfigPool correlation.
     - Detect contradictions: e.g. MCC eviction denial log present, but PDB `disruptionsAllowed > 0`.
- **Hard Invariants:**
  - Resource joins must be 100% deterministic code (zero LLM reasoning in joins).
  - Telemetry from a non-target `cluster_id` must immediately halt with `wrong_cluster_evidence`.
  - Empty results on backend errors must NOT be mapped to empty successes.
- **Acceptance Tests:**
  1. `pytest tests/unit/test_correlation.py -q`:
     - Verify PDB-to-Pod label matching matches golden dossier relationships.
     - Verify mismatched cluster ID triggers `wrong_cluster_evidence`.
     - Verify snapshot age > 300s triggers `pdb_state_stale`.
     - Verify contradiction detection between MCC log and PDB status.
- **Exit Gate:** Dossier correlation produces valid `RelationshipRecord` items and flags quality issues correctly on UC01 test fixtures.

---

## Phase 3: LangGraph State Machine & Persistence Harness

### Task Card: `TC-PHASE-3`
- **Objective:** Construct the 10-node dual-track LangGraph workflow, define routing edges, budget limits, and PostgreSQL persistence.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 0 `schemas/state.py`, Phase 1 adapters, Phase 2 correlation engine.
- **Implementation Work Items:**
  1. Create `graph/state.py`: Initialize `AgentState` with 45s deadline timer and adapter call counters.
  2. Create pipeline nodes:
     - `ingest_event`: Ingest envelope, validate schema, compute idempotency key.
     - `normalize_context`: Check cluster allowlist via `docs/CLUSTER_REGISTRY.md`.
     - `classify_lifecycle`: Deterministic regex/field classifier for lifecycle stage.
     - `load_skill`: Match signatures against allowlisted use cases.
     - `build_evidence_plan` & `assess_evidence`.
     - `persist_and_report`: Insert investigation trace into PostgreSQL (`db/migrations/001_initial.sql`).
  3. Implement conditional routing:
     - Unknown cluster $\rightarrow$ `persist_and_report` (`invalid_cluster_context`).
     - Unsupported lifecycle $\rightarrow$ `persist_and_report` (`lifecycle_stage_not_drain`).
     - Known use case (UC01-16) $\rightarrow$ Track A; Unknown $\rightarrow$ Track B.
- **Hard Invariants:**
  - Enforce total 45-second investigation deadline.
  - Maximum 2 Splunk tool calls per investigation.
  - Zero state transitions through unhandled exceptions.
- **Acceptance Tests:**
  1. `pytest tests/integration/test_graph_lifecycle.py -q`:
     - Test valid event transitions through full graph.
     - Test invalid cluster stops at `normalize_context`.
     - Test deadline expiration halts graph cleanly with status report.
     - Test duplicate event returns existing investigation record (idempotency).
- **Exit Gate:** Graph harness executes deterministically with mock adapters, terminates on all guard conditions, and commits audit records to DB.

=== FILE: docs/SECURITY_MODEL.md ===
# Security Model

## Trust boundaries

1. **Event ingress:** Alertmanager/Kafka/manual API is untrusted input. Authenticate producers and principals; validate schema and cluster scope.
2. **Evidence adapters:** Splunk and Prometheus are read-only data sources. MCP responses are untrusted data, not instructions.
3. **Object collector:** A separately deployed read-only telemetry component. It is not the diagnostic agent and must not have write privileges.
4. **LLM boundary:** The model receives bounded, redacted evidence. It cannot select arbitrary tools or execute commands.
5. **Persistence:** PostgreSQL stores redacted investigation summaries and provenance. Migration role owns DDL; runtime role cannot change schema.
6. **Remediation boundary:** The diagnostic agent emits recommendation payloads only. A separate approval/AAP service owns launch credentials and job execution.

## Identity and authorization

- Authenticate every event producer and manual requester.
- Resolve cluster IDs from an allowlisted registry. Do not accept an arbitrary cluster URL, kubeconfig or bearer token from event payloads.
- Enforce tenant/cluster scope in adapter queries and database access.
- Use distinct service identities for ingress, evidence queries, persistence, collector and AAP integration.
- Use Vault or the enterprise secrets manager for credentials. Never place tokens or passwords in Git, prompts, logs, event bodies or SQL text.

## Minimum privileges

- Agent: Splunk search-only on approved indexes and Prometheus query-only on approved data; PostgreSQL select/insert/update only on required agent tables; no Kubernetes API credentials; no shell/SSH write tools; no AAP launch credential in diagnosis mode.
- Collector: list/watch only the explicitly approved Kubernetes resource kinds and namespaces; no Secret access; no mutation verbs.
- AAP integration: separate identity; exact job-template allowlist; approval and change authorization required; no general-purpose inventory or arbitrary playbook execution.

## Data protection

- Never ingest Secret objects, private keys, tokens, kubeconfigs, environment variable values, raw MachineConfig file contents or webhook CA bundles.
- Strip `metadata.managedFields`, credentials in log messages, authorization headers, URL query secrets and sensitive labels where required.
- Apply bounded evidence excerpts and retention. Store hashes and source references rather than full raw payloads by default.
- Encrypt traffic and storage; rotate credentials; audit access and export; enforce PostgreSQL RLS where cluster/tenant partitioning requires it.

## Prompt-injection resistance

Treat log lines, event messages, annotations, labels, object fields, tickets and user-provided text as hostile content. Delimit them as evidence data; never concatenate them into system/developer instructions. No LLM-produced text may be passed to a shell. Command output is a recommendation template only and must be validated against typed schemas and command allowlists.

## Hard safety gates

- Runtime tool registry must fail startup if a mutation-capable Kubernetes, SSH, shell-write or AAP-launch tool is registered in diagnosis mode.
- Track B must force `playbook_match=false`, `playbook_id=null` and exploratory confidence.
- Missing evidence, stale telemetry, wrong-cluster records, contradictory evidence or version mismatch prohibit high-confidence supported diagnoses.
- High-risk actions (finalizer removal, force deletion, storage detach, VM shutdown, master/etcd restart, reboot, MachineConfig changes, webhook bypass) are never auto-launched.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/SECURITY_REVIEW_CHECKLIST.md ===
# Security Review Checklist

- [ ] Agent runtime has no Kubernetes API token, client certificate, kubeconfig, write-capable tool, SSH credential or AAP launch credential.
- [ ] Collector RBAC is read-only and limited to enabled resource kinds; Secrets are not listable/watchable.
- [ ] Splunk/Prometheus access is query-only and cluster-scoped.
- [ ] Cluster registry prevents arbitrary cluster/query-target selection.
- [ ] Kafka/HTTP producers are authenticated; schema and size limits are enforced.
- [ ] Credentials, env values, MachineConfig content, webhook CA bundles and sensitive log fields are redacted.
- [ ] PostgreSQL runtime, migration, approval and AAP roles are separate; column-level permissions are tested.
- [ ] RLS/tenant isolation is tested where required.
- [ ] Tool registry rejects mutation-capable tools at startup.
- [ ] Prompt-injection tests cover logs, annotations, Events, labels, manual requests and ticket text.
- [ ] Track B cannot produce a playbook ID or automated action.
- [ ] Human approvals bind to exact cluster/target/playbook/variables hash and expire.
- [ ] Audit logs capture investigation, approval and external execution transitions.
- [ ] Backup, retention, deletion, restore and credential rotation are tested.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/SKILL_LOADER.md ===
# Skill Registry and Loader Contract

## Skill package metadata

Each skill must declare structured YAML front matter conforming to [`contracts/SKILL_RULES.md`](../contracts/SKILL_RULES.md): `skill_id`, semantic `skill_version`, title, lifecycle stages, trigger signatures, supported OpenShift/MCO/KubeVirt/CSI versions, required evidence types and fields, freshness limits, decision-tree version, prohibited actions, remediation catalog references, evaluation fixture IDs, source references, and owner.

All exit codes, stop reasons, and uncertainty downgrade markers emitted by skills must be registered in [`contracts/REASON_CODES.md`](../contracts/REASON_CODES.md).

## Loading rules

The YAML front matter is parsed with a safe YAML parser and validated against the schema in `contracts/SKILL_RULES.md`. Unknown keys, duplicate keys, malformed regular expressions, missing mandatory metadata and path escapes are hard validation failures. Metadata is declarative only; it does not grant capabilities.

- Skills are loaded only from the repository's allowlisted `use-cases/<id>/skill.md` paths resolved by the manifest; no user-supplied path or dynamic filesystem traversal.
- Validate skill ID against `manifest.json`, front matter/metadata schema, required sections, version compatibility and catalog references.
- Pin the skill content hash for each investigation and persist it with the result.
- A skill is disabled if its status is `draft` or `deprecated`, required telemetry is unavailable, the supported OpenShift range does not match, a required source is not configured, required fixture references are missing, or its evaluation gate is not passing. `candidate` and `active` are not interchangeable: promotion to `active` requires domain-owner approval and passing versioned evaluations.
- Markdown is procedural knowledge, not executable code. Do not evaluate embedded Python, shell or templates from `skill.md`.
- Parse diagnostic conditions into the constrained rule representation defined in [`contracts/SKILL_RULES.md`](../contracts/SKILL_RULES.md) or maintain their executable equivalent in reviewed code. Tests must prove the runtime behavior matches the document.
- **Prompt Injection Defense Injection:** Individual skills do not mention prompt injection. The loader and runtime harness inject security boundary directives and isolate untrusted evidence (logs, annotations, events) in strictly demarcated `<untrusted_evidence_data>` blocks.
- **Domain-Expert Pass Requirement:** Skills are subject to domain-expert review before activation to correct Kubernetes technical slips (e.g. UC01 overlapping PDB handling, UC03 finalizer location).

## Change management

Any change to trigger signatures, evidence requirements, decision rules, risk classification or remediation matching requires a fixture update and relevant evaluation run. Store skill versions immutably; do not silently replace the skill used by an in-flight investigation.

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: docs/VERSION_COMPATIBILITY.md ===
# Version Compatibility and Source Validation

## Purpose

A diagnosis skill is valid only for the OpenShift/MCO/KubeVirt/CSI versions against which its assumptions, fields, metrics, log signatures and procedures have been checked. Upstream `main` is a research source, not proof of behavior in a released cluster.

## Compatibility record

For every skill release, record:

- OpenShift versions tested (exact z-streams where relevant)
- MCO image digest/source tag and commit
- KubeVirt / OpenShift Virtualization operator version where applicable
- CSI driver name, version, topology and documented attach limit source
- Prometheus metric name, labels, type, unit and first/last verified version
- Kubernetes API group/version and required fields
- Red Hat runbook/solution URL and last verification date
- known deviations, deprecations and unsupported branches
- fixture/evaluation revision proving the skill behavior

## Release gates

1. Resolve the actual MCO source tag corresponding to the deployed OpenShift release.
2. Compare controller/daemon implementation and matching tests; do not copy `main` constants into production logic.
3. Verify Red Hat alert runbook and product documentation for the release.
4. Check API field existence and version changes (for example, policy/v1 PDBs and CSI objects).
5. Verify every Prometheus metric exists in the target platform and has the expected labels/semantics. Metrics such as `kubevirt_vmi_non_evictable` or MCO-specific metrics must not be assumed across all versions without discovery.
6. Test log signatures against captured, sanitized examples from the supported release.
7. Mark unsupported versions explicitly; route to `inconclusive: version_gate_failed` rather than silently running an unverified skill.

## Evidence provenance

Store URLs, upstream commit/tag, access date, reviewer, version range and a short rationale in skill metadata or verified `sre_knowledge`. Do not cite moving `latest` documentation as the only source for a pinned release decision.

=== END FILE ===

  python3 tools/build_reason_registry.py --check  # exit 1 if the JSON is stale"""

from agent.skills.registry import build_registry, JSON_PATH

if __name__ == "__main__":
    text = json.dumps(build_registry(), indent=2) + "\n"
    if "--check" in sys.argv:
        ok = JSON_PATH.exists() and JSON_PATH.read_text() == text
        print("registry up to date" if ok else "registry STALE: run tools/build_reason_registry.py")
        sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)
    JSON_PATH.write_text(text)
    print("wrote", len(json.loads(text)["codes"]), "codes to", JSON_PATH)

=== FILE: tools/create_bundle.py ===
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build self-extracting single-file bundles for easy copy-pasting into VS Code."""
import base64
import io
import os
from pathlib import Path
import tarfile

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
EXCLUDED_DIRS = {".git", "__pycache__", ".pytest_cache", ".system_generated"}
EXCLUDED_EXTS = {".pyc", ".tar.gz", ".swp"}
EXCLUDED_FILES = {"bundle_unpack.py", "repo_bundle.sh", "repo_bundle.txt", "consolidated_repo.txt"}


def collect_files():
    tar_buffer = io.BytesIO()
    file_count = 0
    with tarfile.open(fileobj=tar_buffer, mode="w:gz") as tar:
        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(REPO_ROOT):
            dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in EXCLUDED_DIRS]
            for f in sorted(files):
                if any(f.endswith(ext) for ext in EXCLUDED_EXTS) or f in EXCLUDED_FILES:
                    continue
                full_path = Path(root) / f
                rel_path = full_path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)
                tar.add(full_path, arcname=str(rel_path))
                file_count += 1
    return tar_buffer.getvalue(), file_count


def generate_python_unpacker(compressed_bytes: bytes, file_count: int, output_path: Path):
    b64_payload = base64.b64encode(compressed_bytes).decode("ascii")
    
    # Wrap base64 payload into 80-char chunks for clean copying
    lines = [b64_payload[i:i+80] for i in range(0, len(b64_payload), 80)]
    payload_str = "\n".join(lines)
    
    script_content = f'''#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Single-File Self-Extracting Repository Unpacker.
Usage:
    1. Create a new file in VS Code (e.g. unpack.py) in an empty folder.
    2. Paste this entire file into it and save.
    3. Run in VS Code terminal: python3 unpack.py
"""
import base64
import gzip
import io
import os
from pathlib import Path
import tarfile
import sys

PAYLOAD = """\\
{payload_str}
"""

def main():
    print(f"[*] Unpacking repository ({file_count} files)...")
    target_dir = Path.cwd()
    data = base64.b64decode(PAYLOAD.replace("\\n", "").strip())
    
    with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(data), mode="r:gz") as tar:
        tar.extractall(target_dir)
        names = tar.getnames()
    
    print(f"[+] Successfully extracted {{len(names)}} files and directories to {{target_dir}}")
    print("[*] To verify installation, run:")
    print("      pytest -q")
    print("      python3 tests/validate_repo_contracts.py")
    print("      python3 tools/build_reason_registry.py --check")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
'''
    output_path.write_text(script_content, encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"Generated Python self-unpacker: {output_path} ({len(script_content)} bytes)")


def generate_shell_unpacker(compressed_bytes: bytes, file_count: int, output_path: Path):
    b64_payload = base64.b64encode(compressed_bytes).decode("ascii")
    lines = [b64_payload[i:i+80] for i in range(0, len(b64_payload), 80)]
    payload_str = "\n".join(lines)
    
    script_content = f'''#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Single-File Self-Extracting Shell Unpacker for VS Code Terminal
# Usage: Paste into unpack.sh, then run: bash unpack.sh
set -e
echo "[*] Unpacking repository ({file_count} files)..."
base64 -d << 'EOF' | tar -xz
{payload_str}
EOF
echo "[+] Extraction complete!"
echo "[*] Next steps: pytest -q"
'''
    output_path.write_text(script_content, encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"Generated Shell unpacker: {output_path} ({len(script_content)} bytes)")


def generate_text_bundle(output_path: Path):
    """Plain-text concatenated bundle with simple inline delimiter."""
    lines = []
    file_count = 0
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(REPO_ROOT):
        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in EXCLUDED_DIRS]
        for f in sorted(files):
            if any(f.endswith(ext) for ext in EXCLUDED_EXTS) or f in EXCLUDED_FILES or f == output_path.name:
                continue
            full_path = Path(root) / f
            rel_path = full_path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)
            try:
                content = full_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
                lines.append(f"=== FILE: {rel_path} ===")
                lines.append(content)
                lines.append("=== END FILE ===\n")
                file_count += 1
            except UnicodeDecodeError:
                continue
    
    bundle_text = "\n".join(lines)
    output_path.write_text(bundle_text, encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"Generated text bundle: {output_path} ({file_count} files, {len(bundle_text)} bytes)")


def main():
    print("[*] Collecting repository files...")
    tar_bytes, count = collect_files()
    print(f"[*] Compressed {count} files into {len(tar_bytes)} bytes.")
    
    generate_python_unpacker(tar_bytes, count, REPO_ROOT / "bundle_unpack.py")
    generate_shell_unpacker(tar_bytes, count, REPO_ROOT / "repo_bundle.sh")
    generate_text_bundle(REPO_ROOT / "consolidated_repo.txt")
    print("[+] All bundle formats generated successfully.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

=== END FILE ===

=== FILE: tools/render_skill_rules.py ===
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Render the decision-rule table in a skill body from its front matter (single source of truth).
  python3 tools/render_skill_rules.py <skill.md>           # write
  python3 tools/render_skill_rules.py <skill.md> --check   # exit 1 if the table is stale"""
import pathlib, re, sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from agent.skills.loader import load

BEGIN, END = "<!-- rules:begin -->", "<!-- rules:end -->"


def _cond(c):
    fact, op, *v = c
    return f"`{fact}` {op}" + (f" `{v[0]}`" if v else "")


def render(meta) -> str:
    rows = ["| Rule | When | Result | Why |", "|---|---|---|---|"]
    for r in meta.rules:
        key = "always" if "always" in r.when else ("all" if "all" in r.when else "any")
        if key == "always":
            when = "otherwise"
        else:
            when = (" AND " if key == "all" else " OR ").join(_cond(c) for c in r.when[key])
        t = r.then
        rem = t.get("remediation") or {}
        res = f"{t['classification']}/{t['confidence']}"
        if t.get("reason_code"): res += f", `{t['reason_code']}`"
        if t.get("sub_branch"): res += f", `{t['sub_branch']}`"
        if rem: res += f", {rem['kind']}" + (f" `{rem['playbook_id']}`" if rem.get("playbook_id") else "")
        rows.append(f"| **{r.id}** | {when} | {res} | {r.why} |")
    return "\n".join(rows)


def apply(path) -> tuple[str, str]:
    p = pathlib.Path(path)
    meta, _, _ = load(p)
    text = p.read_text()
    pattern = re.compile(re.escape(BEGIN) + r"\n.*?\n?" + re.escape(END), re.S)
    if not pattern.search(text):
        raise SystemExit(f"{path}: rules markers not found")
    new = pattern.sub(lambda m: f"{BEGIN}\n{render(meta)}\n{END}", text, count=1)
    return text, new


if __name__ == "__main__":
    path = sys.argv[1]
    old, new = apply(path)
    if "--check" in sys.argv:
        print("rules table up to date" if old == new else "rules table STALE")
        sys.exit(0 if old == new else 1)
    pathlib.Path(path).write_text(new)
    print("rendered rules table")

=== END FILE ===
